# The byte sets this project can REVERT `out/stage90/` to, recorded as hashes.
#
# WHO WRITES THIS: by hand, in the step that measured the set, and never by the build. That is the same
# rule `tool-images.txt` follows and for the same reason - a record the build writes agrees with itself
# and constrains nothing. The comparison lives in `tools/verify_revert_set.sh`, which hashes a directory
# against the lines below and refuses when a file is absent, empty, the wrong size, or the wrong bytes.
#
# WHY A REVERT SET NEEDS A RECORD AT ALL. The user's standing constraint is that the device must never
# be bricked and never hang permanently, and the mechanism behind that claim is not the watchdog - it is
# that the arm in `out/` can always be put back to one that was known to reach a specific point. That
# mechanism is a directory of files. Those files are **outside version control**: `out/` is gitignored
# and the 574 park is in `/tmp`, so the hashes below are the identity and the copies are only copies.
# Until this file existed, the identity appeared in prose in 22 documents and in no form a program could
# compare against.
#
# WHAT MAKES A SET COMPLETE, and it is not a list someone remembered. **A revert is complete when the
# gate is green on the reverted tree**, and the gate reads in TWO ways - so the set is the union of two
# derivations, not one:
#
#   A. the paths it names in its own text. `preflight_boot_check.sh` consumes nine under `$OUT`
#      (`SHA256SUMS.txt`, `stage90.bin`, `stage90-build-config.txt`, `stage90.elf`, `stage90-qcdt.img`,
#      `xnu_arm_entry.bin`, `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt`, `xnu_arm_entry.elf`,
#      `xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt`). Both spellings are needed - eight are written `$OUT/name` and one
#      (`xnu_arm_entry.elf`, in a narration path) `out/stage90/name` - so a derivation that greps one of
#      them returns a shorter list, and a shorter list is what a missing record line looks like:
#
#        grep -oE '\$OUT/[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+|out/stage90/[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+' \
#          scripts/preflight_boot_check.sh | sed 's|\$OUT/||; s|out/stage90/||' | sort -u
#
#   B. **the members of the manifest it verifies.** `preflight_boot_check.sh:139` is
#      `( cd "$OUT" && sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS.txt )`, and `sha256sum -c` opens every path the manifest
#      names. That manifest is itself a member of the set, so **this half of the criterion is a property
#      of a file INSIDE the set and not of the gate's text**: no grep of the gate can return these names,
#      and the manifest's member list is build-dependent (it is generated by `build.sh:389`). Today it
#      names five - `stage90_fixture.macho`, `stage90.elf`, `stage90.bin`, `stage90.img`,
#      `stage90-qcdt.img` - of which three are already in A and **two are not**. B is therefore pinned
#      HERE, as the `manifest_members=` field of the manifest's own line, so that the criterion is closed
#      over this record rather than over the live tree: reading it out of `out/stage90/SHA256SUMS.txt`
#      would read the one file whose contents the next build changes, and it would be right today only
#      because the live and parked manifests happen to name the same five.
#
# **A nine-member set built from A alone was this record's first version, and it was wrong**, in the way
# that costs the most: reverting the nine and leaving the live `stage90.img` in place makes the restored
# manifest's own check print `stage90.img: FAILED` - the gate RED - on a record whose whole purpose is
# that a revert cannot leave the gate red. Measured, not argued: the four other members print OK, and
# `stage90_fixture.macho` is `52bc9c35…` in both arms, which is why that one is benign today and
# `stage90.img` (`28ee2c22…` live against `8274b1c4…` frozen) is not. The mechanism half of A+B, and the
# simulation of the red line, are in `tools/rehearse_revert_set.sh`, which derives BOTH halves at run
# time and refuses when the record does not cover them.
#
# The strongest check is still not a derivation at all: revert, then run the gate. Run it before
# believing any record, including this one.
#
# THE TRAP THIS FILE EXISTS TO NOT READ. Every build writes its own `SHA256SUMS.txt` with **absolute**
# paths into the tree that produced it, and a park is a copy of those files *outside* that tree. So
# `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS.txt` run inside a park hashes `/mnt/data/mi4-ios6/out/stage90/*` - the live
# tree, which has moved on since - and prints `FAILED` for every file that changed while the parked
# bytes are all correct. A manifest of absolute paths is a record of a *location* and not of a
# *directory*: it verifies wherever you run it, and what it verifies is the path it names. It has
# already produced one wrong reading in this project (595's session, whose rehearsal mirror re-read the
# real `out/` from inside the mirror). `verify_revert_set.sh` therefore reads THIS file and never the
# target's own manifest, and it prints the absolute path of every path in a manifest it finds as a
# narration line, so the trap is stated at the moment a reader meets the directory.
#
# The park's own `SHA256SUMS.txt` is below as a member of the set, with the hash 591 recorded for it. It
# is deliberately NOT rewritten to relative paths: editing it would falsify 591's record and this one,
# and a frozen artifact is not something to tidy. The defect is recorded instead - which is why the file
# that cannot be trusted as a manifest is still pinned as bytes.
#
# ONE FILE PER LINE, space-separated key=value:
#   set=      which revert set the line belongs to; the tool verifies one set or all of them
#   sha256=   the key into the line; a changed byte anywhere refuses
#   bytes=    a second, independent field of the same bytes - a size and a hash agreeing is what turns
#             a typo in either into a reading instead of a silence
#   file=     the name as it appears in `out/stage90/` and in every park of this set
#   role=     what this file is for. A role is not inferable from a name, and a revert that omits a
#             file it did not recognise is the failure mode this field exists to prevent.
#   manifest_members=  on the line whose file= is the manifest, the member list that manifest carries,
#             comma-separated. This is the record's copy of criterion B (see below) so that B is
#             **closed over the record** instead of read out of the live tree: the manifest a revert
#             puts back is the record's own member, so its member list is a fact the record can pin, and
#             `tools/verify_revert_set.sh` refuses if any name here is not also a `file=` line of the
#             same set. Without this field a reader would have to open the target's manifest - which is
#             the one file whose contents the next build changes.
#
# TWO SETS, AND WHY THE SECOND ONE EXISTS. `frozen-574` is the arm to fall BACK to. `armed-sleepless-696a0f39`
# is the arm the next press SENDS, and it is recorded for a different reason: **the payload build is not
# byte-reproducible** (408), so `out/stage90/` is the only copy of it that has ever existed. Nothing
# wiped it - but nothing protected it either, and a run of `build.sh` or `build_entry.sh` would have
# destroyed the one unrun arm in this project whose loss cannot be undone by rebuilding. A record of the
# bytes, plus a park, is the same protection 611 gave the 574 arm, applied where the risk actually was.
#
# The two sets differ in 9 of their 11 files: the payload (the entry image is embedded in it), the two
# ELFs, the entry image, the boot image, the qcdt, the manifest and the entry's own record and source
# list. Measured by running the tool against the live tree with only the 574 set selected: **9 FAIL
# lines**. `stage90-build-config.txt` and `stage90_fixture.macho` are byte-identical between the two
# arms, which is worth stating because "it happens not to matter for two of the eleven" is exactly the
# reasoning this record exists to replace.
#
# The parked copies, which are copies and not the record:
#   /tmp/r594/frozen-payload/          the 574 set, the arm to fall back to
#   out/stage90/frozen/armed-sleepless-696a0f39/
#                                      the armed set, byte-for-byte, taken while it was still live
#   out/stage90/frozen/                526 and 533 are parked here by an older convention, recorded
#                                      nowhere yet - a reader should know this record does not cover
#                                      them rather than assume the park directory is complete
#
# MEASURED 2026-09-23, each file hashed in place in the park (not copied from a document).

# 666 (2026-09-25): the WATCHDOG-SELFTEST arm - the arm experiment-664 section 4 concluded should be
# next, built for the first time here, and the first set this record has had to ADMIT rather than revert
# to. The gate refused the live out/ with `NO set recorded ... nearest: armed-seam-poc-a43304f2(5/11)`
# and named this remedy, which is the order this file has always specified: recorded BEFORE its press.
#
# WHY THE ARM EXISTS. 664 split the next rehearsal in two: the PS_HOLD forced reset is proven by every
# returning run (`entry_epilogue` is the entry image's own run-ending store), while the hardware
# watchdog's bite has never been observed to fire - against five hangs with no return. So this arm
# forces the bite ALONE. `STAGE90_HW_WATCHDOG_SELFTEST=1` arms the watchdog, deliberately does NOT arm
# the software dead-man, and spins. Its verdict is the log's own content, not a timing: a device back
# without the deadline line means the bite fired; the deadline line - `hw_watchdog SELFTEST: deadline
# reached - the hardware watchdog did NOT fire` - means it did not, and PS_HOLD returned the device.
# Both directions print, so both directions are readable, which is 665 section 5's requirement.
#
# **THE FALLBACK THAT MAKES THAT ARM SAFE WAS BROKEN UNTIL THIS STEP, AND BUILDING THE ARM IS WHAT FOUND
# IT.** `timebase_elapsed_us` multiplied in 32 bits, so it could not return any value above 44,739,242us;
# the deadline is 90,000,000us. The bounded spin's exit test was therefore unsatisfiable: the spin was
# unbounded and `platform_reboot()` behind it was dead code, so the one arm whose own comment promises
# "the device comes back without being touched" was the one arm that could not - it could have hung until
# someone held the power button. Measured three ways (the object's 32-bit `add r0, r0, r0, lsl #2`
# product; a host program on the exact types; and the guard firing only after the conversion was fixed).
# The conversion is repaired exactly and without a libgcc helper (`timebase.c`), and the deadline's
# reachability is now a compile-time `#error` in stage90.h rather than a sentence in a comment -
# falsified by raising the deadline past the 32-bit tick delta and watching the build refuse.
#
# **THIS ARM WAS BUILT TWICE AND ONLY THE SECOND BUILD IS RECORDED HERE.** The first build
# (`stage90-qcdt.img` 23fa1138..., parked at out/stage90/frozen/armed-selftest-wdog-23fa1138/) put the
# self-test's spin BEFORE `build_stage90_apple_dt()`. A spin that never returns makes that static
# function unreachable, the compiler drops it, and the image then carries ONE copy of
# `up_style_idle_exit=1` where 515's repair has to be in two command lines - so the gate refused it at
# exactly that clause, after its bytes had already been parked and recorded. The repair is a placement:
# the self-test now sits AFTER the DT build (the watchdog is still armed before anything else, so the
# builder runs under the hardware net), the 515 leg in `build.sh` passes without any skip, and the image
# carries the token twice. **The first build's park is left in place and named, and it must not be sent**
# - it is not a set in this file, and no line below matches it. The `build.sh` clause this step had
# briefly added to SKIP the 515 leg for a self-test build was deleted with it: the gate re-runs that
# property itself, so a skip could only ever have let through an image the gate then refuses.
#
# THE TOKEN COMES FROM THE PAYLOAD, NOT THE ENTRY, and it is the one naming rule this file breaks. The
# three sets above are named for the entry image because the entry image was what changed. Here the entry
# is byte-identical to armed-seam-poc-a43304f2 (a43304f2..., the seam arm, SEAM_POC=1 SEAM_MEASURE=0
# IDLE_NO_SLEEP=0, unchanged) - the payload never reaches the handoff on this arm, so the entry's seam
# switches cannot affect it either way - and what changes is the payload's own switch. The token is the
# first eight of the payload qcdt's hash, i.e. of the artifact `fastboot boot` sends.
#
# SUPERSEDES ONE SENTENCE RATHER THAN EDITING IT. The role= of the acting arm's stage90-qcdt.img line
# above reads "the arm the next press sends"; from this step that is this set. The line is not rewritten
# - a step's record is not tidied by a later step, the same rule the parked absolute-pathed manifest
# follows above - and this paragraph is the correction.
#
# MEASURED 2026-09-25, each file hashed in place in out/stage90/frozen/armed-selftest-wdog-ef0361a2/.

#
# ====================================================================================================
# 678: armed-seam-endrun-88972ba9 - the seam PUBLISHES ITS PAIR AND THEN ENDS THE RUN ON PURPOSE
# ====================================================================================================
#
# WHY THIS ARM EXISTS, AND IT IS 677's READING THAT ASKED FOR IT. The press was fired 2026-09-25 and
# the device came back (`RUNNER EXIT=0`, capture 2062 B, sha 542edb59...) - so the bet "the device
# returns whatever happens" is now measured, not asserted. But the payload lived AT MOST 22 SECONDS:
# the clock in the capture jumps at 08:05:19, the kernel's own start is 08:05:41, and Android's
# `2717:0368` binds at 08:06:03. The arm that was owed asked about the hardware watchdog's 28-second
# countdown and about the 90-second bounded spin, and a run that ends in 22 seconds reaches NEITHER -
# so the owed question came back UNANSWERED a second time, for a reason this time: not a hang, but an
# early end. Both of 666's nets (the bite and the bounded spin) are downstream of a payload that did
# not live long enough to be bitten. It also means the measurement that arm was built to make can never
# be made by a run that has to RETURN to report it: 663's point - a hang destroys the reading, because
# the log lives in DRAM and only a return can reach it - applies to an early end too.
#
# SO THE NEXT ARM DOES NOT RETURN AT ALL. The frontier (the boot dies at the idle exit's `pop {fp, pc}`)
# is diagnosed by the pair `entry_seam_flush` publishes - `a1` and `b1`, the slot's value before and
# after the flush. Returning to the idle exit to publish them is what every seam arm so far has done,
# and it is the step that dies. This arm publishes the same pair and then ends the run through the
# ENTRY IMAGE'S OWN reset path: `RESTART_REASON (0x0fa0065c) <- 0x78665501`, `dsb sy`, then
# `PSHOLD (0xfc4ab000) <- 0`, `dsb sy`, then `for (;;) wfe`. That is exactly `entry_epilogue`'s tail,
# which is how EVERY returning run comes back ([[mi4-the-run-ends-at-entry-epilogue]]) - so this arm
# uses the one reset mechanism this project has measured to work, rather than inventing one.
#
# **THE DESIGN GOT SMALLER BETWEEN 663 AND THIS STEP, AND BOTH SIMPLIFICATIONS ARE MEASUREMENTS.**
# 663 section 3 specified a new literal and a second `entry_mmio_section` install. 674 section 1
# measured that neither is needed: the payload already maps BOTH registers into `stage90_candidate_l1`,
# the L1 the handed-off kernel runs under (`xnu_arm_vm_init_full_pmap.c:410-416`) - `0xf9000000` (the
# GIC megabyte) and `0xfc400000` (PS_HOLD at `0xfc4ab000`). 673 section 3 then measured that no switch
# ends a run at the seam (`entry_seam_flush` publishes and returns at `entry_trace.c:2316`, and all
# three seam arms return to the exit that dies), so the ending is about ten lines behind a new switch -
# and that this switch is a MODIFIER of an existing arm rather than a new interception.
#
# THE ENTRY IMAGE IS WHAT CHANGED, SO THE TOKEN IS THE ENTRY'S. That is the rule the three sets above
# follow and the exception 666 broke only because its payload was what changed. Here the payload's own
# switch dump is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the seam arm's and the sleeper's (`6c2b6038...`) - the entry
# switches are not in it - so the payload record cannot name this arm at all, and the set is
# identifiable from `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt`, which is the file whose hash is a member of the set.
#
# THE PAYLOAD MOVED TOO, BY 136 BYTES, AND NONE OF IT IS THIS STEP'S. `stage90.bin` is 6,015,492 here
# against 6,015,356 in armed-seam-poc-a43304f2, and the difference is commit 666's repair of
# `timebase_elapsed_us` (a 32-bit product that could not exceed 44,739,242us against a 90,000,000us
# deadline), which landed AFTER that arm's payload was built. It is not this step's edit, and it is
# measured rather than argued: `timebase.c` compiled against the current `stage90.h` and against HEAD's
# is byte-identical, and the payload rebuilt with this step's three source edits stashed is
# byte-identical to the payload rebuilt with them applied (`9fd85093...`, `94c95342...` both ways). The
# edits are a comment correction in `cache_ops.c`/`entry_stubs.c` (676's 2048-set x 8-way x 128-byte L2
# decode, where the comment said 4096) and a one-second tick inside the bounded spin, which is behind
# `STAGE90_HW_WATCHDOG_SELFTEST` and that switch is 0 in this arm - so both are inert in these bytes.
#
# **THIS RECORD IS WRITTEN BEFORE THE GATE HAS PASSED, DELIBERATELY, AND 673 section 3 IS WHY**: the
# order is edit -> build -> park -> record -> gate -> re-arm. The gate is expected to REFUSE this set on
# its first run and the refusal is not a defect in these bytes: `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_END_RUN` is the
# build's fourteenth arm key and the peer lane's `preflight_boot_check.sh` `ENTRY_CFG_KEYS` does not yet
# carry it, so its converse clause ("carries key(s) this gate does not print") fires. That is the
# cross-lane pair ([[mi4-file-lanes]]): the key-set equality is the build's half and the gate's half,
# and the gate's half belongs to the lane that owns that file. **No press may be armed for this set
# until the gate is green on it.**
#
# SUPERSEDES ONE SENTENCE RATHER THAN EDITING IT, the same rule the paragraph above follows. The
# role= of the armed-selftest-wdog-ef0361a2 qcdt line reads "...and-THE-ARM-THE-NEXT-PRESS-SENDS...";
# from this step that is this set. That line is not rewritten.
#
# MEASURED 2026-09-25, each file hashed in place in out/stage90/frozen/armed-seam-endrun-88972ba9/.
# Nine of these eleven differ from armed-seam-poc-a43304f2 and the two that agree are the payload's own
# switch dump and the fixture.

#
# ====================================================================================================
# 686: armed-post-endrun-ee7aca87 - the ending moves to the FAR SIDE of the pop
# ====================================================================================================
#
# WHY THIS ARM EXISTS, AND IT IS 685 THAT ASKED FOR IT. 685 localized the clobber's window to the four
# instructions between `SCTLR.C` and the `pop` and did not find a writer there. There is none, because
# the writer is not in the exit at all: it is this project's OWN instrument. `cpu_idle` loads the idle
# loop's deadline into `r4` (`8000d98c: ldm r6, {r4, r7}`, `r6 = cpu_data + 0xe0`, so `r4 = rtcPop`) and
# `r4` is callee-saved, so it is still there when `cpu_idle` calls this build's
# `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_enter`, whose first instruction is `8047c904: strd r4, [sp, #-12]!`. The
# enter wrapper's post-prologue sp is `X - 8`, so that store lands at `X - 12`; the exit's own wrapper
# entry sp is `X - 8` and `platform_cache_idle_exit`'s `push {fp, lr}` puts its frame at `S = X - 16` -
# so `X - 12` is `[S + 4]`, the `pc` slot of `pop {fp, pc}` at `0x8004633c`. **The word the `pop` dies
# on is planted by the arm's own wrapper, one phase earlier, with the cache ON** - and the exit's
# `push`, which runs with `SCTLR.C` clear (measured on every arm: `xnu_live_seam_sctlr=0x30c57879`), is
# written to DRAM and cannot correct the cached copy. Apple's `FlushPoU_Dcache` is L1-only, so the stale
# copy survives one level below it, and the `pop` - run with `C` set again - is answered by that copy.
# This is the mechanism behind every cell the tree has measured, and 521 already carried its
# consequence in `entry_slot_capture.h` ("`E = X - 8` is what puts the idle enter wrapper's
# `strd r4, [sp, #-12]!` at the address the exit's `pop` reads as `pc`").
#
# TWO CONSEQUENCES, AND THE FIRST ONE CORRECTS A READING THE TREE HAS CARRIED SINCE 652. The seam's
# pair (`b0`/`b1` before Apple's flush, `a0`/`a1` after it) is taken with `SCTLR.C` clear, and a
# `C`-clear load to Normal memory is non-cacheable - so the pair is a DRAM reading, and DRAM is where
# the exit's `push` is correct by construction. `b1 == a1` is therefore a property of how the pair is
# read rather than a verdict about the line: 652 read the equal pair as "the flush did not write DRAM",
# and the stronger and correct reading is that this measurement cannot see the cache at all. 653 section
# 3 asked whether the pair could answer that question; it cannot.
#
# THE SECOND CONSEQUENCE IS THAT 653's OPERATION ARM IS THE RIGHT REPAIR FOR THIS MECHANISM, so its one
# press is 662 section 4's FIRST explanation rather than a mystery. `FlushPoC_DcacheRegion(slot, 8)`
# issues `DCCIMVAC` (clean and invalidate TO THE POINT OF COHERENCY - it walks whole 64-byte lines), the
# two words are restored with the cache still off, and after that the `pop` should miss at every level
# and be answered by the frame the exit pushed. The arm was pressed exactly once, at 03:53:50, and did
# not come back (exit 2, no log, a power press). 662 section 4 refused to claim that explanation with
# n = 1 and a hang that destroys the reading, and **this arm is how the explanation becomes a reading
# without letting the machine run on into whatever hung it.**
#
# WHAT THIS ARM IS: `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_RUN=1` calls the SAME `entry_seam_end_run` - one definition,
# byte-for-byte the body 678's arm calls - from the far end of `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit`'s body,
# after the real exit has returned and after the post site has published, instead of from inside the
# seam. The interception, the two reads, the operation, the restore and both publishes are word for word
# the operation arm's. The switch is refused without `SEAM_POC=1` (an ending behind an operation the
# image does not have would be read as a verdict about a window nothing repaired) and refused together
# with `SEAM_END_RUN=1` (two sites for one ending), in `entry_trace.c` with `#error` and in
# `build_entry.sh` with a `case` refusal.
#
# THE READING IS ONE KEY AND ITS TWO CELLS ARE OPPOSITE, AND BOTH COME BACK WITH A LOG:
#   * `xnu_live_slot_post_calls` PRESENT (>= 1) - the exit RETURNED with the operation behind it. That
#     is 662 section 4's first explanation measured, and the frontier is not the `pop` on this arm. The
#     run then ends at the ending, deliberately.
#   * `xnu_live_slot_post_calls` ABSENT - the `pop` died with the operation in the image: 574-park
#     repeated with the repair enabled, a cell the tree has never filled.
# A run that dies in the `pop` reaches `entry_epilogue` exactly as the 678 arm's non-return did, and the
# ending's own first store is the section translation fault 684 measured - so no cell of this press
# needs the boot to survive, and no cell leaves the machine running into an unknown hang.
#
# THE ENTRY IMAGE IS WHAT CHANGED, SO THE TOKEN IS THE ENTRY'S, AND HERE THAT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN
# ARGUED. Every one of the 544,077 differing bytes between this payload and the 678 park's payload lies
# inside the embedded entry blob - the difference lists are equal after a uniform shift of 494,236 bytes,
# which is `stage90_xnu_entry_blob`'s VMA (0x80a9c) minus the payload's base (0x8000) - so **not one
# byte of the payload's own code differs**, and `stage90-build-config.txt` is byte-identical to the 678
# arm's and the 653 arm's (`6c2b6038...`), which is why that file cannot name this arm. The set is
# identified by `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt`, whose last two arm lines read `SEAM_END_RUN=0` and
# `POST_END_RUN=1` beside `SEAM_POC=1`, `SEAM_MEASURE=0`, `SLOT_NULL=1`. The entry `bin` is 5,519,996
# bytes here and in the 678 arm - the switch changes no code size - and two independent invocations of
# `build_entry.sh` minutes apart produced a BIT-IDENTICAL `bin` (`ee7aca87...` twice), which is the
# reproducibility claim that matters for an arm.
#
# THE CROSS-LANE HALF, AND IT IS THIS STEP'S SECOND OF ITS KIND (the first was 683's, one key over).
# `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_RUN` is the build's fifteenth arm key and the peer lane's
# `preflight_boot_check.sh` `ENTRY_CFG_KEYS` gained it in this step under the operator's standing
# authorisation for exactly this change, together with the two prose counts that name the variant subset
# (nine -> ten). Without it the gate's converse clause ("carries key(s) this gate does not print") would
# refuse every press of this arm before the device was touched, which is what happened to 678's arm. No
# safety clause moves: the entry-sources manifest comparison, the storage tripwire, the arm-identity
# comparison and the exit census are byte-unchanged. **THE GATE IS EXPECTED GREEN on this set**, unlike
# the 678 record above, which was written expecting a refusal. One thing was NOT taken: the gate has no
# clause refusing two ENDINGS in one record the way it refuses `SEAM_POC` and `SEAM_MEASURE` together -
# the build refuses that pair first, and adding a second refusal to that lane's file would widen this
# crossing beyond a name.
#
# **NO PRESS MAY BE ARMED FOR THIS SET UNTIL THE GATE IS GREEN ON IT**, the same rule the 678 record
# states: edit -> build -> park -> record -> gate -> re-arm. The 678 arm was spent by the 2026-09-25
# press and came back (`mechanics` 684), the armed window for it closed on its own at 13:21:30 UTC, and
# no firer was alive when these bytes were built.
#
# MEASURED 2026-09-25 13:40 UTC, each file hashed in place in
# out/stage90/frozen/armed-post-endrun-ee7aca87/ and compared against the live out/stage90/ (all eleven
# agree). NINE of these eleven differ from armed-seam-endrun-88972ba9 and the two that agree are
# `stage90-build-config.txt` and `stage90_fixture.macho` - the two files that do not contain the entry
# image. That is the whole reason the count is nine rather than the one artifact whose switch moved: the
# entry blob is EMBEDDED in the payload, so every artifact downstream of it moves with it, and every one
# of them keeps its size (6,015,492 / 6,077,564 / 6,019,072 / 8,540,160 / 5,519,996 / 6,698,696 bytes
# here and in the 678 arm) - the switch changes no code size anywhere.
# The 678 set's qcdt role line reads "...and-the-arm-the-next-press-sends..."; from this step that is
# THIS set. That line is not rewritten, the same rule the 678 record followed against ef0361a2's.

#
# ====================================================================================================
# 688: THE PASS-COUNT ARM - `armed-post-endrun2-57d441cc`. The 686 arm ended the run on the FIRST post
# call of `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit`, and the press that sent it (2026-09-25, one press, exit 0,
# 20 s) came back with `xnu_live_slot_post_calls=0x00000001`: the idle exit's `pop {fp, pc}` RETURNED and
# the run ended past it, on purpose. What that press could not say is how far past the exit the boot
# gets, because the arm ended the run at the first opportunity it had. This set is the same arm with the
# ending moved one call later: `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_RUN=2` calls the same `entry_seam_end_run` only when
# `g_slot_post.calls >= 2`, so one whole idle pass completes first and the log's own `slot_post_calls`
# becomes a PROGRESS COUNT rather than a yes/no - it reads 2 on a boot that reached the ending and 1 on a
# boot that died between the passes. **The switch's DOMAIN widened (1 -> 2); its NAME did not**, so no
# gate key list moves, the peer lane's `ENTRY_CFG_KEYS` needs nothing, and this step has no cross-lane
# half at all. The gate is expected GREEN on this set.
#
# MEASURED IN THE ELF, not inferred from the switch: `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit` now loads
# `g_slot_post.calls` at 0x8047c9f0 (`ldr r3, [r3, #24]`), tests it at `8047c9f4: cmp r3, #1` /
# `8047c9f8: bhi 0x8047ca08` - unsigned `calls > 1`, which is `>= 2`, the form the compiler chose for
# this N - and calls `entry_seam_end_run` at 0x8047ca08. The returning path keeps its 8 bytes of
# give-back (`8047ca00: add sp, sp, #4` / `8047ca04: pop {pc}`), so both the `#error`-guarded range in
# `entry_trace.c` and the wrapper's own layout clause hold. **The 686 arm had no test at all**: its
# source called `entry_seam_end_run` unconditionally (`#if STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_RUN` with the value used
# as a flag, not as a count), so its wrapper's last `bl` is at 0x8047c9e8 with no load, no `cmp` and no
# branch anywhere in the body. That is why "POST_END_RUN=1" and "unconditional" are the same arm, and it
# is stated here because the 687 press's `slot_post_calls=1` was read as a pass count when it is also the
# only value an arm that ends at the first call could ever print. The count becomes a reading at N=2 and
# not before.
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, and this is the arm's one cost: it does not bound a hang. If the SECOND pass dies
# where the first one did not, the ending never fires and this run is a no-return (exit 2) - the same
# cost as the five hangs before 686, paid with a power press and not with the device. An arm that would
# bound that needs a net armed before the idle loop (666's hardware-watchdog self test, whose bite has
# never been observed to fire) or a CNTVCT deadline ending; NEITHER is in these bytes. This record says
# so plainly because a reader who finds exit 2 here must not read it as the runner failing.
#
# THE ORDER, AND ONE THING THIS STEP GOT WRONG AND MEASURED: edit -> build -> park -> record -> gate ->
# re-arm. `build_entry.sh` was edited a second time AFTER the first 688 entry build (a `say` string, the
# `_ord` ordinal), so that build's manifest named a `build_entry.sh` that no longer existed and the
# gate's entry-source clause would have refused the tree before the device was touched. The entry image
# was rebuilt with `STAGE90_ENTRY_ARM_CHANGE=1`, and the two invocations produced the SAME bytes
# (57d441cc..., 5,519,996 of them, elfs included) - the reproducibility bullet from 686 holding at a
# different switch value. The payload from the first build was KEPT, and only after measuring the thing
# rather than reasoning about it: the entry bin occurs verbatim and exactly once inside
# `out/stage90/stage90-qcdt.img`, at offset 496284, all 5,519,996 bytes ([[mi4-one-value-two-definitions]]
# in the other direction - the artifact named its own input).
#
# MEASURED 2026-09-25 14:26 UTC, each file hashed in place in
# out/stage90/frozen/armed-post-endrun2-57d441cc/ and compared against the live out/stage90/ (all eleven
# agree). NINE of these eleven differ from armed-post-endrun-ee7aca87, and the two that agree are again
# `stage90-build-config.txt` and `stage90_fixture.macho` - the two files that do not contain the entry
# image. Every one of the eleven keeps its exact size (the same byte counts as the 686 park's in all
# eleven cases): this switch changes no code size anywhere.
#
# THE PAYLOAD HALF WAS MEASURED DIRECTLY, not by a diff of diffs. `stage90.bin` differs from the 686
# park in 529,487 bytes and EVERY one of them is inside the embedded entry blob, whose span in
# `stage90.bin` is 494236..6014232. `stage90.img` and `stage90-qcdt.img` differ in 529,507, and the
# extra 20 are at file offsets 576..595, inside the boot image's command line (cmdline offset 514..533)
# - a 20-byte digest the build writes into the command line that follows the entry image. That field is
# not this step's: the SAME 20 bytes at the SAME offsets differ between armed-post-endrun-ee7aca87 and
# armed-seam-endrun-88972ba9. **No payload code differs.** The 686 set's qcdt role line above reads
# "...and-the-arm-the-next-press-sends..."; from this step that is THIS set. That line is not rewritten,
# the same rule the 686 record followed against 678's.

set=armed-post-endrun2-57d441cc sha256=52bc9c357068b791f777bb9abd2e879fa5b0b27364267220b01215dc5faec97b bytes=1744 file=stage90_fixture.macho role=fixture-macho-same-bytes-in-every-arm-but-a-manifest-member-all-the-same
#
# 690: THE CLOCK ARM - `armed-post-endticks-b12616bc`. 688 ended the run at a COUNT (the second post
# call); this one ends it at a CLOCK (an elapsed-ticks deadline) at the same site and through the same
# call. `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_TICKS=115200000` replaces `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_RUN=2`; the two are
# mutually exclusive, refused by `entry_trace.c` (`#error` on both set) and by `build_entry.sh`.
#
# WHY TICKS AND NOT MILLISECONDS, and where the rate comes from. 115,200,000 is 6000 ms at the 19200
# ticks/ms the payload's own device tree states - 3x the fixture's 2000 ms park (`PARK_MS` in
# `entry_ramdisk.s`), so the boundary lands inside a park and not between two. But that rate is a
# COMMENT and a device-tree claim: nothing in this project has ever measured it (grepped). So the
# switch is in the units the hardware counts - CNTVCT ticks, via `entry_counter()` - and the arm
# PUBLISHES the hardware's own statement of the rate, `CNTFRQ`, as `xnu_live_post_cntfrq`.
# `run_and_capture.sh` computes milliseconds from the log's own rate when it is present and PRINTS which
# source it used, so 6000 ms in this record is the expected reading and not an assumed one.
#
# MEASURED IN THE ELF, not inferred from the switch:
#  * `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit` (0x8047cad8) keeps its EIGHT-byte frame (`str r4, [sp, #-8]!` /
#    `str lr, [sp, #4]`), gives it back (`add sp, sp, #8`) and ends in a TAIL BRANCH, not a call:
#    `8047cb44: ldr r1, [r3, #24]` - `g_slot_post` 0x805100f8 + 24, the post site's own `calls` field, so
#    no new counter and no new site - then `8047cb48: b 8047b530 <entry_post_clock>`. **This frame IS the
#    slot**: the exit's `push {fp, lr}` writes *this* `sp` and its `pop {fp, pc}` reads it back, so a
#    16-byte frame moves the reading to an address that is real and quiet and wrong. The 690 build caught
#    exactly that - an inline first version of the clock came out `strd r4, [sp, #-16]!` - which is why
#    the clock is `noinline`.
#  * `entry_post_clock` (0x8047b530) takes `now` and `calls`. Its first-call path (`cmp r1, #1` / `bls`
#    at 0x8047b530/0x8047b540) reads CNTFRQ (`mrc 15, 0, r2, cr14, cr0, {0}`, 0x8047b5e4) and stores the
#    baseline: `g_post_clock_freq` 0x805a0570, `g_post_clock_t0` 0x805a0574, `g_post_clock_elapsed`
#    0x805a0578 - three `static uint32_t`, 12 bytes, which is why `.bss` grew 8 and not 12.
#  * the deadline is a `mov`/`movt` pair - `mov r3, #0xd000` / `movt r3, #0x6dd` = 0x06ddd000 =
#    115,200,000 - compared by `sub r0, r0, r2` / `cmp r0, r3` (r2 = t0) / `bcc` at 0x8047b55c-0x8047b568.
#  * `entry_seam_end_run` is at 0x8047b47c in BOTH ELFs - it did not move - while the wrapper moved from
#    0x8047c994 to 0x8047cad8 (+0x344). The ending is `bl entry_seam_end_run` at 0x8047b5b8, whose
#    IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING instruction is `bl entry_live_write` at 0x8047b5b4: the publish is the last
#    thing before the ending, *locally*, which is the clause the build checks. The stronger test - "the
#    ending is the function's last call by address" - is not a fact about the function (gcc keeps the
#    first-call branch and a second copy of the publish after the ending's block), and the build's own
#    failure text says that rather than pretending otherwise.
#  * the four live keys are in the image verbatim: `xnu_live_post_t0`, `xnu_live_post_cntfrq`,
#    `xnu_live_post_elapsed`, `xnu_live_post_end_calls`. The fourth is published on the ending path ONLY,
#    which is what makes the negative cell readable. The arm's identity is published by the seam as
#    `xnu_live_seam_post_end_ticks` (0x06ddd000) beside `xnu_live_seam_post_end_run` (0).
#
# PRE-REGISTERED HERE, BEFORE ANY PRESS, because the ending moved one call deeper and the abort records
# the call site: the ending is reached by a TAIL BRANCH out of the wrapper, so the `bl` that leaves `lr`
# for `entry_seam_end_run` is the one inside `entry_post_clock` at 0x8047b5b8. This arm's abort should
# therefore read **`xnu_live_sleh_lr=0x8047b5bc`** (the instruction after it, `sub r3, r1, #1`), where the
# 688 arm read 0x8047ca0c (after its counted `bl` at 0x8047ca08). `xnu_live_sleh_pc` stays 0x8047b488,
# the restart-reason store at `entry_seam_end_run+0xc`; and the payload's `cpu_idle` calls this wrapper at
# `8000da3c: bl 8047cad8`, so the wrapper's own return address (0x8000da40) is in `lr` up to 0x8047cb3c
# and is NOT what the abort should show. Three readings that must agree on this arm: the seam's
# `post_end_ticks`, `post_end_run=0`, and this `lr`.
#
# THE NEGATIVE CELL, and this arm's one cost. Ending on a clock makes the ending INDEPENDENT of how many
# passes happen - the first arm here whose ending counts nothing - and it moves the negative from "the
# counter did not reach N" to "the machine stopped before the clock ran out". If a pass dies where the
# first one did not, the ending never fires and this run is a no-return (exit 2), the same cost as 688's,
# paid with a power press and not with the device. And on an arm whose ending is a store that ABORTS,
# a no-return cannot be told from a hang by the log at all - the log only comes back with a return. No
# net is armed in these bytes: 666's hardware-watchdog self test is a different arm.
#
# THE ORDER, AND WHAT THIS STEP GOT WRONG BEFORE ANYTHING WAS BUILT: edit -> build -> park -> record ->
# gate -> re-arm. Four separate defects were caught by the build's own clauses and by nothing else - the
# 16-byte frame above; a tail-branched early return that put a publish AFTER the ending; the "last call
# by address" clause being a claim about gcc's layout rather than about the arm; and three shell
# variable-name slips in the new image clauses, each of which made a CORRECT image fail. The build was
# walked from exit 1 to exit 0 / 0 FAILs, and the shape it measures is the shape written above.
#
# MEASURED 2026-09-25 15:09 UTC, each file hashed in place in
# out/stage90/frozen/armed-post-endticks-b12616bc/ and compared against the live out/stage90/ (all eleven
# agree). NINE of these eleven differ from armed-post-endrun2-57d441cc, and the two that agree are again
# `stage90-build-config.txt` and `stage90_fixture.macho` - the two files that do not contain the entry
# image. Unlike every park before it (the 688 record states all eleven kept their exact byte counts),
# TWO of the nine GROW: `xnu_arm_entry.elf` 6,698,696 -> 6,698,832 (+136; `readelf`: `.text` 0x50e900 ->
# 0x50eae0 = +480, `.bss` NOBITS 0x05cb40 -> 0x05cb48 = +8, `.data` unmoved at the same file offset,
# entry point 0x80000074, BSS_START 0x80543a80) and `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt` 810 -> 847 (+37 = the new
# `STAGE90_XNU_POST_END_TICKS=115200000` line with its newline). The entry bin keeps 5,519,996 bytes. The
# sources manifest differs from the 688 park's in exactly three lines - the entry-hash line, the
# `build_entry.sh` line and the `entry_trace.c` line - and both of those last two were re-hashed against
# the LIVE files (ff2f5e17… and 67e1e2d3…), which is the 688 lesson kept.
#
# THE PAYLOAD HALF WAS MEASURED DIRECTLY AND CLOSES EXACTLY. `stage90.bin` differs from the 688 park in
# 586,101 bytes: 586,096 of them inside the embedded entry blob (span 494236..6014232 in `stage90.bin`,
# with 1,260 bytes of payload after it; 496284 in the qcdt, the same offset as 688's) and FIVE in
# `stage90_xnu_entry_run`, every one of which is the entry image's bss SIZE - `movw r3, #0x5c0` /
# `movt r3, #0x805a` -> `movw r3, #0x5c8` at VA 0x4ac40 (BSS_END 0x805a05c0 -> 0x805a05c8; the compiler
# switches from a rotate-imm8 `mov` to `movw` because 0x5c8 is not an 8-bit-rotated immediate) and two
# `movw r2/r1, #0xcb40` -> `#0xcb48` at 0x4ad04/0x4ad18 (the bss byte count 0x5cb40 -> 0x5cb48, passed to
# `memset` and logged as `xnu_entry_bss_bytes`). `stage90.img` and `stage90-qcdt.img` differ in 586,121,
# and the extra 20 are at file offsets 576..595. **Five bytes, all derivable from the 12 bytes of clock
# statics; no payload code differs.** The 586,096 inside the blob arrive as 64,485 runs, mostly two-byte
# runs at four-byte spacing - the `movw` immediates renumbered because `.text` grew 480 bytes - with the
# largest single run 4,394 bytes.
#
# AND THAT LAST FIELD IS WHERE 688's RECORD IS WRONG, corrected here because the wrong sentence is in
# THIS file and a reader would carry it forward. Bytes 576..595 are not "inside the boot image's command
# line (cmdline offset 514..533)": the v0 header's command line is 64..575 and 576 begins the 32-byte
# `id` field, of which the first 20 are the digest and the last 12 are zero. Nor is it "a digest the
# build writes into the command line that follows the entry image": it is `mkbootimg`'s own id over the
# kernel, and it is REPRODUCED here exactly - `sha1(stage90.bin || len(stage90.bin) || ramdisk(empty) ||
# 0 || second(empty) || 0)` = b4895a518bd220d63aba5ccfd5582afb99031cfa = the field. It differs between
# arms because the payload differs, which is what an image digest is for, and it is not a byte of this
# arm's code.
#
# THE 688 SET'S qcdt role line above reads "...and-the-arm-the-next-press-sends..."; from this step that
# is THIS set. That line is not rewritten - the same rule the 686 record followed against 678's.
set=armed-post-endticks-b12616bc sha256=f5ed8a0b9f390e7ee79db204c8efd909c4d8a6a6962cab43b9eb8fc3c8a3a278 bytes=8540160 file=stage90-qcdt.img role=qcdt-wrapped-payload-this-is-what-fastboot-boot-sends-and-the-arm-the-next-press-sends-STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY-1-this-payload-wraps-the-POST_END_TICKS-115200000-entry-image-and-only-five-bytes-of-its-own-code-differ-from-the-688-parks-all-derivable-from-the-12-bytes-of-clock-statics

set=armed-post-endticks-b12616bc sha256=52bc9c357068b791f777bb9abd2e879fa5b0b27364267220b01215dc5faec97b bytes=1744 file=stage90_fixture.macho role=fixture-macho-same-bytes-in-every-arm-but-a-manifest-member-all-the-same
#
# 692: THE STORAGE ARM - `armed-storage-0da7313b`. 690 ended the run on a clock and came back with 8.008 s
# of live kernel and a measured CNTFRQ; this arm keeps **every one of that arm's switches** and adds one:
# `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=1` makes `xnu_arm_boot/entry_storage.c`'s probe real. Its call is in
# `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit` on the wrapper's first return - the same site 690 takes its baseline
# on - immediately after `entry_note_pcx` and before the clock's block, so the probe's records precede the
# ending's last one.
#
# WHAT THE ARM DOES, AND WHY IT IS THE STORAGE LINE'S FIRST ACT ON THE DEVICE. 529/530/531/532 are the
# host-side readings of the eMMC controller: 531 read the vendor driver's two named windows
# (`hc_mem 0xf9824900`, `core_mem 0xf9824000`) and the mode bit at `CORE_HC_MODE 0x78`; 532 read the
# prerequisite under all of it - **neither window is in any table this image builds, and both are inside
# the same 1 MB section** (`0xf9824000 >> 20 == 0xf9824900 >> 20 == 0xF98`), so the arm's first act is
# ONE `entry_mmio_section(0xf9824000, 0xf9824000, ...)` and not two. The probe is 532 section 6's steps 2
# and 3: install, then read. It writes NOTHING. `xnu_live_storage_writes=0` is published as the arm's own
# safety reading, because the vendor's own probe opens with three writes (`CORE_HC_MODE <- 0`,
# `CORE_SW_RST`, `HC_MODE_EN`) and 531 section 8 gives a fourth reason to avoid the surface: writing 0 to
# `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` **is** a bus-off request.
#
# THE GATE, READ FIRST, AND IT IS THE ONE RISK THIS ARM TAKES. A load from a block whose clock is off is
# a bus wait nothing ends - the one failure this project cannot read a log out of. So before the section
# is installed the probe reads SDCC1's branch gate (`BCR 0x04C0` / `CBCR 0x04C4` at `0xFC400000`, the `+4`
# measured from 528's UART pair) and skips the six loads when `BIT(0)` is clear. That megabyte is already
# mapped in the context the wrapper runs in, proved by `entry_epilogue`'s own PS_HOLD store at
# `0xfc4ab000` on every returning run. The interlock can only fire in the safe direction.
#
# THE MEASURED ELF SHAPE. `entry_storage_probe` is at **0x8000cff8** and the wrapper's `bl` to it is at
# **0x8047cb2c** (the wrapper still opens `str r4, [sp, #-8]!`, keeps its 8-byte frame and still ends
# `ldr r1, [r3, #24]` / `b 0x8047b530 <entry_post_clock>`). In the probe's body: `movw r4,#0x4000` +
# `movt r4,#0xf982`; the section index materialized as `movw r1,#3992` (0xF98) and published twice;
# `movt r3,#0xfc40` with `ldr r1,[r3,#1216]` (BCR) and `ldr r5,[r3,#1220]` (CBCR) and `and r5,r5,#1`;
# `bl entry_mmio_section` with r0 == r1 == 0xf9824000; and exactly SIX loads off r4 -
# `[r4]`, `[r4,#44]` (0x2C), `[r4,#80]` (0x50), `[r4,#120]` (0x78), `[r4,#2304]` (=hc_mem+0x00),
# `[r4,#2368]` (=hc_mem+0x40) - with **no store to any address based on r4**. The two derived bits are
# `ubfx r1,r6,#7,#1` (`CORE_SW_RST`) and `and r1,r5,#1` (`HC_MODE_EN`).
#
# TWO DEFECTS FOUND AND REPAIRED IN THIS STEP, BOTH IN THIS LANE.
# (1) **The switch reached the record and not the file.** The first build of this arm exited 0 with
# `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` added to `STUB_DEFINES` - which reaches `entry_stubs.c` and
# `entry_timebase.c` and NOT `entry_trace.c`, the file whose `#if` consumes it (`entry_trace.c` has its
# own explicit `-D` list, and the file's own `EXIT_POC_FLUSH` note says so). The record carried `=1`,
# the gate's printable key list agreed, and the disassembly of `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit` held no
# `bl entry_storage_probe` at all. Repaired by moving the flag onto `entry_trace.c`'s command line, and
# closed by a **new clause that reads the assembled wrapper in both directions** (the flag on requires
# exactly one call; the flag off requires none), because nothing else in the build could see the hole.
# (2) **`entry_gic.c` could not compile with the tracer off.** Its five `extern`s sat inside
# `#if STAGE90_ENTRY_GIC_TRACED`, while the no-op arm of `GIC_LIVE` still evaluates its arguments - so
# `STAGE90_ENTRY_TRACE=0`, which `build_entry.sh`'s own default sets, was a build that died at
# `entry_gic.c` with `'g_live_mmio_l1' undeclared`. Measured with the object's own command line and
# `-fsyntax-only`. Repaired by moving the declarations outside the `#if`; a declaration is not code, so
# no traced image moves a byte for it. `entry_storage.c` is written the way the repair leaves its sibling.
#
# PRE-REGISTRATION (no device was touched for any of it).
#   * `xnu_live_storage_loads` = 6 beside `xnu_live_storage_writes` = 0 - the controller answered and
#     nothing was written to it.
#   * `xnu_live_storage_map` = 1 and `_l1` a kernel-window base, `_l1_moved` - 532 section 6 step 2's
#     four refusals told apart by the numbers, not by a sentence.
#   * `xnu_live_storage_gate` = 1 opens the loads; 0 gives `_gated_out` = 1 with `_loads` = 0 (the
#     section installed and the block deliberately not touched).
#   * `xnu_live_storage_mode_bit` (`CORE_HC_MODE & 1`) and `_sw_rst` (`CORE_POWER >> 7 & 1`) - the two
#     bits 531 section 6 left open, and the first reading this project has of the state the bootloader
#     handed over. A `_mci_version` of 0 or 0xffffffff is *the block did not answer*, a reading of this
#     arm and not a failed run.
#   * The NEGATIVE CELL is a NON-RETURN (exit 2): the gate said the branch was on and the block still
#     did not answer, so the six loads never came back. It owes a power press. A mapping failure with a
#     live gate is a translation fault instead and comes back with `xnu_live_sleh_far = 0xf9824000`.
#   * The ending is 690's clock, unmoved, so `xnu_live_post_end_calls` is still this arm's ending cell.
#
# 692 WAS PRESSED ON 2026-09-25 AT 15:57:19 UTC (readiness exit 0, 5/5; gate exit 0 / 568 stdout lines; one
# runner; RUNNER EXIT=0; the device back 17 s after the send, seen via host log + port; capture 598,621 bytes,
# sha256 8783abd0b6a4742f6e4ebc10794de45eb4b69f5270e0e7a6c11e63109a881e19), AND THE GATE PARAGRAPH ABOVE IS
# FALSIFIED BY THE ARM'S OWN LOG - corrected here because the sentence is in THIS file and a reader would
# carry it forward.
#   The paragraph above reads "That megabyte is already mapped in the context the wrapper runs in, proved by
# `entry_epilogue`'s own PS_HOLD store at `0xfc4ab000` on every returning run". **It is false.** The press
# published exactly five keys - `xnu_live_storage_calls=0x00000001`, `_live_state=0x00000001`,
# `_section=0x00000f98`, `_hc_mem_section=0x00000f98`, `_windows_share_section=0x00000001` - and then
# nothing, and its abort is `pc=0x8000d0c4` (= `entry_storage_probe+0xcc`, `ldr r1,[r3,#1216]` with
# r3 = 0xfc400000: the SDCC1 BCR read, the probe's third line) with `fsr=0x00000005`, a SECTION TRANSLATION
# FAULT, `far=0xfc4004c0` = r3 + 0x4c0, and the panic's own `r0 = 0x80487fc8` = the string
# `xnu_live_storage_bcr`. So `0xFC4` is **not** mapped in the context the wrapper runs in. The claim's shape
# is the defect and not its arithmetic: a STORE inside a code path is not proof that an ADDRESS is mapped
# (`mi4-a-device-address-can-be-right-and-undereferenceable`, citing 532 - the experiment this arm's plan
# came from).
#   **And the proof was already contradicted by a measurement in the previous arm's parked capture, which
# the 690 block above did not join.** 690's fatal abort is `pc=0x8047b488` = `entry_seam_end_run+0xc`, the
# `str r3, [r2, #0x65c]` with r2 = 0x0fa00000, i.e. a store to `RESTART_REASON 0x0fa0065c`, and its
# `far_frame` is exactly `0x0fa0065c`. So **690's forced ending faulted on its FIRST store** and never
# reached the `dsb sy`, the PS_HOLD store at `0xfc4ab000` or the `wfe`. No arm has been observed to complete
# the PS_HOLD store; the same tail sits at `0x8000577c-0x80005794` inside `entry_epilogue`, storing to the
# same two addresses, and it is reached from the abort path.
#   The NEGATIVE CELL above named the wrong site: it says a mapping failure with a live gate "comes back with
# `xnu_live_sleh_far = 0xf9824000`". The probe reads the GATE before it installs anything, so the first
# fault address is the gate's megabyte and not the controller's. The SHAPE (a translation fault, logged,
# coming back with a log) was right; the address was not, and the megabyte that had never been measured is
# the gate's.
#   THE NEXT ARM'S FIRST CHANGE IS THEREFORE ONE MORE MAPPING: `entry_mmio_section(0xfc400000, 0xfc400000,
# ...)` before the gate is read. `0xFC4` is a different L1 index from the `0xF98` install, so 532 section
# 3.2's occupied-slot refusal cannot fire and the two calls cannot collide. The same question now applies to
# the reset path's own two stores (`0x0fa0065c` and `0xfc4ab000`, two different unmapped megabytes).
#
# THE 690 SET'S qcdt role line above reads "...the-arm-the-next-press-sends..."; from this step that is
# THIS set, and that line is not rewritten - the same rule the 690 record followed against the 688 set.

set=armed-storage-0da7313b sha256=52bc9c357068b791f777bb9abd2e879fa5b0b27364267220b01215dc5faec97b bytes=1744 file=stage90_fixture.macho role=fixture-macho-same-bytes-in-every-arm-but-a-manifest-member-all-the-same
#
# 693: THE STORAGE ARM'S SECOND MAPPING - `armed-storage-gcc-1fc30bfe`. 692 pressed the storage probe and
# its FIRST device read died: `entry_storage_probe`'s gate read, `ldr r1,[r3,#1216]` with
# `r3 = 0xfc400000`, took a section translation fault (`fsr_frame = 0x00000005`,
# `far_frame = 0xfc4004c0`, and the panic's own `r0 = 0x80487fc8` is the pointer to the string
# `xnu_live_storage_bcr`). The published claim - that the megabyte is "already mapped ... proved by
# `entry_epilogue`'s own PS_HOLD store at `0xfc4ab000` on every returning run" - read a STORE inside a
# code path as evidence that an ADDRESS is mapped, which is the class of
# `mi4-a-device-address-can-be-right-and-undereferenceable` and whose citation is 532. **This arm is
# 692's arm with that claim replaced by the install it needed**: one more `entry_mmio_section` for the
# GATE's megabyte, taken BEFORE the gate is read, with the storage block's own install reordered to
# follow it.
#
# WHY THE REORDER IS SAFE AND WHY IT IS WORTH IT. An install is a PAGE-TABLE write and not an access to
# the medium's controller, so moving the storage block's install ahead of the gate read does not weaken
# the interlock: the gate still guards every one of the six loads. What the reorder buys is that a run
# which ends up gated out still publishes `_map`/`_slot_before`/`_desc`, so "the block was not touched"
# and "the block could not be reached" stay two different cells - the same distinction 692's own record
# asked for and could not make.
#
# THE TWO INSTALLS CANNOT COLLIDE, and that is 532 section 3.2 answered before it can fire: the GATE's
# megabyte is L1 index `0xFC4` (4036) and the storage block's is `0xF98` (3992). Both are in TTBR1's
# table (both VAs are above 0x80000000), both indices are inside 0..4095, and they are different
# indices - so `entry_section_install`'s occupied-slot refusal cannot be how either call ends.
# `xnu_live_storage_gcc_share_section` publishes that they are different, as a reading.
#
# AND THE SAME MEGABYTE IS THE RESET PATH'S. `entry_epilogue`'s two stores are at `0x0fa0065c`
# (RESTART_REASON) and `0xfc4ab000` (PS_HOLD) - two different unmapped megabytes. 684 measured the first
# faulting on 678's arm, and 692's own ninth abort shows the payload's deliberate ending faulting at
# `0x0fa0065c` as well. `0xfc4ab000` shares this arm's `0xFC4` index, so `xnu_live_storage_gcc_map = 1`
# beside a readable `_bcr` is ALSO the measurement that the PS_HOLD half of the reset path becomes
# reachable - the repair 684 named, taken here as a side effect of the gate's own mapping.
#
# THE MEASURED ELF SHAPE (the parked entry elf, read with objdump and not inferred). `entry_storage_probe`
# is at **0x8000cff8** and the wrapper's `bl` to it is unchanged at **0x8047cb2c**. The gate's base is
# `movt r6, #0xfc40` at 0x8000d084 with `mov r6, r3` (r3 is the function's zero) at 0x8000d064, so r6 is
# `0xfc400000` from 0x8000d084 on and every later read through it is that megabyte. The three index keys
# are immediately visible in the immediates: `movw r1,#0xf98` at 0x8000d090 (`_section`) and again at
# 0x8000d0a0 (`_hc_mem_section`), the constant 1 at 0x8000d0b0 (`_windows_share_section`), and
# `movw r1,#0xfc4` at 0x8000d0c0 (`_gcc_section` - and `_gcc_share_section` follows it as r5, which is
# the 0 the guard read, so the compiler folded that comparison to 0). The FIRST `entry_mmio_section` is
# at 0x8000d0ec with `r0 == r1 == r6` (0xfc400000) and the SECOND at 0x8000d144 with `r0 == r1 == r4`
# (0xf9824000, materialised as `movw r4,#0x4000` + `movt r4,#0xf982`); the gate is `ldr r9,[r6,#1216]` at
# 0x8000d1dc and `ldr r6,[r6,#1220]` at 0x8000d1ec with `and r6,r6,#1` at 0x8000d210. **The six loads are
# the same six and they are all reads**: `[r4]`, `[r4,#44]` (0x2C), `[r4,#80]` (0x50), `[r4,#120]` (0x78),
# `[r4,#2304]` (=hc_mem+0x00), `[r4,#2368]` (=hc_mem+0x40) at 0x8000d250-0x8000d268 - and **no store to
# any address based on r4 or r6 anywhere in the function**; the only `str`s are the frame save/restore on
# `sp` (`strd r4,[sp,#-32]!` at 0x8000cffc). The two derived bits are `and r1,r6,#1` for `HC_MODE_EN`
# (0x8000d2f4, where r6 has been reloaded with `hc_mode`) and the `CORE_SW_RST` extract from `CORE_POWER`
# (`ubfx r1,r7,#7,#1` at 0x8000d2e4).
#
# **AND THE SAME FAULT SITE PRINTS A DIFFERENT `r0` IN THIS ARM, WHICH IS A READING AND NOT A MOVED SITE.**
# 692's abort carried `r0 = 0x80487fc8`, and in 692's own entry image that address holds the string
# `xnu_live_storage_bcr` - verified by reading both pools at the same address: `0x80487fc8` is
# `xnu_live_storage_bcr` in `armed-storage-0da7313b` and `xnu_live_storage_gcc_section` in this arm's
# image. The two arms schedule the same pair of loads differently, and that is the whole reason: in 692
# the `_bcr` key's own immediate pair (`movw r0,#0x7fc8` at 0x8000d0b8, `movt` at 0x8000d0c0) is placed
# *between* the base's materialisation and the faulting `ldr r1,[r3,#1216]` at 0x8000d0c4, so the
# leftover `r0` happens to name the very key that load feeds; in this arm the same key's `movw` is placed
# *after* both loads (0x8000d1f4), so `r0` at 0x8000d1dc is still **`0x80488168` (`_ttbr1`)** from the
# key write at 0x8000d1c8/0x8000d1d0. So a repeat fault here would print a pointer five keys back from
# 692's and it would still be the same instruction. **The `pc` is what identifies a fault site; the `r0`
# of a data abort is a leftover register a reader can mis-read as a name.**
#
# THE PAYLOAD HALF IS ONE THING, AND IT IS MEASURED. `stage90-build-config.txt` is
# `6c2b6038d3bcbe30da60084c571682100946a15f191437161dbf1ed1385b547b` - **byte-identical to the 692, 690,
# 688, 686, 678, 653 and sleeper arms'**, so this file cannot name this arm; the set is identified by
# `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt` instead. `stage90.bin`, `stage90.elf`, `stage90.img` and `stage90-qcdt.img`
# all keep **exactly the byte counts of the 692 park** (6015492 / 6077564 / 6019072 / 8540160) and differ
# only in content, so the payload-side change is exactly the embedded entry blob and the entry bss-size
# immediates, as it has been for every arm since the entry blob was introduced.
#
# THE ENTRY-SIDE SWITCHES ARE 692's, UNCHANGED (see `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt`): `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=1`
# beside `SEAM_POC=1`, `SEAM_MEASURE=0`, `SEAM_END_RUN=0`, `POST_END_RUN=0`, `POST_END_TICKS=115200000`,
# `SLOT_NULL=1`, `IDLE_CACHE_ENABLE=0`, `ISTACK_SEPARATE=0`, `IDLE_STACK=1`, `IDLE_NO_SLEEP=0`,
# `REAL_ARM_INIT=1`, `ENTRY_TRACE=1`. **So the ending is still 690's clock** and, because the probe's call
# is placed BEFORE the clock's block, a run that gets past the probe is ended at ~6.0 s on a pass boundary
# exactly as 691 measured - which is what bounds this press.
#
# PRE-REGISTRATION (no device was touched for any of it).
#   * `xnu_live_storage_gcc_map` = 1 and `_gcc_slot_before` = 0 with `_gcc_desc` a block descriptor whose
#     PA field is 0xfc400000 - the gate's megabyte was unmapped and this arm mapped it.
#   * `xnu_live_storage_gate_read` = 1 with `_bcr` and `_cbcr` both readable - **the reading 692 could not
#     take**, and the one that says the GCC block answers.
#   * `xnu_live_storage_gate` = 1 opens the six loads; 0 gives `_gated_out` = 1 with `_loads` = 0.
#   * `xnu_live_storage_map` = 1 (the storage install, second call, its own slot numbers), then
#     `_loads = 6` beside `_writes = 0`, the six words, and the two pre-registered bits `_mode_bit`
#     (`CORE_HC_MODE & HC_MODE_EN`) and `_sw_rst` (`CORE_POWER >> 7 & 1`) - the two bits 531 section 6
#     left open and the first reading this project has of the state the bootloader handed over.
#   * A `_mci_version` of 0 or 0xffffffff is *the block did not answer*, a reading of this arm and not a
#     failed run.
#   * The NEGATIVE CELLS, each a different key: `_gcc_map = 0` with `_gate_read = 0` (the GATE's
#     megabyte could not be installed, and nothing was read through it); `_gate = 0` with
#     `_gated_out = 1` (installed, clocked off, deliberately not touched); `_map = 0` with
#     `_gate_read = 1` (the storage block's install refused after the gate was read).
#   * The map-failure fault address, if one happens anyway, is `xnu_live_sleh_far` = `0xfc400000` for the
#     gate's megabyte and `0xf9824000` for the storage block's - two addressable cells, which is what
#     makes the two installs tellable apart in a fault record.
#   * The ending is 690's clock, unmoved, so `xnu_live_post_end_calls` is still this arm's ending cell.
#
# THE 692 SET'S qcdt role line above reads "...the-arm-the-next-press-sends..."; from this step that is
# THIS set, and that line is not rewritten - the same rule the 692 record followed against the 690 set.

# ================================================================================================
# 694: THIS SET WAS PRESSED, AND IT ANSWERED. Read this block before the 693 one above, whose
# pre-registration is what these sentences confirm or refute - and note that **nothing in the set
# changed**: the arm that was sent is the arm recorded above, byte for byte, so the role line that
# calls `stage90-qcdt.img` "the arm the next press sends" is still true of it.
# ================================================================================================
#
# THE PRESS: 2026-09-25, readiness exit 0 (5/5, adb listing `4a2fe00b` as `device` and the neighbour
# `33e80afe` off both lists), `preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry` **exit 0 / 569 lines**,
# exactly one `run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-gcc-1fc30bfe`, and
# **RUNNER EXIT=0** - returned and captured, "the device came back 24s after this run called
# `fastboot boot` (seen via: host log, serial)". Capture 611,558 B, sha256
# `d5c42bf9ea86d3b8d72084676f372adcb8607384e4ab8c841c5a69d6dfb9ab91`, archived by hand under
# `out/stage90/captures/694-storage-gcc-pressed-2026-09-25-{last_kmsg.txt,run.log,gate.log}` (out/ is
# gitignored, so the capture is not in this record and cannot be). Nothing was flashed, nothing was
# written to any storage controller, and no device state changed but the boot.
#
# THE ANSWER, AND EVERY CELL THE 693 BLOCK PRE-REGISTERED. All twenty-eight keys are in the log, in
# the source's own order, with no abort at any of the probe's loads:
#   * `_gcc_map=1`, `_gcc_slot_before=0`, `_gcc_desc=0xfc41040e` - the GATE's megabyte had an **empty**
#     level-1 slot, so 692's premise was false at the descriptor level and not only at the fault.
#   * `_map=1`, `_slot_before=0`, `_desc=0xf981040e`, `_l1=0x80704000`, `_l1_moved=1`, `_ttbr0`/`_ttbr1`
#     both `0x8070404a` - the controller's megabyte was empty too, and the table the mapper read is NOT
#     the one the console latched (`xnu_live_l1=0x80700000`, `xnu_live_ttbr0=0x8070004a`): 484's finding,
#     reproduced on a returning run.
#   * `_gate_read=1`, `_bcr=0x00000000`, `_cbcr=0x00004ff1`, `_gate=0x00000001` - **the reading 692 could
#     not take.** The GCC block answers and the branch is ENABLED, and `0x4C0`/`0x4C4` as a BCR/CBCR pair
#     is confirmed on hardware rather than inherited from `clock-8974.c` plus the UART pair's own `+4`.
#   * `_gated_out=0`, `_loads=6`, `_writes=0` - the six loads ran and **nothing was written to the
#     block**. `_core_power=0x441` (`CORE_SW_RST` clear), `_mci_data_ctrl=0x8000`,
#     `_mci_version=0x10000011` (not 0, not 0xffffffff: the "did not answer" cell is falsified),
#     `_hc_mode=0x2000`, `_hci_version=0x00000010`, `_capabilities=0x742dc8b2`.
#   * `_sw_rst=0` and **`_mode_bit=0`** - the two bits 531 section 6 left open. `HC_MODE_EN` is CLEAR, so
#     the vendor's mode sequence is a **PREREQUISITE**: `CORE_HC_MODE <- 0`, then `CORE_SW_RST`, then
#     `HC_MODE_EN` are all still ahead of this boot, and `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` is still the register the
#     driver itself avoids (531 section 8: writing 0 to it IS a bus-off request).
#   * `hci_version` and `capabilities` were taken through a block that is **not yet in SDHCI mode**, which
#     is exactly the caveat 531 section 6 raised. They are pre-mode words of that window; the arm claims
#     only that the window answers with non-zero, non-saturated values.
#
# THE ATTRIBUTE, AND A TOOL THAT CANNOT SEE TRE. Both descriptors carry `0x…1040e`, and on this machine
# `SCTLR.TRE` is set (`xnu_live_sctlr=0x30c5787d`), so the C/B bits are a `PRRR` index: the log's own
# `xnu_live_prrr=0x1f08022a` has its first zero two-bit field at **index 3**, and the mapper's recipe
# encodes that index as `B=1, C=1, TEX[0] (bit 12) =0` - the `0x040e` observed. The channel publishes the
# attribute word itself as **`xnu_live_attr=0xc`**, which is `(1<<3)|(1<<2)`, and `PRRR` field 0 is the
# Strongly-ordered encoding. **So both sections are Strongly-ordered shareable device mappings**, which is
# what makes the 693 block's PS_HOLD sentence true with device semantics rather than only as an address.
# **And `tools/decode_armv7_descriptor.py` printed `Reserved` for this combination**: it decoded TEX/C/B as
# a memory type with no `SCTLR.TRE` input, which is the rule for the payload's own tables (their regime has
# TRE clear) and not for XNU's or the entry image's. **Repaired after the press**: `--tre`, `--prrr=` and
# `--nmrr=`; and the repair's first draft named a wrong field (index 7's, not 3's), which the 694 document
# section 4 records beside the evidence that `PRRR` field 0 sits on exactly the two indices Apple's
# `CACHE_ATTRINDX_*` calls DISABLE, while field 0b10 hands the attribute to `NMRR`. The table in
# `docs/reference/pmap-attribute-map.md` is unchanged and is now labelled with the regime it belongs to.
# **And the misnaming is in the entry image's own source**: `entry_stubs.c:2322` says `TEX[2] = i[2]`
# beside the `<< 12` it describes, and bit 12 is `ARM_TTE_BLOCK_TEX0SHIFT`, i.e. the descriptor's TEX[0].
# The arithmetic is right and the name is wrong; it is a comment-only fix, owed, and it waits for the next
# edit inside `xnu_arm_boot/` because that directory is content-hashed into this arm's identity (673).
#
# THE ENDING FIRED AND DID NOT END THE RUN - and this is the one sentence in the 663/678/686/690 line that
# this press corrects. `xnu_live_post_end_calls=0x00000007` is PRESENT, so the ending FIRED on the seventh
# return, with `xnu_live_post_elapsed=0x06e109da` = **115,411,418 ticks against the armed 115,200,000** -
# 211,418 ticks (11.011 ms) past the deadline, 6,011.0 ms of live kernel - and
# `xnu_live_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800` = **19,200,000 Hz**, which is the machine's own statement of the tick
# rate and confirms the device tree's 19200 ticks/ms exactly. Then the ending's own first store faulted:
# the ninth abort is `pc=0x8047b488` = `entry_seam_end_run+0xc` (`str r3,[r2,#0x65c]`),
# `lr=0x8047b5bc` = **exactly the value 690's record pre-registered for this arm**, `far_frame=0x0fa0065c`
# = RESTART_REASON, still in a megabyte no arm has mapped, recovered rather than fatal (`frame_ok=1`, no
# `panic ... sleh_abort`). **And the boot went on**: the log carries exactly 4,837 `xnu_live_` lines and
# the channel reports exactly 4,837 records of 8,192, so every record is here once in write order, and
# three counters continue past the abort - `xnu_live_cls_calls` 0x3d -> **0x3e**, `xnu_live_walk_seq`
# 0x07 -> **0x08**, and a new `xnu_live_path_ret` (0xc05f17b8 against 0xc05f0e18 before). So
# **"this arm ends the run on purpose" means the ending was REACHED and not that it took effect**;
# 678/686/690's sentence needs that qualifier, and the reset that returned the device at 24 s is
# **unattributed** - the third in a row (8 s on 690, 17 s on 692, 24 s here) - with the armed hardware
# watchdog (`hw_watchdog_enabled=1`, counter running, countdown plausible, readback ok) and the payload's
# dead-man as the two candidates that leave no line.
#
# AND THE CONTROL TABLE IS CONFIRMED BY AN ARM THAT MOVED. Episodes 1-8 carry byte-identical `lr`s to
# 692's capture (`0x8029231c`, `0x80293cd0`, `0x800454a0`, `0x8029368c`, `0`, `0`, `0`, `0x80296114`), the
# three user-address episodes (`0x1118`, `0x1124`, `0x11a4`) have not moved at all, and the `pc`s inside
# the entry image have moved `+0xE0` (the `0x8001_7…` pair) and `+0xDC` (the `0x8000_d…` pair) - the entry
# image growing, which is the shift a rebuild should produce and the reason a fault site is identified by
# `pc` minus the image base and not by `pc`. Episode 9 is each arm's own: 692's died in the probe and this
# one in the ending.
#
# WHAT IS STILL OWED, and none of it is in this set. (1) **What actually returns a run** is now three
# measurements (8 s / 17 s / 24 s) and no answer. (2) **The mode sequence is a WRITE and is the next arm**:
# with `_mode_bit=0` it is a prerequisite, so an arm that performs `CORE_HC_MODE <- 0`, `CORE_SW_RST`, the
# poll and `HC_MODE_EN` must be pre-registered as a write before it is built, must not touch
# `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, and must name its polling bound and its failure path. (3) The gate's storage
# tripwire still prints `none` about payloads that carry `0xf982` and `0xfc40` (692 section 4; the peer
# lane's file, reported by message and not edited here). (4) The descriptor decoder's missing `SCTLR.TRE`
# input. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld**: the OS is not observed booting, so the user's condition is
# unmet and no write has been made.
