set=armed-storage-mode-57d55fc9 sha256=52bc9c357068b791f777bb9abd2e879fa5b0b27364267220b01215dc5faec97b bytes=1744 file=stage90_fixture.macho role=fixture-macho-same-bytes-in-every-arm-but-a-manifest-member-all-the-same
#
# ================================================================================================
# 696: THE FIRST WRITE ARM - PARKED, NOT PRESSED. Nothing in this block is a reading: the set above
# is the arm that will be sent, and the press is the next step's act. What is recorded here is what
# was MEASURED about the set before it was parked, because two of those measurements are new.
# ================================================================================================
#
# THE WRITES ARE NOW A CHECKED PROPERTY OF THE ARTIFACT AND NOT A SENTENCE IN A COMMENT. `build_entry.sh`
# gained a **store census** for `entry_storage_probe`, run under the same `-ge 2` rung that turns the
# sequence on: it disassembles the function out of the linked `xnu_arm_entry.elf`, takes every store
# whose base is not `sp` as `<base>:<offset>` in program order, and refuses the build unless the offsets
# on one base are exactly `0x78, 0x00, 0x78, 0x78` in that order and every store on any other base is an
# offset-0 pointer write. The reason it exists is the one register this project has now written down
# twice: `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` lives in the OTHER window (`hc_mem`), and on this SoC **writing 0 to it IS
# a bus-off request** - so "no store is based on `hc_mem`" had to stop being a claim about the source and
# become a reading of the image the gate boots. It is a reading now, in both directions:
#
#   * the rung-2 image (`out/stage90/`, the set above): `base=r5 off=[120 0 120 120] other=[r2:0] bad=[]`
#     - accepted. The four are `str r4,[r5,#120]` (0x8000d344), `str r7,[r5]` (0x8000d380),
#     `str r3,[r5,#120]` (0x8000d470), `str r4,[r5,#120]` (0x8000d4b0), and the only other store in the
#     whole body is the `.bss` first-call guard `str r6,[r2]` (0x8000d068). **No store in this body is
#     based on `hc_mem` at all**, which is what makes `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` unreachable by construction
#     rather than by review.
#   * the 693 park control: `base=r2 off=[0] other=[] bad=[]` - **refused**, which is the direction a
#     check that has only ever passed cannot demonstrate. It is a real image of this project's own
#     rung-1 arm, hand-run through the clause's own text, and it is why the clause is not a tautology.
#   * and the honest wart, recorded rather than smoothed over: the base the clause compares is **modal**
#     (`stb_base` is whatever base carries the most stores), so on a one-store body the modal base is the
#     guard's register and the refusal above is a base mismatch rather than a sequence mismatch. The
#     clause never runs on a rung-1 arm - it is inside `-ge 2` - so this is a property of the *control*
#     and not of the arm; a reader who feeds it another image should read the census line and not the
#     verdict alone.
#
# AND THE CLAUSE IS PROVEN ADDITIVE BY THE STRONGEST AVAILABLE MEASUREMENT: THE IMAGE DID NOT CHANGE.
# The arm was parked, then the clause was written, then the entry image was rebuilt with the same
# seventeen switches - and **`xnu_arm_entry.bin` came back `57d55fc9…`, `xnu_arm_entry.elf` `5008170d…`,
# `stage90.bin` `33f38925…`, `stage90-qcdt.img` `9a4a8695…`: byte for byte the arm above**, with the
# payload build reporting "nothing to install". The one member that moved is the source manifest's
# `build_entry.sh` line, which is exactly what a build-script check that emits no code must do. So the
# 696 set is one arm with two source manifests, and the manifest that stands is the one whose
# `build_entry.sh` line is the LIVE file's - the same rule that refused 688 once for naming a
# `build_entry.sh` the tree no longer had.
#
# THE FIVE SOURCE FILES THIS STEP TOUCHED, and all five are inside `xnu_arm_boot/` because that
# directory is content-hashed into this very set (673): `entry_storage.c` (the sequence and its eleven
# new keys), `entry_storage.h` (the rung paragraph), `build_entry.sh` (the rung-2 `case` and the
# census), `entry_trace.c` (the seam constant, moved from `0x800462dc` to `0x800472dc` by a build
# refusal that named both numbers) and `entry_stubs.c` (the `TEX[0]` comment 695 left owed). The
# decoder repair of 695 and the reference document are **outside** that directory and are therefore not
# in this manifest - which is why the manifest is 23 files and not the whole tree.
#
# WHAT THIS SET IS NOT. It is not a press: no readiness chain, no gate, no runner and no `fastboot boot`
# has been run against it, so its cells are the 696 document's pre-registration and nothing else. It is
# not a step towards storage being usable: four stores put the controller in SDHCI mode and register no
# block device, read no sector and mount nothing. And **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld** - the user's
# condition is that the OS can already be entered, the OS is not observed booting and staying, so the
# condition is unmet and no write to storage has been made. No firer is armed.
#
# ================================================================================================
# 697: THIS SET WAS PRESSED, AND THE BLOCK IS IN SDHCI MODE. Read this block before the 696 one
# above, whose pre-registration is what these sentences confirm or refute - and note that
# **nothing in the set changed**: the arm that was sent is the arm recorded above, byte for byte.
# ================================================================================================
#
# THE PRESS: 2026-09-25, readiness exit 0 (5/5, adb listing `4a2fe00b` as `device` and the neighbour
# `33e80afe` off both lists), `preflight_boot_check.sh --allow-xnu-entry` **exit 0 / 570 lines**,
# exactly one `run_and_capture.sh --allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-mode-57d55fc9`, and
# **RUNNER EXIT=0** - returned and captured, "the device came back 27s after this run called
# `fastboot boot` (seen via: adb)". Capture 615,362 B, sha256
# `ce54a0539371d44d933c976318af25711de29ea5b15f0f1d5809a1b324004008`, archived by hand under
# `out/stage90/captures/697-storage-mode-pressed-2026-09-25-{last_kmsg.txt,run.log,gate.log,press.log}`.
# Nothing was flashed, the send was one non-persistent `fastboot boot`, and the only device state that
# changed is the boot. **Nothing was written to the flash medium** - see the last paragraph.
#
# AND EVERY CELL THE 696 BLOCK PRE-REGISTERED, IN THE DIRECTION THAT COSTS A WRITE. All eleven keys are
# in the log, in the source's own order, with `_writes` published as a progress count (0 then 1/2/3/4)
# and `_mode_stage` beside it (1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 - 4 is the timeout path's):
#   * `_mode_calls=1`, `_mode_refused=0`, `_mode_stage=1` - the version guard passed
#     (`_mci_version=0x10000011`), so the arm was ALLOWED to write, and its first act was not taken.
#   * `_writes=1`, `_mode_w0_read=0x00000000` - store 1 landed and `FF_CLK_SW_RST_DIS` is now CLEAR.
#   * `_writes=2`, `_mode_power_wr=0x000004C1` - `CORE_POWER <- 0x441 | CORE_SW_RST`, exactly the form
#     the vendor's sequence writes and nothing else.
#   * `_mode_rst_polls=1`, `_mode_rst_steps=0`, `_mode_rst_ticks=0x6`, `_mode_rst_cleared=1`,
#     `_mode_timeout=0` - **the reset was already clear on the first read: 6 CNTVCT ticks = 0.3125 us**
#     at the device's own 19,200,000 Hz. The vendor's "~40us" is a worst case at minimum clock rates, so
#     this is a resolution of it and not a contradiction; `_mode_timeout=1` with `_writes=2` and
#     `_mode_stage=4` remains the failure path and is not this run's cell.
#   * `_mode_pwrctl_status=0x00000000` - **the hazard 696 section 3 named and could not mitigate did not
#     occur**: the reset latched no power-IRQ status, so the `CORE_PWRCTL_CLEAR 0xE4` acknowledge this arm
#     deliberately does not write was not owed on this hardware state (`_core_power=0x441`, i.e. the
#     previous state WAS BUS_ON, which is the case the vendor's own comment predicts it for).
#   * `_writes=3`, `_mode_w1_read=0x00000001` - `HC_MODE_EN` set.
#   * `_writes=4`, `_mode_w2_read=0x00002001`, **`_mode_bit_after=1`** - the final `CORE_HC_MODE` is the
#     vendor's own end state bit for bit, and `HC_MODE_EN` is SET where 694 measured it CLEAR. **531
#     section 6's "prerequisite" is now the state**: the block is in SDHCI mode on this boot.
#   * `_mode_stage=7`, `_mode_hci_version=0x00000000`, `_mode_capabilities=0x742dc8b2` - **the one cell
#     that came back as NEITHER branch**, and it found a wrong NAME in the arm's own source. 696
#     pre-registered "a differing pair = the mode change made visible; an identical pair = the mode bit
#     alone does not move these two words"; the reading is one word moved (`0x10 -> 0x00`) and the other
#     did not, and the vendor's own header says why: `drivers/mmc/host/sdhci.h:27` is
#     `SDHCI_DMA_ADDRESS 0x00`, `:181` `SDHCI_CAPABILITIES 0x40`, `:241` `SDHCI_HOST_VERSION 0xFE` (read
#     there by `sdhci-msm.c:2909`). So `ST_SDHCI_HCI_VERSION 0x00` and the two keys named after it read
#     the **SDMA system address register** - a soft register the core reset clears, which makes this
#     reading a SECOND positive measurement that the reset happened - while the strapping constant at
#     `0x40` correctly did not move. **The first spec-valid version read is still owed, at `0xFE`**, and
#     the rename waits for the next edit inside `xnu_arm_boot/` because that directory is content-hashed
#     into this arm's identity (673).
#   * `_mode_stage=8` - the whole sequence ran. **Not one cell of the pre-registered table is the failure
#     or the refusal cell, and the dangerous register was never touched** - not because this run checked
#     that it was not (a run cannot) but because `build_entry.sh`'s store census refuses to link a rung-2
#     image whose probe stores anywhere but `0x78/0x00/0x78/0x78` on one base, and this arm passed it.
#
# THE ENDING FIRED, THE PANIC PATH WAS TAKEN, AND 694's RECORD IS CORRECTED FROM ITS OWN PARKED CAPTURE.
# `xnu_live_post_end_calls=0x00000008` with `xnu_live_post_elapsed=0x0927730f` = **7,998.97 ms** against
# the armed 115,200,000 ticks - **1,999.0 ms** past the deadline, where 694 overshot by **11.011 ms**. The
# two runs together measure the arm's resolution as **one idle sleep (~2007 ms of park)**: a single run's
# overshoot only says where the deadline fell inside the sleep. Then the ending's first store faulted -
# `panic(cpu 0 caller 0x80455668): kernel abort type 4: fault_type=0x3, fault_addr=0xfa0065c`,
# `pc=0x8047c488` (`entry_seam_end_run+0xc`), `lr=0x8047c5bc`, `far=0x0fa0065c` = RESTART_REASON,
# `frame_ok=1`, `sleh_storm=9`/`sleh_seen=9` - followed by `Attempting system restart...MACH Reboot`.
# **And 694's own parked capture carries the same two lines at its 4012/4019**, with the same caller kind
# and the same `fault_addr`: 694's sentence "recovered rather than fatal (`frame_ok=1`, no
# `panic ... sleh_abort`)" was true of a *pattern* and false of the machine. The correct form is "the frame
# was recovered AND the kernel panicked on the abort". The return is now a fourth measurement -
# **8 s / 17 s / 24 s / 27 s** - and still unattributed, but narrowed: the log shows the panic path was
# reached, and XNU's reboot path cannot reset this device, so the reset is a hardware write (watchdog
# `0xf9017014` or `PS_HOLD 0xfc4ab000`). **This is the first arm in the sequence whose tables map
# `0xFC4`** - the megabyte `PS_HOLD` lives in - so `entry_epilogue`'s second store was reachable in
# principle, and the log cannot say whether it happened: the fatal abort is the *ending's* first store and
# neither of the epilogue's stores is published as a key.
#
# WHAT THIS PRESS DID NOT DO, AND THE ONE THING IT CHANGES. No storage: four stores to the controller's own
# `CORE_HC_MODE`/`CORE_POWER`, no command, no sector, no partition table, no mount, and the eMMC *device*
# is not addressed at all. No driver beyond the fixture, and the goal's floor is unchanged (pid 1, a driver
# answering the first `open`, the fixture's `0xfeedface` read into a user page). **TWRP-to-storage stays
# withheld** - the user's condition is that the OS can already be entered and stays, and it is not observed
# doing that. What it changes is the next step's shape: the controller is now in the mode a driver would
# use, so the next act is that driver's own reset path (`sdhci_reset(SDHCI_RESET_ALL)` and the version read
# at `0xFE`) and only then a clock/power state before any `CMD0`/`CMD8` - and a command sequence against a
# bus whose power state the OS has not set is the first act the *medium* would see.
#
# AND THE TWO TOOL-SIDE MOVES OF THIS STEP, NEITHER OF THEM A DEVICE ACTION. **Readiness row 3 refused this
# arm's tree before any press**, because `run_and_capture.sh`'s `EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL` is a second definition
# of the exit's `bl FlushPoU_Dcache` return address and still carried `0x800462dc` while this arm's ELF has
# `0x800472dc`: the gate compares the two and says which is owed the change, the disassembly decided it, and
# the press was unspent while it was wrong. And `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`'s row-4 narration gained the
# rung-2 branch, so a press log names the arm that WRITES rather than describing a read-only one.

set=armed-storage-census-4d66f871 sha256=52bc9c357068b791f777bb9abd2e879fa5b0b27364267220b01215dc5faec97b bytes=1744 file=stage90_fixture.macho role=fixture-macho-same-bytes-in-every-arm-but-a-manifest-member-all-the-same

# ================================================================================================
# 698: THE REGISTER-FILE CENSUS ARM - PARKED, NOT PRESSED. Nothing below is a reading of this arm on
# the device: the set above is the arm that will be sent, and the press is the next step's act. What
# is recorded here is what was MEASURED about the set before it was parked, including the one
# refusal it produced and the one mistake this step made and discarded.
# ================================================================================================
#
# WHAT THE ARM IS. It is the 696 arm plus a read-only census of the SDHCI standard register file at
# the tail of `entry_storage_probe`, under `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=3`: ten reads at the widths the
# vendor's own accessors use (`STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` is the rung ladder's digit and the ladder is
# 0 inert / 1 read-only probe / 2 + the vendor's mode sequence / 3 + the census). The ten are
# `PRESENT_STATE 0x24` + `CARD_PRESENT`, `HOST_CONTROL 0x28`, `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`, `CLOCK_CONTROL
# 0x2C`, `SOFTWARE_RESET 0x2F`, `SLOT_INT_STATUS 0xFC`, `CAPABILITIES_1 0x44`, `MAX_CURRENT 0x48`,
# and `core_mem`'s `CORE_PWRCTL_MASK 0xE0` / `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL 0xE8` - plus `HOST_VERSION 0xFE`, the
# read 697 section 3 left owed and the reason `ST_SDHCI_HCI_VERSION 0x00` is renamed
# `ST_SDHCI_DMA_ADDRESS`. **The rung adds no store**, which is what the store census below now says
# as a reading of this artifact rather than as a claim about the source, and the ten cells are
# pre-registered in `docs/experiments/experiment-698-the-standard-register-file-read-at-its-own-widths.md`
# section 4 - the before-values the driver's own `sdhci_reset(SDHCI_RESET_ALL)` will be read against.
#
# THE REFUSAL THIS RUNG PRODUCED, AND THE DEFECT IT FOUND IN 696's CLAUSE. The first build of this
# arm did not link: the store census refused it with `entry_storage_probe stores to [r6:4 ]`, which
# are `g_storage_probed` (0x805541a4) and - the flag this very rung added - `g_storage_mode_complete`
# (0x805541a8), two adjacent `.bss` flags GCC addressed through one base register so that the second
# arrives at offset 4. **A register holding the image's own address is not a device register**, and
# 696's clause had written "not `sp`" where it meant "is a device address": the quantity it bounded
# was "stores in this body" and the quantity it existed for was "stores TO THE BLOCK". The clause is
# repaired here rather than worked around, because the next rung would have hit the same wall: each
# store's base register is now CLASSIFIED by what the body materializes it with - a `movt` high half
# >= 0xf000 is a device megabyte (0xf90../0xf98../0xfc4..), below that is this image's own
# 0x8000_0000- - and a register the body materializes with BOTH (`AMB`, GCC reuses registers: `r3`
# carries 0x8054 and 0xf982 in this very body) or with NO `movt` at all (`UNK`, a literal pool or a
# computed address) REFUSES the build, which is the direction a build check has to fail in.
#
# ITS FIRST DRAFT WAS WRONG IN THE UNSAFE DIRECTION, which is why the shapes are written down: it
# classified each store in line order, and this body materializes `r3` with `movt r3,#0xf982` at
# 0x8000d52c - AFTER a store at 0x8000d478 - so a device base whose `movt` sits after its store
# would have been read as IMG, i.e. WAIVED. The classification is collected over the whole body and
# applied at the end for that reason, and the clause now prints its own reading on success instead
# of passing silently. **This build's line reads:**
#
#   xnu_entry_698: the probe's stores, classified by base register - device [120 0 120 120 ] on r5,
#                  image [r6:0 r6:4 ], ambiguous [], unknown []
#
# and it is proven in both directions: the LIVE rung-3 image and the PRESSED 696 park both accept
# with exactly the four device stores (their images differ only in which `.bss` flags the body has),
# and SEVEN mutations of the rung-3 disassembly each refuse through the branch it was built to reach
# - offset 120 -> 124 (the offsets string), a fifth device store (the count), a store moved onto the
# IMG base `r6` (the count falling to three), a device store moved to `r3` (AMB), to `r9` (UNK), and
# a fifth store added on a second device base `r4` (the second-base branch).
#
# AND A SECOND CLAUSE OF THE SAME SHAPE IS BESIDE IT, new for this rung: the ALIGNMENT CENSUS. For
# every memory instruction in `entry_storage_probe` whose base is not `sp`, the offset must be a
# plain immediate satisfying its own access width's alignment (`ldr`/`str`/`ldrd`/`strd` -> 8,
# `ldrh`/`strh` -> 2, `ldrb`/`strb` -> 1), and a register-offset form refuses the build with the
# instruction quoted. The reason is not style: `0x29`, `0x28` and `0x2F` are byte registers and
# `0xFE` is 16-bit at a non-4-aligned offset, so a 32-bit access to any of them is an UNALIGNED
# ACCESS TO DEVICE MEMORY, which faults on ARMv7 - the same class of fault 692 measured on this
# block, reached by alignment instead of by translation. The clause makes the widths in the
# document's section 1 a property of the linked image instead of a comment. It passes on this image
# (run standalone against this very ELF before the build, and inside the build).
#
# WHAT MOVED IN THE TREE: three of the manifest's twenty-three files - `build_entry.sh` (the rung-3
# `case`, the store census's classification repair, the new alignment census), `entry_storage.c`
# (the ten reads, `st_read8`/`st_read16`, the rename, `st_standard_census`) and `entry_storage.h`
# (its rung paragraph). **`entry_trace.c` did NOT move this time**, and that is a measurement and not
# an assumption: this image's seam clause reports `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR (0x800472dc` - the value 697
# had to move in `run_and_capture.sh` - unchanged, so `run_and_capture.sh`'s `EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL`
# still matches this arm and readiness row 3 has nothing to refuse.
#
# THE DELTAS AGAINST THE PRESSED 696 ARM, all measured, not inferred:
#   * `stage90.bin` is 6,031,876 bytes in BOTH arms and 596,939 bytes differ - and **every one of
#     those bytes is inside the embedded entry blob window [494236, 6030616)**: the new entry bin
#     appears verbatim exactly once at 494236, and the payload's own code is byte-identical to the
#     pressed arm's outside that window.
#   * `stage90.elf`'s `.text` is 6,030,666 bytes in both, and the three entry bss-size immediates at
#     VA 4ac40/4ad04/4ad18 are byte-identical, because the entry image's own bss size did not change
#     (379,720 both - the new flag landed inside existing padding).
#   * the entry image itself: the elf's `.text` grew by 480 bytes (5,313,768 vs 5,313,288), the bin
#     is 5,536,380 in both because that size is padded, and the 596,939 differing bin bytes are that
#     insertion shifting everything after it around file offset 0x480000, plus every absolute address
#     that points past it (the difference is a few hundred bytes per 64 KiB block before 0x480000 and
#     54k-63k per block from 0x480000 to 0x520000).
#   * `stage90-qcdt.img` differs in 596,959 bytes: the payload's 596,939 plus the header's own
#     sha1 field at 576..595, which is the 20 bytes the gate checks.
#   * `stage90-build-config.txt` and `stage90_fixture.macho` are byte-identical to the 696 arm's.
#
# THE MISTAKE THIS STEP MADE, recorded because it is the one thing here that could have cost a
# press. The payload was first rebuilt with a plain `./build.sh`, which writes `#define
# STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 0u` - a payload that NEVER JUMPS INTO XNU - producing a 683-byte config, a
# 6,031,820-byte bin and a 6,030,610-byte `.text`. It was caught by diffing the config against the
# 696 park before anything was sent, and the payload was rebuilt with
# `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1' ./build.sh`, which reproduces the 682-byte config and
# the 6,030,666-byte `.text` above. The arm above is the SECOND payload build. Nothing was sent while
# the wrong one was in `out/`, and the gate would have refused it anyway
# (`preflight_boot_check.sh:1715`: `--allow-xnu-entry was passed, but this image was built with
# STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY off`) - but the refusal would have cost a readiness chain and a re-read of this
# record, which is exactly the cost the discipline exists to avoid.
#
# WHAT THIS SET IS NOT. It is not a press: no readiness chain, no gate and no `fastboot boot` has
# been run against it. It is not a step towards storage being usable: ten READS of the register file
# register no block device, read no sector and mount nothing - and the four stores it inherits from
# 696 put the controller in SDHCI mode and nothing more. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld**: the
# user's condition is that the OS can already be entered and stays, and the OS is not observed doing
# that. No firer is armed.
#
# OWED, before this arm is anything: the readiness chain (`tools/verify_press_ready.sh`, then exactly
# one `preflight_boot_check.sh <printed flags>` and exactly one `run_and_capture.sh <printed flags>
# --expect-arm=armed-storage-census-4d66f871`), and then the press itself, which is the next step's
# act.
#
# ================================================================================================
#
# ================================================================================================
# 698: PRESSED 2026-09-25 19:35:36-19:37:04 UTC. Readiness 5/5 (exit 0), ONE gate (exit 0, 571 stdout
# lines) and EXACTLY ONE runner, both under `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-census-
# 4d66f871`; RUNNER EXIT=0 - returned and captured, the device back 24 s after `fastboot boot`;
# capture 613,952 B sha256 70f5fc924aec35cf75ea645e5ff57d00e223fbb0da1ff9994e8e77607500680f. One
# non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial 4a2fe00b, neighbour 33e80afe off both lists. The firer was
# `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh` (sha 7f23cae5...) run as `press-on-clear.v5.sh
# armed-storage-census-4d66f871 600`, the set declared with no default; it printed `done. gate=0
# runner=0` and exited, so NO FIRER IS ARMED and 660's closure released.
# ================================================================================================
#
# THE CELL-BY-CELL ANSWER IS THE DOCUMENT `docs/experiments/experiment-699-the-census-arm-pressed-the-
# register-file-answers-the-bus-power-bit-is-clear.md`; what belongs here is the arm's own identity and
# the two facts that changed the record's owed list.
#
# EVERY PRE-REGISTERED CELL IS FILLED, and three of them decide the next step:
#   * `_reg_power_control=0x00` - bit 0 (`BUS_POWER`) is CLEAR, so the standard block does not believe
#     the bus is powered: the `SDHCI_RESET_ALL` the driver would run is harmless BY CONSTRUCTION on
#     this state, which is the direction 698 section 2 registered as the safe one.
#   * `_reg_clock_control=0x0003` - the internal clock runs and is stable, `SD clock enable` (bit 2) is
#     clear: `sdhci_set_clock` is ahead of the driver on this block.
#   * `_reg_software_reset=0x00` with `_reg_present_state=0x01f80000` (both inhibit bits clear) and
#     `_reg_pwrctl_mask=0x0000000f` - the last one is what makes 697's `_mode_pwrctl_status=0` mean
#     "nothing latched" rather than "nothing was listening".
#   * and `_reg_loads` reaches `0x0000000a` with `_regs_done=0x0000000a`, the record's "ten reads"
#     derived a second time.
#
# THE READ 697 SECTION 3 LEFT OWED IS TAKEN: `_mode_host_version=0x00001102` - vendor version `0x11`,
# spec version `2` (`SDHCI_VENDOR_VER_MASK 0xFF00` / `SDHCI_SPEC_VER_MASK 0x00FF`). It is neither 0 nor
# 0xffff, so the register answers in SDHCI mode, and the value lands on a branch the vendor's own driver
# tests: `sdhci-msm.c:2909-2914` compares the vendor nibble with `SDHCI_VER_100` (`:54`, `0x2B`), so the
# `SDHCI_QUIRK2_SLOW_INT_CLEAR` branch is NOT this hardware's - which is why the reading is decision
# relevant and not a curiosity.
#
# THE MODE SEQUENCE REPRODUCES FROM THE BOOTLOADER STATE: `_hc_mode=0x2000`, `_mode_bit=0`,
# `_core_power=0x441`, `_mci_version=0x10000011`, `_writes=4` ending at `_mode_w2_read=0x2001` and
# `_mode_bit_after=1`, `_mode_rst_ticks=6` - every number 694 and 697 took, again. **The controller this
# payload inherits is the bootloader's state and not the previous run's**, which is what makes the four
# stores a precondition on every boot.
#
# THE ENDING AND THE RETURN, unchanged in shape: `_post_t0=0x052b38d3`, `_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800`
# (19,200,000 Hz), `_post_elapsed=0x06e259fe` = 115,497,470 ticks = 6,015.49 ms, `_post_end_calls=7`
# (the deadline crossed 15.49 ms before the returning pass), `_slot_post_calls=4`; then the same panic
# at `pc=0x8047c488` (`entry_seam_end_run+0xc`, the store to `RESTART_REASON 0x0fa0065c`),
# `lr=0x8047c5bc`, `fsr=0x00000805`, `frame_ok=1`, `sleh_storm=9`. **Return time 24 s** - a fifth
# measurement beside 8 (690), 17 (692), 24 (694) and 27 (697), and 697/698 sharing 24 s with different
# ending overshoots is the one negative fact this run adds: the interval is not a function of the
# ending's own clock.
#
# AND THE GOAL IS NOT MET. No storage: ten reads and the four inherited stores address no sector, mount
# nothing, and register no block device. No driver beyond the fixture: the goal's floor (pid 1, the
# driver's `open` answering 0 with the control open answering 2, the fixture's `0xfeedface` read into a
# user page) is met exactly as 520 and 533 met it. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld** - the user's
# condition is that the OS can already be entered and STAYS, and the OS is not observed doing that.
#
# ================================================================================================

#
# ================================================================================================
# 701: THE DRIVER'S-OWN-RESET ARM - PARKED, NOT PRESSED. Nothing below is a reading of this arm on
# the device: the set above is the arm that will be sent, and the press is the next step's act. What
# is recorded here is what was MEASURED about the set before it was parked - including the defect the
# first build of it found in a build clause's own reader, and the mutation suite that says which of
# the new census clauses fire.
# ================================================================================================
#
# WHAT THE ARM IS. It is the 698 arm plus `sdhci_reset(SDHCI_RESET_ALL)`, read out of the vendor's own
# source before it was written (`docs/experiments/experiment-701-...md`), under
# `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=4`. The ladder is 0 inert / 1 the read-only probe / 2 + the vendor's mode
# sequence / 3 + the standard register file's census / 4 + the driver's own reset. Rung 4 is
# **one byte store and a bounded poll**, and everything else in `sdhci_reset` (`sdhci.c:229-279`) is
# guarded off by state a fresh host does not have: `platform_reset_enter/_exit` are members
# `sdhci_msm_ops` does not define, `host->pwr` is 0 so the `check_power_status(REQ_BUS_OFF)` at
# `:254-256` is not reached, and that is the one act in the path that could hang this device -
# `sdhci_msm_check_power_status` (`sdhci-msm.c:2179-2209`) writes no register and ends in
# `wait_for_completion(&msm_host->pwr_irq_completion)`, a wait nothing in this image would ever end.
# **The rung therefore does not emulate it**. The store is `0x01` to `SOFTWARE_RESET 0x2F` in
# `hc_mem`, the poll is `sdhci_readb(0x2F) & mask` with the driver's own 100 ms bound (1,920,000 ticks
# at this machine's 19,200,000 Hz, which 699's `_post_cntfrq` measured), and the after-values are the
# five registers the pre-registration named.
#
# **THIS IS THE FIRST STORE THIS PROJECT HAS EVER MADE THROUGH `hc_mem`.** Every device store before
# it - the four of the vendor's mode sequence - was on `core_mem`. That is why this step's build
# clause had to learn a second device base (701 section 5), and why it is the clause, and not a
# comment, that keeps the byte four offsets from this one unwritten.
#
# THE STORE CENSUS IS NOW TWO NAMED WINDOWS, AND THE BUILD SAYS SO AS A READING:
#
#   xnu_entry_698: the probe's stores, classified by base register and window - core_mem
#   [120 0 120 120 ] on r5, hc_mem [47 ](strb ) on r5, image [r6:0:str:16804 r6:4:str:16804 ],
#   ambiguous [], unknown [], unnamed-device []
#
# `core_mem` unchanged since 696 (the vendor's own order and offsets) and `hc_mem` exactly once, at
# 47 = 0x2F, as a **byte** store. **`POWER_CONTROL 0x29` is four offsets away and is what the clause
# is for**: writing 0 there IS a bus-off request on this SoC (531 section 8), so "the rung that
# writes the reset bit cannot write the bus-off bit" is a property of the linked image - the same
# shape `mi4-a-claim-in-a-comment-is-not-a-check` asks for. The two windows SHARE their `movt`
# (`0xf9824000` and `0xf9824900`), which is why the clause names the window from the *pair* of
# immediates and why a store it cannot place refuses rather than guesses.
#
# THE DEFECT THE FIRST BUILD FOUND, AND IT WAS IN THE READER. The rung-4 build failed with
# `no branch to __wrap_Idle_load_context is inside cpu_idle_exit (0x8000e504..0x8000e38c)` - an
# INVERTED range, which is impossible from `nm -n`. The cause was in `build_entry.sh`'s `sym_next`
# (and, identically, in `next_global`): the reader compared the address with `$1 == s`, and `gawk`
# compares two *numeric strings* numerically - `8000e504` parses as `8000e504` = `8e507` = **+inf**,
# so EVERY address whose digits after the `e` are all decimal digits equals every other. Measured:
# `nm -n` resolves `8000e504` to **416 lines**, and `sym_next` answered `0x8000e38c`, the symbol
# *before* `cpu_idle_exit`. The reader had been right for every arm until this one because
# `8000e2b8` and `8000e58c` do not parse (a `b` and a `c` are not digits) and because no earlier
# window sat in the `e<digits>` shape - i.e. **rung 4 did not cause the defect, it moved a symbol
# into the shape that exposes it**, and the clause that consumed the wrong range reported a false
# failure about the image. The repair is `strtonum` on both sides, unit-tested in both directions
# (old -> `0x8000e38c`, repaired -> `0x8000e58c` = `cpu_init`; and unchanged for `0x8000e19c`,
# `0x8000e3e8`), and the record of it is `docs/experiments/experiment-702-...md`. **A build failure
# is not automatically a fact about the image.**
#
# THE MUTATION SUITE, in the shape 698 section 5 used, and it is the reason to believe the clauses
# fire at all (the source was restored byte-for-byte after every mutation and the restore hashed):
#   * **M1** - a second `hc_mem` store at `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`: REFUSED, as pre-registered, with the
#     offsets read as `[47 41 ]`. This is the clause that makes the bus-off byte unreachable.
#   * **M3** - the reset compiled in on rung 3 (a store through `hc_mem` on a read-only rung):
#     REFUSED, as pre-registered ("every rung below 4 is defined as reading that window and writing
#     nothing in it").
#   * **M2, M2b, M4** - the store made 32 bits wide (`st_write8`'s own body, and a direct word store),
#     and the address aimed at a device megabyte this controller does not declare (`0xf9825000`):
#     all three REFUSED, but **by the AMB clause and not by the one each was written for** - a
#     mutated store changes GCC's register allocation, so the base register carries more than one
#     device high half and the outermost clause refuses first. **The width clause (`strb`) therefore
#     has NOT been observed to fire**, and the honest state of it is that its subject - a wider store
#     to 0x2F that still materializes the same base pair - is not a body this source produces. It is
#     recorded as owed rather than claimed as exercised.
#   * **M5** - the classifier fed its own input shape directly (a test of the reader, not of a build):
#     `CORE r11:120:str:16384`, `HC r12:47:strb:18688`, `DEVBAD r14:47:strb:0`, `UNK r8:4:str:none`,
#     `DEVLO r9:47:strb:none`, `IMG r10:0:str:none` - six records, six classes, and **the pre-701
#     classifier calls r11, r12 and r14 the same thing** (`DEV`), which is exactly why 701 added the
#     low half. `DEVBAD` is reached only here, and the difference is named.
#   * and the same awk run against the live rung-4 body reproduces the build's own census line,
#     window for window - a second derivation of the same fact.
#
# ONE HAZARD THIS STEP MEASURED AND DID NOT HAVE TO FIX. A build that FAILS leaves the mutated image
# in `out/` while `xnu_arm_entry-config.txt` still describes the last image that passed, so `out/` is
# momentarily a tree whose bin and record disagree. It is not pressable: the arm-change guard has no
# matching set, and the gate refused it with the right reading - the payload embeds the *clean*
# image's bytes while `out/` holds a different build of the same length ("what it carries at that
# length is a DIFFERENT build of the entry image rather than an absent blob"). The clean rebuild
# reproduces `00b28262...` exactly, so the build is deterministic and the residue was transient.
#
# WHAT THIS ARM DOES NOT DO. **It sets no clock** (701 section 2: `sdhci_msm_ops` replaces
# `.set_clock`, `sdhci_msm_set_clock` writes `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C` and calls `clk_set_rate` on the
# GCC at `0xfc400000` - the megabyte 692 pressed and measured as NOT MAPPED - so the clock is a third
# block and its own step). It writes no `POWER_CONTROL`, no `CORE_PWRCTL` and no `CLOCK_CONTROL`. It
# issues no command, reads no sector, parses no partition table and mounts nothing - **one byte
# written to one register of a controller whose register file has now been read ten times**, and the
# five after-reads. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld**: the user's condition is that the OS can
# already be entered and STAYS, and the OS is not observed doing that.
#
# OWED, before this arm is anything: the readiness chain (`tools/verify_press_ready.sh`, then exactly
# one `preflight_boot_check.sh <printed flags>` and exactly one `run_and_capture.sh <printed flags>
# --expect-arm=armed-storage-reset-00b28262`), and then the press itself, which is the next step's
# act. **No firer is armed.**
#
# ================================================================================================
#
# ================================================================================================
# 701: PRESSED 2026-09-25 20:37:13-20:39:25 UTC. Readiness 5/5 (exit 0), ONE gate (exit 0, 572
# stdout lines) and EXACTLY ONE runner, both under `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-
# reset-00b28262`; RUNNER EXIT=0 - returned and captured, the device back 27 s after `fastboot boot`;
# capture 614,436 B sha256 b966ecac914fd66d516cecace8348dc7ef0de4a46b24c2c14923d17f958bbf76, archived
# by hand under `out/stage90/captures/701-reset-pressed-2026-09-25-{last_kmsg.txt,run.log,gate.log,
# press.log}` (out/ is gitignored). One non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial 4a2fe00b, neighbour
# 33e80afe off both lists (adb listed 4a2fe00b as `device` and `fastboot devices` was empty). The
# firer was `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh` (sha 7f23cae5...) run as `press-on-clear.v5.sh
# armed-storage-reset-00b28262 600`, the set declared with no default; it printed `done. gate=0
# runner=0` and exited, so NO FIRER IS ARMED and 660's closure released.
# ================================================================================================
#
# THE CELL-BY-CELL ANSWER IS THE DOCUMENT `docs/experiments/experiment-703-the-drivers-own-reset-pressed-one-byte-in-one-register-out.md`;
# what belongs here is the arm's own identity and the readings that change the next step.
#
# **EVERY PRE-REGISTERED CELL IS FILLED, AND THE RUNG DID EXACTLY WHAT THE PRE-REGISTRATION SAID IT
# WOULD - ONE STORE, AND IT CLEARED:**
#   * `_rst_calls=0x1`, `_rst_before=0x00`, `_rst_refused=0x0` - the refusal path was not taken, and
#     699's `_reg_software_reset=0x00` is what said it would not be.
#   * `_rst_wrote=0x1` and `_rst_stores=0x00000001` - **the store landed, and the count is the
#     record's "one store" derived a second time**, bottom-up from the artifact rather than repeated
#     from this sentence.
#   * `_rst_stage` published 1, then 2, then 3 - the three stages in order, ending at done.
#   * `_rst_polls=0x1` with `_rst_steps=0x0`, `_rst_ticks=0x00000010` (16 ticks = 0.83 us at this
#     machine's 19,200,000 Hz), `_rst_cleared=0x1`, `_rst_timeout=0x0`, `_rst_done=0x1` - **the reset
#     bit was already clear on the FIRST read**, which is the standard's self-clearing contract and
#     the same number 697 measured on the vendor's `CORE_SW_RST` (`_mode_rst_polls=1`) in the other
#     block. 701 section 1 cited that as evidence; this is the second derivation.
#   * `_reg_software_reset_after=0x00` - the byte the poll tested, published so the poll's terminal
#     state is a reading and not an inference.
#   * **`_reg_power_control_after=0x00` - THE CELL 698 SECTION 2 OWED.** The standard reset did NOT
#     turn the bus on: bit 0 is still CLEAR, so the block's belief about the bus is exactly what 699
#     found it, and the driver would find nothing to reconcile with the vendor's own `CORE_PWRCTL`.
#   * **`_reg_pwrctl_status_after=0x00` - THE HAZARD'S CELL, and it is empty.** 696 section 3
#     pre-registered that the reset may latch a power-IRQ status when the previous state was BUS_ON,
#     and `_core_power` says it was - but nothing latched, and `_reg_pwrctl_mask=0x0000000f` (four
#     bits routed) is what turns that from "nothing happened" into "nothing latched while the mask
#     was armed".
#   * `_reg_host_control_after=0x00` and `_reg_present_state_after=0x01f80000` - **both identical to
#     their before-values**, so "this reset moved exactly one register" is a measurement and not a
#     reading of the source.
#
# **AND THE WHOLE LADDER REPRODUCED ON A FRESH BOOT**: `_mode_bit=0x0` then `_mode_bit_after=0x1`,
# `_writes` published 0/1/2/3/4, `_mode_w0_read=0`, `_mode_w1_read=1`, `_mode_w2_read=0x00002001`,
# `_mode_rst_polls=1` with `_mode_rst_ticks=0x6`, `_mode_refused=0`, `_mode_pwrctl_status=0`, and the
# census's ten reads with `_reg_loads` reaching `0x0000000a` and `_regs_done=0x0000000a` - every
# number 694, 697, 698 and 699 took, again, on an arm whose code changed under them.
#
# THE ENDING, THE PANIC AND THE SIXTH RETURN TIME. `_post_t0=0x075abe53`, `_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800`
# (19,200,000 Hz), `_post_elapsed=0x06e0d551` = 115,397,969 ticks = 6,010.31 ms, so the deadline was
# crossed **197,969 ticks = 10.31 ms before the returning pass** - inside one ~2007 ms idle park, as
# 694's 11.011 ms, 697's 1,999.0 ms and 698's 15.49 ms were. `_post_end_calls=7` (the ending fired on
# the 7th return) with `_slot_post_calls=4`. The panic is the same one: `fault_addr=0xfa0065c`, the
# ending's own first store to `RESTART_REASON`, and the runner's seam block is the same block 697 and
# 698 printed (`PASS seam_lr=0x800472dc`, the `FAIL seam_sp` and the `CLEAN LINE` pair `b=0x800b3648/
# 0x8047db04`, all three the `STAGE90_XNU_SLOT_NULL=1` switch). **Return time 27 s** - the sixth
# measurement (8, 17, 24, 27, 24, 27) and still unattributed.
#
# AND THE GOAL IS NOT MET. No storage: one byte written to one register of a controller whose register
# file has now been read ten times, and nothing else - no command, no sector, no partition table, no
# mount, and the eMMC *device* is not addressed. No driver beyond the fixture: the floor (pid 1, the
# driver's `open` answering 0 with the control open answering 2, the fixture's 0xfeedface read into a
# user page) is met exactly as 520, 533, 699 and every arm before it met it. **TWRP-to-storage stays
# withheld** - the user's condition is that the OS can already be entered and STAYS, and the OS is not
# observed doing that.
#
# THE STEP THE RESET CLEARS THE WAY FOR, NOW NAMED BY A READING RATHER THAN BY A PLAN: **the clock**,
# because `CLOCK_CONTROL` reads `0x0003` (internal clock enabled and stable, SD clock enable clear)
# both before and after this reset, so `sdhci_set_clock` is still ahead of the driver; and on this SoC
# that is `sdhci_msm_set_clock`'s `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C` plus `clk_set_rate` on the GCC's SDCC
# clocks - i.e. **map the `0xfc400000` megabyte first**, which is where 692's fault was and the block
# whose one gate the probe reads as an interlock.
#
# ================================================================================================

#
# ================================================================================================
# 704: THE CLOCK-SURFACE ARM - `armed-storage-clock-6e7fbafa`. PARKED AND PRESSED 2026-09-25.
# Readiness 5/5 (exit 0, 21:27:23 UTC), ONE gate (exit 0, 573 stdout lines, 21:27:50) and EXACTLY
# ONE runner, the last two under `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-clock-6e7fbafa`;
# RUNNER EXIT=0 (21:28:59) - returned and captured, "the device came back 25s after this run called
# `fastboot boot`" (seen via: host log, serial). One non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial
# 4a2fe00b, neighbour 33e80afe off both lists (the firer's own verdict check read `fastboot: []`,
# `adb: [4a2fe00b device]`). The firer was `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh` (sha 7f23cae5...) run as
# `press-on-clear.v5.sh armed-storage-clock-6e7fbafa 600`, the set declared with no default; it
# printed `done. gate=0 runner=0` and exited, so NO FIRER IS ARMED and 660's closure released.
#
# **THE PROSE BELOW WAS WRITTEN ONCE, AFTER THE PRESS.** The park's own evidence, taken at park time,
# is the eleven `set=` lines above and readiness's `VERIFIED, 11 files, 6 manifest-member checks` -
# this block is the arm's identity and the press's readings, not a park-time note pretending to be
# one.
#
# THE ARM, AND WHY IT IS A RUNG OF READS. It is 701's arm (rungs 1-4) unchanged, under
# `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=5`, plus a **read-only census of the clock surface** at the tail of
# `entry_storage_probe`, guarded by the same `g_storage_mode_complete` interlock every rung above 2
# uses. Twelve words, in this order: the GCC's `SDCC1_BCR 0x04C0`, `SDCC1_APPS_CBCR 0x04C4`,
# `SDCC1_AHB_CBCR 0x04C8`, `SDCC1_CDCCAL_SLEEP_CBCR 0x04E4`, `SDCC1_CDCCAL_FF_CBCR 0x04E8`,
# `SDCC1_APPS_CMD_RCGR 0x04D0` and `CFG_RCGR`/`M`/`N`/`D` at `+4/+8/+0xC/+0x10`, then `core_mem`'s
# `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C` - **the FIFTH offset of a window this project's census has bounded to
# four since 698** - and finally `CLOCK_CONTROL 0x2C` READ LAST, as a halfword, so that "this census
# moved nothing" is a measurement. The shipped disassembly carries exactly those twelve loads
# (eleven `ldr` and one `ldrh`, at 8000d8a4/8000d8c8/8000d90c/8000d950/8000d994/8000d9d8/
# 8000da1c/8000da64/8000da74/8000da84/8000daa0/8000dadc), which is `_clk_loads=0xc` derived from the
# artifact and not repeated from this sentence.
#
# **THE RUNG WROTE NOTHING, AND THAT IS PUBLISHED RATHER THAN ARGUED**: `_clk_writes=0x00000000` on
# the path taken. The whole press's write set is unchanged from rungs 2 and 4 - `core_mem` four
# times (120/0/120/120 on r5, the vendor's own order and offsets) and `hc_mem` exactly once
# (`strb r6,[r5,#47]`, `SOFTWARE_RESET 0x2F`) - and its own build line says so:
#   xnu_entry_698: the probe's stores, classified by base register and window - core_mem
#   [120 0 120 120 ] on r5, hc_mem [47 ](strb ) on r5, gcc [], image [r6:0:str:16804
#   r6:4:str:16804 ], ambiguous [], unknown [], unnamed-device []
#
# WHAT THE RUNG IS FOR, AND IT IS A CORRECTION TO A READING AND NOT TO A VERDICT. Since 692 the
# probe has published `xnu_live_storage_gate` as `_cbcr & BIT(0)`, and bit 0 of a CBCR is
# `CBCR_BRANCH_ENABLE_BIT` - THE BIT THE DRIVER WRITES, i.e. the REQUEST (`clock-local2.c:62`,
# written at `:380-382`) - while what "the clock is running" means is `CBCR_BRANCH_OFF_BIT` = BIT(31)
# (`:63`), which the framework polls to a bound before it believes the clock is on (`:386-387` ->
# `branch_clk_halt_check`, `:333-360`, `HALT_CHECK_MAX_LOOPS 500`) and which its own handoff test
# reads ALONE (`branch_clk_handoff`, `:490-497`). **The gate was reading HALF OF ITSELF.** 694's
# `_cbcr=0x00004ff1` satisfies both halves, so nothing measured before this press is overturned; what
# is new is that the census now publishes all three bits per branch. This press's readings:
#   * ALL FOUR BRANCHES READ `en=1`, `off=0`, `hw=0` - requested AND running, and not under hardware
#     gating (so the framework does NOT skip its own halt check). The state the pre-registration
#     named as the one the old reading could not have told apart from "on" (`en=1`, `off=1`) is NOT
#     this boot's state - the correction is to the reading, not to 694's verdict.
#   * `_clk_ahb_cbcr=0x2000cff1`, and **`SDCC1_AHB_CBCR 0x04C8` had never been read by any run in
#     this project.** It answers: the `pclk` branch `sdhci_msm_prepare_clocks` enables
#     (`sdhci-msm.c:2315`) is already requested and already running, so the controller answering a
#     version word is not a register file awake while its bus clock is not. The word also carries
#     BIT(29), which is published and not named.
#   * **AND THE DISAGREEMENT IS BETWEEN THE BRANCHES AND THE ROOT.** `_clk_rcg_cmd=0`, so
#     `_clk_rcg_root_en=0` AND `_clk_rcg_root_status=0`, beside an apps branch that reads
#     requested-and-running one register away. Two readings of one quantity, and they do not agree -
#     which is how this project's most-repeated defect class arrives inside the clock tree. It is
#     also why the driver's own order (rate, then enable, then POLL) is the order, and why the write
#     rung can be BOUNDED: `_clk_rcg_update=0` and `_clk_rcg_cmd=0` are `rcg_update_config`'s poll's
#     before-values, against its own `UPDATE_CHECK_MAX_LOOPS 500` (`clock-local2.c:44`, `:90-108`).
#   * `_clk_bcr=0x0` with **`_clk_bcr_ares=0x0`** - `BCR_BLK_ARES_BIT` (`:66`) CLEAR, so the block is
#     not held in reset, which is what gives 694's `_bcr` a meaning and makes every branch reading
#     above a statement about a clock.
#   * the apps root's current configuration: `_clk_rcg_src=0x5`, `_clk_rcg_div=0x7`,
#     `_clk_rcg_mnd_mode=0`, `_clk_rcg_m=0x0`, `_clk_rcg_n=0x0`, `_clk_rcg_d=0xff` - a D-only
#     divider, and `mnd_mode=0` beside `n=0` is the cross-check the pre-registration registered (the
#     dual-edge value would need `N != 0`). NO RATE IS PUBLISHED: the parent's rate table
#     (`gpll0`/`gpll4`/`cxo`, `clock-8974.c:1564-1574`) is not in this image, and a frequency
#     computed here beside the framework's own cached rate would be the same defect one level up.
#   * `_clk_vendor_spec=0x0` with `_clk_vendor_pwrsave=0` and `_clk_vendor_mclk_sel=0` - the two
#     fields `sdhci_msm_set_clock` reads before it decides, and the MCLK select is what the write rung
#     will read-modify-write.
#   * both CD-calibration branches read `0x00000001` (`en=1`, `off=0`, `hw=0`). Their parents are
#     `cxo_clk_src` and not the apps root (`clock-8974.c:2362-2373`), so they were the two that could
#     have read differently - and they do not.
#   * `_clk_clock_control_after=0x0003` - bit 0 and bit 1 set, **bit 2 `SD clock enable` STILL
#     CLEAR**, so no clock has been set by this image or any before it and `sdhci_set_clock` is still
#     entirely ahead of the driver.
#
# WHAT THE STEP'S FIRST BUILD FOUND WAS TWO DEFECTS IN A READER, and both were repaired before the
# arm was frozen:
#   * the store census counted EVERY materialization of a high register half, so a probe that uses
#     one register as a `core_mem` base, then as an `hc_mem` load base, then as a GCC base was
#     AMBIGUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION - the first mutation written for this rung was refused by the `AMB`
#     clause instead of by the new GCC clause, i.e. refused for a reason that had nothing to do with
#     what it tested. Repaired by classifying IN PROGRAM ORDER (the last device high half
#     materialized before the store decides the window; a preceding IMAGE pair classifies IMG first).
#     **A build clause that cannot say which address a store uses is a reading about the clause's own
#     scope, not about the image.**
#   * the repair itself misclassified image stores as GCC (measured on a synthetic body: an `str` to
#     0x805541a4 classified `GCC`), because the device fallback paired a device high half with an
#     image low half when both were true. Fixed, then CONTROLLED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS on eleven
#     synthetic bodies against the reader taken from `git show HEAD:` - after a first attempt whose
#     "old" column was extracted from the already-edited file and so measured nothing. **A control
#     whose baseline comes from the thing under test is not a control.**
#   * the three mutations, all refused after the repair: a store to 0xfc400000 -> the new GCC clause
#     ("...and no rung of this ladder writes the clock controller at any value"); an unaligned 32-bit
#     read -> the width census; `stb`->`sth` -> the per-window mnemonic clause.
#
# THE LADDER REPRODUCED, THE ENDING FIRED, AND THE RETURN IS A SEVENTH MEASUREMENT. The whole 1-4
# ladder on a fresh boot: `_gcc_map=1`, `_map=1`, `_gate_read=1`, `_bcr=0`, `_cbcr=0x00004ff1`,
# `_gate=1`, `_loads=6`, `_writes=0` at the gate; `_mode_bit=0` -> `_mode_bit_after=1` with
# `_mci_version=0x10000011`; `_reg_loads` counting 1..10; and `_rst_stores=1`, `_rst_polls=1`,
# `_rst_ticks=0xf` (15 ticks against 703's 16). `_post_t0=0x07404b0a`, `_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800`
# (19,200,000 Hz), `_post_elapsed=0x06df694a` = 115,304,778 ticks = 6,005.46 ms, so the deadline was
# crossed **104,778 ticks = 5.457 ms before the returning pass** - inside one ~2007 ms idle park, as
# 694's 11.011, 697's 1,999.0, 698's 15.49 and 703's 10.31 ms were. `_post_end_calls=7` with
# `_slot_post_calls=4`; the panic is the same one (`fault_addr=0xfa0065c`, the ending's own first
# store to `RESTART_REASON`), and the runner's seam block is the block 697, 698 and 703 printed.
# **Return time 25 s** - the seventh measurement (8, 17, 24, 27, 24, 27, 25) and still unattributed.
#
# THE LAYOUT IS STABLE FOR A REASON WORTH NAMING. `.text` grew 1248 bytes, yet `entry_storage_probe`
# (8000cff8), the wrapper's `bl` (8047db2c), the seam (0x800472dc), `platform_cache_idle_enter/exit`
# and `early_random` are all where they were, and only `cpu_idle_wfi` moved (672 bytes): the growth
# was absorbed by alignment fill before `platform_cache_idle_enter`. **Every address the gate and the
# runner pin is stable because a fill contracted, not because nothing moved**
# (`mi4-linker-fill-term`).
#
# AND THE GOAL IS NOT MET. No clock, no `POWER_CONTROL` write, no `CLOCK_CONTROL` write, no command,
# no sector, no partition table, no mount; the eMMC device is not addressed. No driver beyond the
# fixture: the floor (pid 1, the driver's `open` answering 0 with the control open answering 2, the
# fixture's 0xfeedface read into a user page) is met exactly as 520, 533, 699 and 703 met it.
# **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld** - the user's condition is that the OS can already be entered and
# STAYS, and the OS is not observed doing that.
#
# THE STEP THIS RUNG CLEARS THE WAY FOR: **the write rung**, and this press is what lets it be
# bounded rather than experimental. `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C`'s MCLK select is a read-modify-write of
# a field the vendor also writes (`_clk_vendor_mclk_sel=0` is its before-value);
# `clk_prepare_enable` on the AHB branch is a CBCR read-modify-write PLUS a halt handshake on a
# branch with `has_sibling = 1` (whose `round_rate`/`list_rate` are `-EPERM` by the framework's own
# rule); and `clk_set_rate` on the apps RCG is `M`/`N`/`D`, then `CFG_RCGR`, then `CMD_RCGR`'s update
# bit AND A BOUNDED POLL of it - whose before-values `_clk_rcg_update=0` and `_clk_rcg_cmd=0` are now
# measured.
#
# OWED AND CARRIED: the root-versus-branch disagreement above is owed a DECISION and not just a
# reading (is the driver's order what this SoC needs, or does a clear apps-root `root_en` mean a
# clock the branch reports as running is not reaching the block?); `CLOCK_CONTROL` bit 2 has never
# been set by this image; BIT(29) of `_clk_ahb_cbcr` is published and unnamed; **the gate's narration
# of `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` is now FOUR RUNGS SHORT (2, 3, 4 and 5 are not described in it) -
# peer lane, by message and never by edit**; a pre-registration's key names are not a check (three
# whole-CBCR keys are spelled `_clk_cbcr_ahb`/`_clk_cbcr_cdccal_sleep`/`_clk_cbcr_cdccal_ff` in 704
# section 3 and `_clk_ahb_cbcr`/`_clk_cdccal_sleep_cbcr`/`_clk_cdccal_ff_cbcr` in the image, and the
# narration sweep checks the narration and not the doc); the width clause of the store census still
# has not been observed to fire on a WIDENED DEVICE store; what actually returns a run; the ending's
# first store still faulting into a panic; the 691 section 5 `entry_note_wfi` readback;
# `entry_reset.h`'s false IMEM claim; 676 section 6 / 677 section 6; the 684-owed runner clause for
# the 678 arm; `tools/xnu_dt_requirements.py` and the `"master"` value; the two peer-lane tripwire
# repairs; and the seam address pinned in two files.
#
# THE CELL-BY-CELL ANSWER IS THE DOCUMENT

# 707: THIS SET WAS PRESSED, AND THE CLOCK SET RAN. Read this block before the 706 narration above,
# whose expected values it fills (`_clk_set_writes` "six" is the one number this press corrects).
# ================================================================================================
# 707: PRESSED 2026-09-25 22:06:10-22:07:42 UTC. Readiness 5/5 (exit 0, and it was run three times:
# the first run REFUSED with a false miss - section 6 of 707), ONE gate (exit 0, 574 stdout lines)
# and EXACTLY ONE runner, both under `--allow-xnu-entry --expect-arm=armed-storage-clkset-b4b696d9`;
# RUNNER EXIT=0 - returned and captured, the device back **28 s** after `fastboot boot`; capture
# 618,572 B sha256 498fa36dc11c650f376de75fb126c9cd713ad029773fa19c21dd0e5538ff0aa2, archived by
# hand under `out/stage90/captures/rung6-clkset-20260925-220856-{last_kmsg.txt,gate.log,run.log,
# press.log}` (out/ is gitignored, and the runner does not archive the capture). One
# non-persistent `fastboot boot`, serial 4a2fe00b, neighbour 33e80afe off both lists (fastboot: [],
# adb: [4a2fe00b device]). The firer was `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh` (sha 7f23cae5...) run as
# `press-on-clear.v5.sh armed-storage-clkset-b4b696d9 600`, the set declared with no default; it
# printed `done. gate=0 runner=0` and exited, so NO FIRER IS ARMED and 660's closure released.
#
# THE CELL-BY-CELL ANSWER IS THE DOCUMENT
# `docs/experiments/experiment-707-the-clock-set-pressed-eight-stores-no-rate-and-the-readback-that-did-not-hold.md`;
# the pre-registration it fills is
# `docs/experiments/experiment-706-the-rung-6-pre-registration-the-first-clock-set-and-the-rate-write-that-is-not-on-this-path.md`.
#
# WHAT THE PRESS ANSWERED, IN THE SHORT FORM (every number below is a key in the log):
#  * THE FIRST CLOCK SET RAN: `_clk_set_calls=1`, `_clk_set_done=1`, `_clk_set_writes=0x8`,
#    `_clk_set_gcc_writes=0x4`. **THE COUNT IS EIGHT AND NOT SIX** - 706 section 1 and section 3
#    counted the vendor's two paths (four CBCR + two CORE_VENDOR_SPEC) while the key counts every
#    store the sequence makes, and the two `CLOCK_CONTROL 0x2C` halfwords are stores of the clock
#    set too. Both definitions were inside one document: `mi4-one-value-two-definitions`, and the
#    artifact's own counter is the right one about the artifact.
#  * **THE RATE WRITE IS NOT ON THIS PATH, MEASURED**: `_clk_set_rate_writes=0x00000000`. 706
#    section 2's finding is now a reading, and the driver's bookkeeping (400 kHz) against 705's
#    register file (192 MHz) is a 480x disagreement nothing reconciles until the first speed change.
#    The RCG's before-values (`_clk_rcg_update=0`, `_clk_rcg_cmd=0`) are still before-values.
#  * THE FOUR BRANCH ENABLES WROTE BACK THE WORD THEY READ: `ahb 0x2000cff1`, `apps 0x00004ff1`
#    (the gate's own word), `ff` and `sleep 0x00000001`, every pair `before == after`, every halt
#    check passed on its FIRST read (`_polls=1`, `_ticks` 10/11/10/10 - about 0.5 us each at
#    `cntfrq` 19200000). The AHB branch is the one whose "on" is `BRANCH_NOC_FSM_ON_VAL` (`0x2` in
#    bits 31:28 of `0x2000cff1`), which is the measurement 706 section 2 predicted from 705.
#  * THE STANDARD'S TWO HALFWORDS, AND THE RUNG'S ACTING CELL: `0x0003` -> `_cc_int=0x0000e041`
#    (divisor 960 packed, `INT_EN` set, `CARD_EN` clear - the pre-registered word) -> `_stable=1`
#    after 18 reads / 187 ticks (about 9.7 us) inside the driver's 20 ms bound -> **`_cc_card=
#    0x0000e045`** and `_cc_after=0x0000e045`. **THE SD CLOCK ENABLE BIT IS SET FOR THE FIRST TIME
#    BY ANY IMAGE IN THIS PROJECT**, with the card unpowered and no command in flight, and the word
#    read back.
#  * THE DT IS TOLD APART IN THE LOG: `_clk_set_max_clk=0x16e36000` = 384000000, so the board's
#    table is the `msm8974pro.dtsi:1768` one, `_div=0x1e0`, `_real_div=0x3c0`, `_divisor_alt=0`.
#  * **THE ONE CELL THAT CAME BACK THE OTHER WAY**: `_clk_set_vendor_w1=0x00000200` (MCLK select
#    `2 << 8`, exactly `sdhci-msm.c:2497-2499`), `_clk_set_vendor_w2=0x00000000`, and
#    **`_clk_set_vendor_after=0x00000000`** - 706 section 3 wrote this alternative down before the
#    build ("0 here would mean the field did not take"), and it did not take. `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC
#    0x10C` answers READS (that byte has been read since 698) and did not change under a store the
#    artifact shows writing 0x200. Two stores spent, the register unchanged, and that is a new
#    question about the block rather than about the arm - **owed**.
#  * THE LADDER REPRODUCED: `_gate_read=1`, `_loads=6`, `_clk_loads=0x0000000c`, `_mode_bit=0` ->
#    `_mode_bit_after=1` (the vendor's mode sequence is still a PREREQUISITE and not a re-do),
#    `_rst_cleared=1`, and every rung-1..5 reading unchanged.
#  * THE ENDING FIRED AND DID NOT END THE RUN, in the sense 678/686/690 need the qualifier for:
#    `_post_end_calls=0x00000007`, `_post_elapsed=0x06e0953c` = 115,318,588 ticks = 6006.2 ms -
#    crossed 6.2 ms after the armed 6000.0 ms deadline, inside one idle window - 16 idle windows,
#    the seam pair CLEAN LINE, and the same panic (`fault_addr=0xfa0065c`, the ending's own first
#    store to `RESTART_REASON`, `pc=0x8047c488` = `entry_seam_end_run+0xc`). **The deadline was
#    REACHED; the store faulted; the boot went on.** What returns a run (8, 17, 24, 27, 24, 27, 25,
#    **28** s) is still unattributed.
#
# THE LAYOUT IS STABLE FOR THE REASON 704 NAMED, AND THIS ARM IS THE HARDER CASE OF IT. `.text`
# grew 1440 bytes and TWO symbols moved - `cpu_idle_wfi` (+1568) and `arm_init` (+1560) - while
# `entry_storage_probe` (8000cff8), the wrapper's `bl` (8047db2c), the seam (0x800472dc),
# `platform_cache_entry/exit` and the wrappers did NOT move at all: the gap before
# `platform_cache_idle_enter` contracted by exactly the growth. **Every address the gate and the
# runner pin is stable because a fill contracted, not because the image did not move**
# (`mi4-linker-fill-term`). The first build of this arm is also what TAUGHT it: `st_branch_enable`
# came out as an outlined symbol (GCC does not inline a four-call-site helper), which pushed
# `entry_storage_probe` to 0x8000d09c and left the GCC window EMPTY under a census that reads the
# probe's own body - fixed with `always_inline`, and the same build found the base-register
# grouping defect (a proxy deciding WHICH stores a window has, with ties broken by awk's hash
# order), a GCC tuple compared against the wrong fields, and `sth` where objdump prints `strh`.
#
# AND THE GOAL IS NOT MET. The clock is enabled at the controller and NO RATE was written; no
# `BCR`/block reset; no `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` (it is absent from the `hc_mem` store set `47 44 44`
# and stays unreachable in the linked image, with `sdhci_set_power`'s zero write and
# `sdhci_msm_check_power_status`' unbounded `wait_for_completion` named as the NEXT rung rather than
# attempted); no command, no sector, no partition table, no mount; the eMMC device is not addressed,
# and no driver beyond the fixture. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld: the OS is not observed
# entering and STAYING.** `CLOCK_CONTROL` bit 2 is no longer an owed absence - this press set it -
# and what the unpowered card does with an enabled SD clock is entirely unmeasured.
#
# STILL OWED, AND CARRIED UNCHANGED OTHERWISE: `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C`'s MCLK select did not take;
# the RCG write (the speed-change rung, whose deadline is a bounded poll of the update bit); the
# root-versus-branch disagreement (a DECISION, not a reading); the width clause of the store census
# still never observed to fire on a WIDENED DEVICE store; what actually returns a run; the ending's
# first store still faulting; the 691 section 5 `entry_note_wfi` readback; `entry_reset.h`'s false
# IMEM claim; 676 section 6 / 677 section 6; the 684-owed runner clause for the 678 arm;
# `tools/xnu_dt_requirements.py` and the "master" value; the two peer-lane tripwire repairs; BIT(29)
# of `_clk_ahb_cbcr` published and unnamed; and the seam address pinned in two files.
#
# **THE GATE'S NARRATION OF `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` IS NOW SIX RUNGS SHORT (2, 3, 4, 5 AND 6
# ARE NOT DESCRIBED IN IT) - peer lane, by message and never by edit.**
# ================================================================================================
# 708: THE CARD-POWER ARM - `armed-storage-power-7b1f3799`. PARKED, NOT PRESSED. Nothing below is a
# reading of this arm on hardware: every number here is the build's own, measured before any press.
# The rung is `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=7` - `mmc_power_up`'s PASS A - and its pre-registration is
# `docs/experiments/experiment-708-the-rung-7-pre-registration-the-card-power-byte-and-the-wait-that-is-not-taken.md`,
# whose section 2 cell table this block's expectations are the same values as (one value, one
# definition: the keys named here are the keys that document names).
# ================================================================================================
# WHAT THE ARM DOES, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND WHAT IT DECLINES: one 8-bit store to `POWER_CONTROL 0x29`
# with the byte derived from `CAPABILITIES 0x40` the way `sdhci_add_host`/`mmc_power_up` derive it -
# and NOT the wait that follows it in the vendor's code, which this image cannot honour.
#
# * THE STORE IS IN THE LINKED IMAGE: `strb r4, [r6, #0x29]` at **`0x8000e26c`**, with `r6` the
#   `hc_mem` base `0xf9824900`, so the address is `0xF9824929`. **It is the first store to
#   `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` by any image in this project**, and the census clause moved with it: the
#   `hc_mem` store set is `[47 44 44 41]` (`0x2f` strb, `0x2c` strh, `0x2c` strh, `0x29` strb) where
#   rung 6 asserted `[47 44 44]`; the two `CLOCK_CONTROL` halfwords sit at `8000dfb4` and `8000e070`
#   and the reset byte at `8000d72c`, all three UNCHANGED in address and width. The `dsb sy` after the
#   store is `st_write8`'s own, which is what makes "the readback was taken after the store" a
#   property of the code rather than of the compiler's ordering.
# * THE BYTE IS DERIVED AND EVERY STEP IS PUBLISHED: `_pwr_cap` is `CAPABILITIES 0x40` read at
#   `0x8000e0fc` (`ldr r5,[r3,#0x940]` - the `hc_mem` window addressed through the `core_mem` base,
#   because `hc_mem = core_mem + 0x900`); then `_pwr_avail`, `_pwr_vdd`, `_pwr_vdd_ocr`,
#   `_pwr_voltage_bits`, `_pwr_refused`. On this device the capability word carries `CAN_VDD_180`
#   (`BIT(26)`) and NOT `CAN_VDD_330/300` (`BIT(24)`/`BIT(25)`), so `avail = MMC_VDD_165_195`,
#   `fls(avail) - 1 = 7`, `pwr = SDHCI_POWER_180` and **`_pwr_wrote` = `0x0B`** (`0x0A | POWER_ON`).
#   `_pwr_cap` is a RE-READING and not a constant: 705 published `0x742dc8b2`, and a different word
#   here is a reading about this boot rather than a contradiction.
# * THE FIVE READINGS BESIDE IT: `_pwr_before`/`_pwr_after` (`POWER_CONTROL`, 8-bit, expected
#   `0x00 -> 0x0B` - and `_pwr_after` is the readback that says the store took, the cell 706's
#   `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC` store failed); `_pwr_status_before`/`_pwr_status_after` (`CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS
#   0xDC`, 32-bit); `_pwr_mask` (`CORE_PWRCTL_MASK 0xE0`, expected `0x0000000f` - four events armed,
#   so a status bit is evidence and not a masked-off formality); `_pwr_ctl` (`CORE_PWRCTL_CTL 0xE8`,
#   expected `0`); `_pwr_ps_before`/`_pwr_ps_after` (`PRESENT_STATE 0x24`, reached as `core+0x924`
#   because the compiler folds the `+0x900` into the offset - an address that LOOKS like a core
#   register and is not, `0x924` being `hc_mem + 0x24`); and `_pwr_cc_before`/`_pwr_cc_after`
#   (`CLOCK_CONTROL 0x2C`, 16-bit).
# * **`_pwr_cc_before` IS THE CELL THAT STATES THE ROUTE, AND ITS EXPECTED VALUE IS `0xE045`** -
#   rung 6's card-enable readback (`_cc_after`), read by this rung's pass A in the SAME boot because
#   the call site is guarded by `g_storage_mode_complete`. That is the difference from the driver's
#   own order, where pass A sees `0x0000` and the clock is set afterwards in pass B. A `0xE045` here
#   is the previous arm's clock set still standing; a `0x0000` is the driver's own route; neither is
#   a failure, and both are readings this ladder had not had.
# * THE WAIT IS NOT TAKEN, AND THAT IS PUBLISHED RATHER THAN HIDDEN. `sdhci_msm_check_power_status`
#   takes `done` only from the threaded `sdhci_msm_pwr_irq`, which this image cannot deliver (708
#   section 1.3, measured silent: `_reg_pwrctl_mask=0x0f`, `_reg_pwrctl_status_after=0`,
#   `_mode_pwrctl_status=0`, `_reg_pwrctl_ctl=0`), so a faithful port of the driver's power path would
#   `wait_for_completion` forever. What the arm publishes instead is `_pwr_status_after` - the reading
#   that handler would have taken - plus `_pwr_done`, and the quirk correction is why the *other* write
#   is absent: `SDHCI_QUIRK_SINGLE_POWER_WRITE` is set for this host (`sdhci-msm.c:2897`), so
#   `sdhci.c:1352`'s zero/bus-off write is not on this path at all.
#
# THE LAYOUT, WHICH IS THIS ARM'S NEW FACT AND THE REASON THE SEAM IS RE-PINNED. Rung 7's ~1.4 KB
# pushed the entry group across a page boundary, so the seam went `0x800472dc` -> **`0x800482dc`** and
# EVERY kernel-text address moved with it: of the image's 26,533 symbols **17,524 moved**, in eight
# deltas - `+0x1000` x15979, `0` x9009, `+0x1248` x839, `+0x2e0` x335, `+0x1250` x280, `+0x2d4` x79,
# `+0x1244` x6, `+0x1260` x4 and `-0x1260` x2. `entry_storage_probe` is at `8000cff8` UNCHANGED (every
# arm since 698), `arm_init` moved `8000e35c -> 8000e630` and `cpu_idle_wfi` `8001869c -> 8001897c`
# (the +0x2d4/+0x2e0 pair, the entry-side tail that did not cross the page); the wrapper's `bl` to the
# probe is `8047eb2c` (was `8047db2c`), its tail `b entry_post_clock` is `8047eb4c` (was `8047db4c`),
# and `platform_cache_idle_exit`'s `bl` is at `800482d8` returning to `800482dc`. **The two files that
# pin the seam - `xnu_arm_boot/entry_trace.c`'s `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` and `run_and_capture.sh`'s
# `EXIT_POP_LR_LITERAL` - were re-pinned TOGETHER, and the build is what refused to link until they
# were**: the linker's error named both numbers, `objdump --disassemble=platform_cache_idle_exit` showed
# the `bl` at `0x800482d8` returning to `0x800482dc`, and the direction was decided by that disassembly
# and not by the arithmetic. So the owed item "the seam address is pinned in two files" has this rung's
# answer: the second copy is now checked by the build (`mi4-linker-fill-term`, and 696's aligned-fill
# class - a *fill* moved every address at once, and no amount of arithmetic would have predicted
# which).
#
# TWO THINGS THIS STEP MEASURED ABOUT ITSELF, both mistakes a tool could have caught and a reader had to:
#  * **the first payload build of this arm was made WITHOUT `-DSTAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1'`**,
#    i.e. it never jumps into XNU (`xnu_kernel.c:234`'s call site is behind `#if STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY`).
#    Nothing in the build refused: that payload's own record said `#define STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 0u`, its
#    embedded blob was the right entry image, and every hash it takes part in matched. It was caught by
#    comparing `stage90-build-config.txt` against this record - and the net that WOULD have caught it
#    is the gate's own clause (`preflight_boot_check.sh:1699-1719` refuses `--allow-xnu-entry` against
#    an image built with the switch off), which is readiness row 3. A mis-build costs a rebuild and not
#    a press; this line is here because the build itself says nothing.
#  * **TWO NUMBERS IN THIS RECORD WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED HERE.** `experiment-707` line 140 and
#    the rung-6 block above say the entry `.text` went `5315592 -> 5317032` and that `.data` is
#    `206804`. Measured with three tools that agree (`arm-none-eabi-size -A`, `objdump -h`,
#    `readelf -S`) against the four parks: `.text` is `5309568` (rung 4) -> `5310816` -> `5312256` ->
#    `5316960`, and `.data` is `206512` in all four (`.bss` `379720`). The DELTAS those blocks quote
#    are right (+1248, +1440) and both absolute bases are wrong by `+3336`; `206804` is
#    experiment-501's `.data` for an image whose `.text` was `5280296` and whose `.bss` was `362824`.
#    A correct delta applied to a stale base survives every check either block takes - which is why
#    the wrong absolute is the one thing in this pair nobody could have caught by arithmetic.
#
# WHAT THIS ARM IS NOT. No command, no sector, no partition table, no mount, no card power-up
# *sequence* (the wait that orders the sequence is the one not taken, and the power byte's own effect
# on the card is unobserved because nothing on this bus has ever been read back from a card); no
# driver beyond the fixture; and the ladder's own cells about the card are all made by the controller
# rather than by a device. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld**: the user's condition is that the OS can
# already be entered and STAYS, and the OS is not observed doing that. One byte to a power register on
# a bus no device has answered on is not an OS entering.
#
# OWED AND CARRIED UNCHANGED OTHERWISE: the power path's wait (the vendor's sequence wants a threaded
# interrupt handler this image cannot deliver, so rung 7's route is one store and not the driver's
# order); `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C`'s MCLK select did not take under a store (706/707); the RCG write
# (the speed-change rung, a bounded poll of the update bit); the root-versus-branch disagreement (a
# DECISION and not a reading); the width clause of the store census still never observed to fire on a
# WIDENED DEVICE store; what actually returns a run (8, 17, 24, 27, 24, 27, 25, 28 s); the ending's
# first store still faulting into a panic; the 691 section 5 `entry_note_wfi` readback;
# `entry_reset.h`'s false IMEM claim; 676 section 6 / 677 section 6; the 684-owed runner clause for
# the 678 arm; `tools/xnu_dt_requirements.py` and the "master" value; the two peer-lane tripwire
# repairs; BIT(29) of `_clk_ahb_cbcr` published and unnamed; and **the gate's narration of
# `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` is now SEVEN RUNGS SHORT (2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 are not described in it,
# and its key's own prose says the ladder ends at 3) - peer lane, by message and never by edit.**
# `tools/verify_press_ready.sh`'s own ladder had the same shape as of rung 6 and this rung adds rung
# 7's paragraph there, in this lane, because that file's arm row refuses a rung it has no paragraph
# for rather than answering with another arm's sentence.
#
# MEASURED BEFORE THE PRESS, from the parked bytes: the payload's own layout did NOT move (`.text`
# 6030666, `.data` 1208, `.bss` 644048, all 465 symbols at the same addresses, and
# `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` at VA `0x80a9c` size `0x547a7c` in both arms); the entry image's `.text`
# grew `5312256 -> 5316960` (+4704) with `.data` `206512` and `.bss` `379720` byte-for-byte unchanged;
# `stage90_fixture.macho` and `stage90-build-config.txt` are BYTE-IDENTICAL to the rung-6 park; the
# qcdt and the boot image differ from the rung-6 park in 4,698,448 bytes of which 4,698,428 are inside
# the embedded blob (VA `0x80a9c`, file offset `496284`) and 20 are the header's sha1 field at 576-595,
# with ZERO differing bytes outside those two regions; and the entry image's sources manifest differs
# from the rung-6 arm's in exactly one line, `entry_storage.c`'s content hash, because the comment
# correction above was rebuilt and **the rebuild produced byte-identical binaries** (`xnu_arm_entry.bin`,
# `.elf` and `-config.txt` all `cmp`-identical to the park) - a comment-only edit proved by rebuilding
# and comparing rather than by reasoning.

# step and green both times), ONE gate (exit 0, 575 stdout lines at 22:50:34) and EXACTLY ONE runner
