# c5bdeac77428ebbd9207366b36b8b57efb858383b6517fc12889a73c1e5349c6, archived by hand under
# `out/stage90/captures/rung7-pwr-20260925-224929-{last_kmsg.txt,gate.log,run.log,press.log}` (out/ is
# gitignored, and the runner does not archive the capture: 672's rule; it parks the PREVIOUS log at
# /tmp/cancro-last_kmsg.txt.prev.N and printed that one through the same verdict list, which is how the
# seam criterion was seen to reject the previous arm's log and accept this one). One non-persistent
# `fastboot boot`, serial 4a2fe00b, neighbour 33e80afe off both lists (fastboot: [], adb:
# [4a2fe00b device]). The firer was `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh` (sha
# 7f23cae5fb4edf21159d6241606f203c4320eed59641d207fb9a641ec6c485e6) run as
# `press-on-clear.v5.sh armed-storage-power-7b1f3799 600`, the set declared with no default; it ran its
# own readiness first (`readiness exit=0`, and `the arm survived the wait and is still
# 'armed-storage-power-7b1f3799'`), and it printed `done. gate=0 runner=0` and exited, so NO FIRER IS
# ARMED and 660's closure released.
#
# WHAT THE PRESS ANSWERED, IN THE SHORT FORM (every number below is a key in the log):
#  * THE ACT RAN AND COMPLETED: `_pwr_calls=1`, `_pwr_done=1`, `_pwr_refused=0`.
#  * THE BYTE WAS DERIVED AS ITS INPUTS SAID: `_pwr_cap=0x742dc8b2` (exactly 705's
#    `_mode_capabilities`, a RE-READING and not a constant), `_pwr_avail=0x80`, `_pwr_vdd=7`,
#    `_pwr_vdd_ocr=7`, `_pwr_voltage_bits=0x0a`, **`_pwr_wrote=0x0b`** - the pre-registered byte,
#    `SDHCI_POWER_180 | SDHCI_POWER_ON`.
#  * **`_pwr_before=0x00` -> `_pwr_after=0x0b`: THE STORE TOOK AND THE READBACK HELD.** This is the
#    FIRST DEVICE WRITE IN THIS PROJECT WHOSE OWN READBACK CARRIES THE VALUE WRITTEN. The cell it is
#    measured against is 706's - rung 6 wrote 0x200 to CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C and read back 0 - and
#    that failure is why this cell was pre-registered as the one to read.
#  * **THE CONTROLLER ANSWERED, AND WITH A REQUEST**: `_pwr_status_before=0` ->
#    `_pwr_status_after=0x00000002` = BIT(1) = `CORE_PWRCTL_BUS_ON`, with `_pwr_mask=0x0000000f`
#    (all four power events armed) and `_pwr_ctl=0x00000000`. Nothing cleared it: the two reads are
#    the same register either side of the one store, so this is the store's own consequence.
#  * THE ROUTE CELL READ AS PRE-REGISTERED: **`_pwr_cc_before=0x0000e045`** - rung 6's card-enable
#    word still standing, because this probe runs AFTER the clock set in the same boot, where the
#    driver's own pass A would see 0x0000 - and `_pwr_cc_after=0x0000e045` unchanged, so powering did
#    not disturb the clock register. NO CARD: `_pwr_ps_before=_pwr_ps_after=0x01f80000`, whose
#    CARD_PRESENT bit is clear.
#  * **AND THE PRESS ANSWERED A QUESTION THE PRE-REGISTRATION DID NOT ASK: THE POWER IRQ ARRIVED.**
#    The log carries four keys rung 6's does not - `xnu_live_irq_other_count=0x1`,
#    **`xnu_live_irq_other_iar=0xaa`**, `xnu_live_irq_other_cli=0x8055412c`, and
#    `xnu_live_irq_timer_count_final=0x5` - and they are published by ONE path, the arm the handler
#    takes for a line this image handed out to nobody (entry_irq.c:546-557): EOI it, count it, record
#    the intid, and stop the run through `entry_epilogue("exception: irq line")`. **`0xaa` = 170 =
#    SPI 138 + 32, and SPI 138 is this controller's `pwr_irq`**: `msm8974.dtsi:502`'s node
#    `sdhc_1: sdhci@f9824900` - the base this arm writes through - declares
#    `interrupts = <0 123 0>, <0 138 0>` with `interrupt-names = "hc_irq", "pwr_irq"`. So the three
#    readings are one event in the order they happened: the byte was written, the controller asked for
#    the bus, and the request arrived as an interrupt on the line the vendor's
#    `sdhci_msm_check_power_status` waits on. **The previous arm is the CONTROL**: rung 6 ran sixteen
#    idle windows and past a 6,000 ms deadline and took no unexpected interrupt at all (its log has no
#    `_irq_other_*` key), and the two arms differ in the rung-7 block and in nothing but a uniform
#    +0x1000 address move. **This is 708 section 1.3 measured from the other end: not only is the
#    wait's completion undeliverable, the request the handler would answer arrived and ended the run.**
#  * THE ENDING'S CELLS ARE UNREAD ON THIS BOOT, and the reason is the IRQ: `_post_end_calls` is
#    ABSENT, `_post_elapsed=0x00000000` (0.0 ms at the hardware's own `_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800` =
#    19200000 Hz), `_slot_post_calls=1`, `_seam_calls=1`, and **no panic at all**
#    (`xnu_entry_panic_entered=0`, `panic_len=0x24`, against rung 6's `1` / `0x13fa` and its
#    `fault_addr=0xfa0065c`), with `_sleh_storm` absent. The trace published its zeroth sample and no
#    more: **the machine stopped inside the first idle pass, before the armed deadline**
#    (`_seam_post_end_ticks=0x06ddd000` = 115,200,000 ticks = 6000 ms). The 690 ending is a clock that
#    never ran out and its first store's fault was not reached again.
#  * THE SEAM IS LIVE, AND ITS CRITERION RE-EXAMINED THE PREVIOUS ARM: `xnu_live_seam_lr=0x800482dc`
#    is this arm's own log line, and the runner's list - whose `lr criterion` is derived at run time
#    from the arm in out/ - printed **PASS for this log and FAIL for the previous press's log**
#    (`seam_lr=0x800472dc is not the exit's own call's return address`). So the re-pinned constant and
#    the image agree, measured on two logs rather than argued from one. The rest of the runner's list
#    is the shape 697+ print: PASS no panic, PASS `slot_cwe_win` C=0, PASS `slot_post_calls=1`, PASS
#    `seam_sctlr` C=0, FAIL `seam_sp=0x80553ec8 is not sleh_sp-8` (the `SLOT_NULL=1` fingerprint), the
#    paired UNREADs, PASS `b1=0x8047eb04`, CLEAN LINE, and the floor (pid 1, the driver's `open`
#    answering 0 while the control open answers ENOENT, the fixture's 0xfeedface read into a user page,
#    two AST records). Eight abort records (`_slot_ab_calls` 1..8) and no storm.
#
# AND THE GOAL IS NOT MET. `POWER_CONTROL` carries a real 1.8 V byte and reports it, the controller has
# asked for the bus, and the clock is enabled at the controller - and there is still no command, no
# sector, no partition table, no mount, and no driver beyond the fixture; the card has never been
# addressed, and what a card does with a powered bus is unobserved because nothing on this bus has ever
# been read back from a card. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld: the OS is not observed entering and
# STAYING** - and this press makes that condition more clearly unmet rather than less, because the boot
# now stops on an interrupt the storage controller itself raises.
#
# THE NEXT RUNG'S SUBJECT IS THE POWER IRQ, AND IT IS A DECISION: either mask the power events before
# the store (`CORE_PWRCTL_MASK <- 0`, which is a write to the controller and therefore its own arm) or
# hand intid 170 to a client that clears the latch (`CORE_PWRCTL_CLEAR 0xE4`, sdhci-msm.c:64) and
# completes what `check_power_status` would have completed - the second is what the driver does and what
# the ladder owes the storage driver.
#
# STILL OWED, AND CARRIED UNCHANGED OTHERWISE: the status latch (`_pwr_status_after=0x02`) has no clear
# in this image; the store's effect on the CARD is unobserved; `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C`'s MCLK select
# did not take under a store; the RCG write (the speed-change rung); the root-versus-branch disagreement
# (a DECISION); the width clause of the store census still never observed to fire on a WIDENED DEVICE
# store; what actually returns a run; the ending's first store faulting (rung 6's measurement, UNREAD
# here); the 691 section 5 `entry_note_wfi` readback; `entry_reset.h`'s false IMEM claim; 676 section 6
# / 677 section 6; the 684-owed runner clause for the 678 arm; `tools/xnu_dt_requirements.py` and the
# "master" value; the two peer-lane tripwire repairs; BIT(29) of `_clk_ahb_cbcr` published and unnamed;
# the seam address pinned in two files (this arm moved it, re-pinned BOTH, and the build now refuses a
# link where they disagree with the image); and **the gate's narration of `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` is
# now SEVEN RUNGS SHORT (2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 are not described in it) - peer lane, by message and never
# by edit.**
#
# ================================================================================================
# 710 (2026-09-25): THE RUNG-8 PRE-REGISTRATION - owning intid 170, and the `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC`
# address this ladder has been reading in the wrong window
# ================================================================================================
#
# The pre-registration this block records is
# `docs/experiments/experiment-710-the-rung-8-pre-registration-owning-intid-170-and-the-vendor-spec-address-the-ladder-has-been-reading-in-the-wrong-window.md`,
# and it is a PRE-REGISTRATION: no arm was built at rung 8, `out/` still holds
# `armed-storage-power-7b1f3799` (708's rung-7 arm, PRESSED by 709, unchanged), and no firer is armed.
#
# THE DECISION 709 OWED IS TAKEN, AND IT IS THE SECOND OF THE TWO OPTIONS: rung 8 owns intid 170
# (SPI 138, `pwr_irq`) rather than masking the power events. The reason is structural and not taste -
# `entry_irq_register_client` and `entry_irq_enable_line` already exist and were measured at 496/498/500
# on intid 40, and **rung 8 is their first caller in the ladder** (`grep` over the tree: zero call
# sites today) - and masking (`CORE_PWRCTL_MASK <- 0`) would leave the driver's own handshake
# permanently incomplete, because `sdhci_msm_check_power_status`' completion is written by the handler
# rung 8 installs (`sdhci-msm.c:2092-2096`).
#  * THE HANDLER IS THE VENDOR'S OWN, WITH THE THREE ARMS THAT SLEEP ABSENT, and the vendor's own
#    `devm_request_threaded_irq(..., NULL, sdhci_msm_pwr_irq, IRQF_ONESHOT, ...)` (`:2937-2939`) is the
#    evidence: a NULL primary over ONESHOT makes it a THREADED handler, because
#    `sdhci_msm_setup_vreg`, `sdhci_msm_setup_pins` and `sdhci_msm_set_vdd_io_vol` may sleep - and this
#    image's client runs in `fleh_irq_kernel`'s frame. What is left is the register handshake, which is
#    the reading act: `readb(CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS 0xDC)`, `writeb(status, CORE_PWRCTL_CLEAR 0xE4)`, `mb()`,
#    the decode, `writeb(ack, CORE_PWRCTL_CTL 0xE8)`.
#  * THE ACK VALUE IS THE VENDOR'S OWN ON A PATH WITH NO REGULATORS: the probe's pre-acknowledge
#    (`:2872-2889`) reads `STATUS`, writes it to `CLEAR`, ORs `BUS_SUCCESS` into `CTL` with no vreg call
#    and no card present, and its comment says why (`CORE_SW_RST` may trigger a power IRQ if the previous
#    status was BUS_ON). The rung's handler is that preamble moved to the moment the line is raised.
#  * ONE CONSEQUENCE OF THE DECODE, PRE-REGISTERED BECAUSE IT CHANGES THE CENSUS: for
#    `irq_status = BUS_ON` the vendor sets BOTH `pwr_state = REQ_BUS_ON` and `io_level = REQ_IO_HIGH`
#    (`:2028-2029`), so act 6 runs - **`host->ioaddr + CORE_VENDOR_SPEC`'s read-modify-write in `hc_mem`**
#    (`:2079-2085`) - and the handler is a THREE-store handler, not the two-store one a reader would
#    predict. `hc_mem` therefore becomes `47 44 44 41 268` (`strb strh strh strb str`).
#
# AND THE SAME READING FOUND A DEFECT IN THIS LADDER: `#define CORE_VENDOR_SPEC 0x10C` (sdhci-msm.c:92)
# is used **23 times in that file and every one of them is through `host->ioaddr`** (`:587`, `:597`,
# `:646`, `:653`, `:2079-2085`, `:2416-2433`, `:2490-2512`) and **zero times through
# `msm_host->core_mem`**. `host->ioaddr` is `hc_mem` (0xf9824900), so the register the driver's MCLK
# select lives in is `hc_mem + 0x10C` = **0xF9824A0C** (inside the standard file's own vendor-specific
# area, 16 bytes inside `hc_mem`'s declared 0x11c), while this ladder has read and written
# `core_mem + 0x10C` = **0xF982410C** since 705. **707 section 2's "the field did not take" is an
# ADDRESS ERROR and is corrected in place; whether the MCLK field took is UNMEASURED.** The census could
# not catch it: it names windows by the `movt`/`movw` pair the ARM materializes, and the arm really does
# materialize `core_mem`. Rung 8 reads BOTH addresses in one run and settles two-registers-or-alias;
# until then no rung moves the store.
#
# THE COMMENT-ONLY CORRECTION IS PROVED BY A REBUILD AND A `cmp`: `entry_storage.c`'s claim that 0x10C
# is "the fifth `core_mem` offset" is replaced by the correction paragraph, and the image rebuilt at
# THE PRESSED ARM'S OWN SWITCHES (`STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=7` with the sixteen entry switches
# `out/xnu_arm_entry-config.txt` records - a blind invocation is REFUSED BY NAME, and two of this step's
# refusals were the build being right: `ENTRY_TRACE=0` "would shape nothing while the record said it
# did" (692's clause, the m720 shape a second time), then `ISTACK_SEPARATE` defaulting to 1 where the
# arm has 0). The build exits 0, its census line reads `core_mem [120 0 120 120 268 268 ] ... hc_mem
# [47 44 44 41 ](strb strh strh strb )`, and **all seven arm files are byte-for-byte identical**:
# stage90.img 9cacee09, stage90-qcdt.img f0ad4405, xnu_arm_entry.bin 7b1f3799, xnu_arm_entry.elf
# ad9cac90, stage90.elf 00c28a18, stage90.bin 71086f45, stage90_fixture.macho 52bc9c35. So `out/` still
# holds the pressed rung-7 arm with the identity readiness finds by hashing its `stage90-qcdt.img`.
#
# ONE THING THIS STEP MEASURED ABOUT ITSELF: **two builds at once produce a false verdict about the
# artifact.** Two of the three rebuild attempts overlapped, and the loser exited 1 with
# `FAIL: ast_taken_user is not in the linked image - 511's clause needs...` - a sentence about the
# ARTIFACT, produced while the other build was mid-link rewriting the same object files. Run alone, the
# same switches exit 0. The message was a reading of a half-written link: a check whose input another
# writer is rewriting reports a property of the race. **The gate's rule for its firer - one gate, one
# runner - has a third member: one build.** Memory row m721.
#
# STILL OWED AFTER THIS STEP: the rung-8 arm itself (this block is a pre-registration, not a build);
# `CORE_VENDOR_SPEC`'s MCLK readback at the vendor's own address (rung 8 takes the two-address reading,
# and moving rung 6's stores would be a re-baseline of its cells); the completion itself (a kernel
# object); the RCG rate write; the card's rail (nothing in this image powers a supply). Carried
# unchanged: what actually returns a run (8/17/24/27/24/27/25/28/18 s); the ending's first store
# faulting (0x0fa0065c, UNREAD in 709); the width clause of the store census never fired on a WIDENED
# DEVICE store; the 691 section 5 `entry_note_wfi` readback; `entry_reset.h`'s false IMEM claim; 676
# section 6 / 677 section 6; the 684-owed runner clause; `tools/xnu_dt_requirements.py` and the
# "master" value; the two peer-lane tripwire repairs; BIT(29) of `_clk_ahb_cbcr` published and unnamed;
# the seam address pinned in two files; and the gate's narration of `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE`, now
# EIGHT RUNGS SHORT once rung 8 exists (2-8) - peer lane, by message and never by edit (710's message to
# `run-experiment-526` carries the rung list rather than asking the peer to read it out of the source).
#
# ==================================================================================================
# 710: THE RUNG-8 ARM, BUILT AND PARKED. The block above is the PRE-REGISTRATION and its "STILL OWED
# AFTER THIS STEP: the rung-8 arm itself (this block is a pre-registration, not a build)" is SUPERSEDED
# HERE rather than edited - the arm now exists, in `out/` and in the park below. Nothing in it was
# wrong about the build; ONE OF ITS PREDICTIONS WAS, and the correction is in the next paragraph.
#
# WHAT THE ARM IS. `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=8` = 708's arm PLUS the vendor's own `sdhci_msm_pwr_irq`
# (`sdhci-msm.c:1990-2099`) as a client of this image's dispatcher: `entry_irq_register_client(170,
# st_pwr_irq, 0)` and `entry_irq_enable_line(170, 0x01)`, both BEFORE the card-power byte, so the latch
# the byte sets is ANSWERED rather than left standing. 709's press is the reason: the rung-7 arm's log
# carries `_irq_other_count=0x00000001` and `_irq_other_iar=0x000000aa` (170 = SPI 138 = `pwr_irq`,
# `msm8974.dtsi:502`), the arm the dispatcher takes for a line handed to nobody, and the run ended
# there. The handler is its own function - `st_pwr_irq`, 0x1cc bytes at `0x8000cff8` - and its body is a
# THREE-STORE device handler: `CORE_PWRCTL_STATUS 0xDC` read as a byte AND as a word from one moment,
# `CORE_PWRCTL_CLEAR 0xE4 <- the status` (the vendor's acknowledge), `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL 0xE8 <- the ack`
# with its readback, and `hc_mem + 0x10C`'s read-modify-write (act 6, `IO_HIGH` clearing
# `CORE_IO_PAD_PWR_SWITCH` bit 16). The three arms that may sleep (`sdhci_msm_setup_vreg`,
# `setup_pins`, `set_vdd_io_vol`) are ABSENT because the vendor's own
# `devm_request_threaded_irq(..., NULL, sdhci_msm_pwr_irq, IRQF_ONESHOT, ...)` (`:2937-2939`) declares
# a THREADED handler, and this client runs in `fleh_irq_kernel`'s frame.
#
# THE PREDICTION THAT WAS WRONG, AND IT IS THE INTERESTING KIND. Experiment-710 section 2 and section
# 5.2 predicted the probe's store census would read `hc_mem [47 44 44 41 268 ]` because "the handler's
# 0x10C" was counted as a store of the probe's own body. **The build says `hc_mem [47 44 44 41 ]`,
# unchanged from the pressed rung-7 arm** - the handler is a SEPARATE SYMBOL, so its 0x10C store is
# outside `entry_storage_probe`'s window, and the only movement inside that census line is the
# messenger set (`through [sl r7 r3 r6 ]` -> `through [sl r9 r3 r7 ]`). The one value - `hc_mem`'s
# store set - had two readings, and the pre-registration wrote the wrong one down
# ([[mi4-one-value-two-definitions]]); the document and the README row were corrected in this step and
# the `268` store is asserted by `st_pwr_irq`'s OWN clause, against `0xf9824a0c`.
#
# THE RUNG-8 CLAUSE NOW PRINTS ITS READING. The clause's first form had assertions only, so a passing
# rung-8 build and a build that never entered the clause - it is guarded by `-ge 8`, and a switch no
# build reads is how a whole clause goes missing (m720) - printed the same thing: nothing. Rebuilt with
# a success line, and the build's own output is the arm's evidence:
#
#   xnu_entry_710: st_pwr_irq's device accesses are
#     [f98240dc:ldrb f98240dc:ldr f98240e4:strb f98240e8:ldrb f98240e8:strb f9824a0c:ldr f9824a0c:str]
#     with counts [f98240dc:ldrb=2 f98240dc:ldr=1 f98240e4:strb=1 f98240e8:ldrb=4 f98240e8:strb=2
#                   f9824a0c:ldr=3 f9824a0c:str=1],
#     its non-device accesses are [IMG:ldr IMG:str], and entry_storage_probe calls
#     entry_irq_register_client 1 time(s) and entry_irq_enable_line 1 time(s)
#
# THE COUNTS ARE A MINIMUM AND THE BUILT BODY SHOWS WHY: 0xDC:ldrb=2 and 0xE8:ldrb=4 are the
# compiler's tail duplication along the decode's paths, so section 5.3's "exactly one ldrb at 220" was
# a claim about a SOURCE and this clause is a claim about an IMAGE. The property that survives both is
# which addresses, at which widths, with the readbacks present.
#
# MEASURED AFTER THE REBUILD, WHICH IS THE POINT OF REBUILDING: only ONE line of `build_entry.sh`'s
# clause changed (the echo), and **EVERY non-source file came back byte-identical** - xnu_arm_entry.bin
# `df4d38e5`, .elf `92956749`, -config.txt `9e970291`, stage90.bin `d753b410`, stage90.elf `e345ee08`,
# stage90.img `73678226`, stage90-qcdt.img `454acfe1`, SHA256SUMS.txt `20b01671`, fixture `52bc9c35`.
# `xnu_arm_entry-sources.txt` is the one file that moved, `a8635b6b` -> `0e94e43f`, because it records
# `build_entry.sh` by content - a manifest whose subject changed while its artifact did not.
#
# THE ARM'S DELTA FROM THE PRESSED RUNG-7 PARK, MEASURED BY `cmp` AND BY BLOB OFFSET. The embedded
# entry image changed and NOTHING ELSE did: `stage90.bin` differs in 581029 bytes, ALL of them inside
# `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` (file offset 494236 = 0x78a9c, first differing byte 495192 = blob+956, last
# 5997985 = blob+5535749); the qcdt and the boot image differ in 581049 - those 581029 plus **20 bytes
# of the boot header's own `id` field at 576-595, which is `sha1(kernel, ramdisk=0, second=0)` and
# therefore MUST change** - with zero differing bytes outside those two regions. The `.img` carries the
# entry blob one 2048-byte header page later (`496284` = 0x7929c) and is otherwise `stage90.bin` plus a
# 1532-byte zero tail; the qcdt adds the device-tree blob (2521088 bytes) and differs from the `.img` in
# the header's `dt_size` word at offset 40-43 (0x00000000 -> 0x00267800, two bytes) and nowhere else.
# THE PAYLOAD'S OWN LAYOUT DID NOT MOVE: `.text` 6030666, `.data` 1208, `.bss` 644048, all 465 symbols at
# the same addresses (verified by diffing `nm -S` of both payload els), and `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` at
# VA `0x80a9c` size `0x547a7c` in both arms - so a 768-byte growth of the entry image's own `.text`
# (`5316960 -> 5317728`, with `.data` 206512 and `.bss` 379720 byte-for-byte unchanged) moves nothing
# the payload's code does arithmetic with.
#
# AND THE SEAM DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE FIRST RUNG SINCE 698 FOR WHICH THAT IS TRUE: `entry_trace.c`'s
# `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` and `stages/stage90/run_and_capture.sh`'s literal are both still `0x800482dc`
# and were NOT re-pinned, because `platform_cache_idle_exit` (0x800482d4) and
# `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit` (0x8047ead8) are at the addresses the rung-7 arm had them at. What
# DID move: `entry_storage_probe` 0x8000cff8 -> 0x8000d1c4 (+0x1cc, the size of `st_pwr_irq`, which the
# linker placed before it) and its body grew 0x1638 -> 0x1734 (+0xfc, the two wiring calls and the two
# publications); the rung-7 card-power store moved 0x8000e26c -> 0x8000e50c and now reads
# `strb r4, [r7, #41]` (the base register changed r6 -> r7, which is what a new function's register
# pressure does); and the entry elf's symbol deltas against the rung-7 arm are +0x0 for 12602 symbols,
# +0x1cc for 1, +0x2c0 for 226, +0x2c8 for 35 and +0x300 for 559, with exactly three new symbols
# (`g_pwr_irq_calls`, `g_pwr_irq_arms`, `st_pwr_irq`) and none gone.
#
# THE ENTRY IMAGE'S SOURCES MANIFEST MOVED IN EXACTLY TWO OF ITS TWENTY-THREE FILE LINES -
# `build_entry.sh` (the `sym_size` helper, the rung-8 case, the ladder's 0-to-8 message, the rung-8
# clause and its success echo) and `entry_storage.c` (the rung-8 block, the `ST_VENDOR_SPEC` rename, the
# ladder `#error` text) - and the entry config record differs from the pressed arm's in EXACTLY TWO of
# its twenty-two lines, the entry SHA256 and the rung digit (7 -> 8), verified by `diff`. The gate's
# narration of `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` is SEVEN RUNGS SHORT (2 through 8) - peer lane, by message
# and never by edit.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 713: THE RUNG-9 ARM BUILT AND PARKED - `armed-storage-pwrwait-8b824cac`
#
# WHAT THE ARM ADDS, IN ONE SENTENCE: the driver's own next statement after rung 7's power byte -
# `sdhci_set_power`'s `check_power_status(host, REQ_BUS_ON)` (sdhci.c:1371-1372), whose vendor
# implementation is an unbounded `wait_for_completion` - ported as a BOUNDED tick poll whose end
# condition is the CONTROLLER's `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL & BUS_SUCCESS` and not the image-side flag, with
# CLOCK_CONTROL read as a halfword on BOTH sides of the wait (the reading 711 section 3 left open).
# The one new switch is `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS=1920000` (100 ms at 19,200,000 Hz), refused
# outside [1, 19200000] by an `#error` in entry_storage.c and asserted present in the linked body.
#
# **MEASURED CONTAINMENT, AND IT IS THIS ARM'S OWN MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN THE RUNG-8 PARK'S
# INHERITED ONE.** That park's record says "zero differing bytes outside those two regions"; for
# THIS arm that sentence is FALSE and the true one is one byte-count more interesting. Against the
# PRESSED rung-8 park (`armed-storage-pwrirq-df4d38e5`):
#   stage90.bin        differs in 644008 bytes: 644004 inside `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` (file offset
#                      494236 = 0x78a9c, first differing byte 495192 = blob+956, last 5997985 =
#                      blob+5535749) and **4 OUTSIDE IT**, at 273472/273473 and 273668/273688.
#   stage90.img        differs in 644028 = those 644008 + the boot header's own 20-byte `id` field at
#                      576-595, which is `sha1(kernel, ramdisk=0, second=0)` and therefore MUST
#                      change. ZERO differing bytes outside those two regions.
#   stage90-qcdt.img   differs in the same 644028 and NOWHERE ELSE; the 2521088-byte device-tree blob
#                      after the payload is BYTE-IDENTICAL (`q[len(im):] == oq[len(oi):]`), and the
#                      header's `dt_size` word at offset 40 is `00 78 26 00` - i.e. 0x00267800
#                      little-endian, the extension's own length, and IDENTICAL to the pressed park's.
#                      (A hex dump is not a number until you say which end: reading those four bytes
#                      big-endian gives 0x00782600, a value that appears nowhere in the image.)
#
# **AND THE FOUR BYTES OUTSIDE THE BLOB ARE NOT STRAY - THEY ARE THE PAYLOAD'S COPY OF THE ENTRY
# IMAGE'S REGENERATED LAYOUT.** Disassembled in both payloads they are two instructions, each +0x40:
#   VA 0x4ac40/0x4ac41 (`stage90_xnu_entry_run+0x68`): `movw r3, #0x45c8` -> `#0x4608`, paired with
#     the UNCHANGED `movt r3, #0x805a` at 0x4ac44 - i.e. the constant `0x805a45c8` -> `0x805a4608`,
#     the entry image's own `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY_BSS_END`.
#   VA 0x4ad04 and 0x4ad18: `movw r2/r1, #0xcb48` -> `#0xcb88` with `movt ..., #5` unchanged - the
#     pair `0x0005cb48` -> `0x0005cb88`, the entry image's BSS LENGTH (BSS_END - BSS_START =
#     0x805a4608 - 0x80547a80), handed to `memset` at 0x4ad14 and re-materialized at 0x4ad18.
# Both are +64, which is EXACTLY the entry image's `.bss` growth, and `out/stage90/xnu_arm_entry.h`
# HAS been regenerated (`build_entry.sh` writes it; mtime between the entry build and the payload
# build) with `BSS_START 0x80547a80` / `BSS_END 0x805a4608` / `BIN_BYTES 5536380` - so the payload was
# built against the new layout and the constant it carries agrees with the image it hands over. The
# header is a gitignored build byproduct and is in NEITHER park's 11 files (the pressed rung-8 park
# lacks it too, and is not retrofitted); what this arm's record can say instead is the measurement
# above, which is strictly stronger than carrying the file.
#
# **THE PAYLOAD'S OWN LAYOUT DID NOT MOVE, AND THIS IS MEASURED OVER ALL 465 OF ITS SYMBOLS.**
# `.text` 6030666, `.data` 1208, `.bss` 644048 - identical in both arms - and `nm -S` of the two
# payload ELFs compares EQUAL as a whole (name, address and size for every symbol), which is a
# stronger statement than the rung-8 record's "all 465 symbols at the same addresses" and is why the
# only out-of-blob difference can be a layout constant at all.
#
# **THE ENTRY IMAGE, MEASURED SECTION BY SECTION** (objdump -h, the record's convention):
#   .text  5317728 -> 5318464 (+736: `st_pwr_wait` is 592 bytes, `st_pwr_irq` grew +108, and the rest
#          is alignment)
#   .data  206512  -> 206512 (UNCHANGED)
#   .bss   379720  -> 379784  (+64: the three published flag words + alignment; this is the number
#          the payload's four out-of-blob bytes carry)
#   26540 symbols against the rung-8 arm's 26536: FOUR new names and NONE gone -
#          `st_pwr_wait` (0x8000d230, +592), `g_pwr_curr_state` (0x805541a8), `g_pwr_curr_io`
#          (0x805541ac) and `g_pwr_irq_done` (0x805541b0), i.e. the waiter and the three words it
#          publishes beside `g_pwr_irq_calls` (0x805541a4, the rung-8 arm's own word, unmoved).
#   The address deltas over the 26536 shared symbols: +0 for 21016, +64 for 3776, +752 for 1126,
#          +704 for 335, +16 for 184, +708 for 79, +12 for 13, +736 for 4, +700 for 1, and TWO
#          symbols moved BACKWARD (-672 and -736) - the linker re-laid out as it does every rung, and
#          the backward pair is why the deltas are measured rather than assumed monotone.
#   `st_pwr_irq` 0x8000cff8 +460 -> +568 (the +108 its tail's three publications cost);
#   `entry_storage_probe` 0x8000d1c4 +5940 -> 0x8000d480 +5948 (the +8 of the `bl` to the waiter).
#
# **AND THE SEAM DID NOT MOVE, THE SECOND RUNG RUNNING FOR WHICH THAT IS TRUE:** `entry_trace.c`'s
# `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` and `run_and_capture.sh`'s literal are both still `0x800482dc` and were NOT
# re-pinned, because `platform_cache_idle_exit` (0x800482d4, +108) and
# `__wrap_platform_cache_idle_exit` (0x8047ead8, +120) are at the addresses the rung-8 arm had them
# at.
#
# **THE ENTRY SOURCES MANIFEST MOVED IN EXACTLY THREE OF ITS LINES** - the entry SHA256 field, and
# the two files that changed by content: `build_entry.sh` (2478d3e9 -> 2ea248de: the ladder's `9) ;;`
# case and its widened message, the new `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS` switch and its three plumbing
# sites, the two factored classifier functions `classify_body`/`resolve_img_addrs` with their two
# repairs, the rung-8 clause's widening to the resolver, and the new rung-9 clause) and
# `entry_storage.c` (aadf08bc -> 17ec162d: the two-pass `PWR_WAIT_TICKS` switch, the four `ST_REQ_*`
# constants, the three published words, `st_pwr_irq`'s timestamp and vendor tail, the whole
# `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE >= 9` block with `st_pwr_wait` and its probe wiring) - and the file
# keeps its 2348 bytes. The entry CONFIG record differs in exactly THREE of its lines (the SHA256
# field, the rung digit 8 -> 9, and the new `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS=1920000` line) and grew 875 ->
# 910 bytes: it is the ONE record that names this arm.
#
# **THE TWO INSTRUMENT FINDINGS THIS BUILD FORCED, both of which the arm's own success echo now
# depends on.** (1) The rung-8 clause's program-order pair tracker was FALSIFIED FROM THE OTHER SIDE:
# GCC duplicated the handler's tail across a `bne` (8000d0fc -> 8000d1fc) and re-materialized only
# `movt r3, #0xf982` at the target over a `mov r3, #0x4000` two blocks earlier, leaving a stale
# `mov r3, #1` as the last low half - so a linear scan reported three of the handler's seven device
# accesses at `f98200e9`/`f9820a0d`, addresses no code writes. The repair is a NEW ADDRESS MODEL, not
# a wider expected set: a pair is FRESH only when both halves arrived since the last control transfer
# (the control-transfer set is `b`/`bx`/`blx`, `ldm*`/`pop` with pc, `ldr`/`ldrd` with pc, `mov pc`),
# and a stale pair is ENUMERATED against the record's DECLARED device set - accepted only when exactly
# one candidate is declared, else refused as `NODECL-<hi>-<off>` or `AMBI-<hi>-<off>`. Mnemonics are
# normalized by dropping the condition suffix and KEEPING the width, so `strne`/`str` are one store on
# two paths while `str`/`strb` stay two widths. (2) The `.bss` resolution's waiver set was VACUOUS in
# its first draft - it marked every `.bss` symbol waived, so the `not-waived` direction, the one the
# whole widening exists for, could never fire. It was caught by a smoke test in which the handler's own
# neighbour `g_pwr_irq_arms` read as waived; the reader now builds `inbss[]` and the `END` block
# builds the waiver set from the recorded names, and the refusal is exercised on every branch in the
# build log.
#
# **AND THE PAYLOAD BUILD'S ENTRY SWITCH HAS NO DEFAULT, WHICH IS THE HAZARD THIS STEP'S FIRST
# BUILD STEPPED IN.** `./build.sh` carries no default for `STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY`: the switch exists only
# if `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1'` is passed, and `stage90.h:7083`'s
# `#if !defined(STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY)` otherwise defines it as `0u` - so a bare `./build.sh` writes a
# DIFFERENT ARM into `out/stage90/` in place, in silence. 681 section 4 measured exactly that, and
# measured with it that the payload build IS byte-for-byte reproducible WHEN the arm's own flags are
# passed. **A MECHANISM THIS RECORD FIRST ASSERTED FROM THE SYMPTOM IS FALSIFIED BY ITS OWN
# MEASUREMENT AND IS CORRECTED HERE**: the first version said `./build.sh` "silently drops `#define
# STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 1` ... because the payload's macro export has no trailing space and the writer's
# last pattern never matches it". The `diff` quoted beside it says otherwise - the two lines differ in
# the VALUE (`1` vs `0u`) and in the POSITION (line 5 vs line 7), so the `0u` line WAS WRITTEN and
# nothing was dropped. Re-measured on `build.sh`'s own `-dM` pipeline: with no `-D` the line is
# `#define STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 0u` at line 7; with `-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1` it is `#define
# STAGE90_XNU_ENTRY 1` at line 5 (a command-line `-D` is defined before the header is read). This
# arm's payload was therefore built as `STAGE90_EXTRA_CFLAGS='-DSTAGE90_XNU_ENTRY=1' ./build.sh`, after
# which `stage90-build-config.txt` is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pressed rung-8 park's. The item for the
# lane that owns `build.sh` is therefore not "repair the writer" (there is nothing wrong with it) but
# "the arm-critical switch has no default" - and no press may be fired from a tree whose config record
# was written by a build that did not pass the arm's own flags.
#
# THE GATE'S NARRATION OF `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE` IS NOW EIGHT RUNGS SHORT (2 through 9) - peer
# lane, by message and never by edit.
#

#
# 713 ADDENDUM, SAME DAY, AFTER THE FIRST READINESS RUN - and the first run refused for two reasons, of
# which ONE WAS THE READINESS TOOL ITSELF.
#
# (1) **A NARRATION LINE CAN PASS `bash -n` AND NEVER RUN AS WRITTEN.** The rung-9 `entry_arm` assignment
# contained `read as "no wait"` - two BARE double quotes inside a double-quoted string. Bash does not
# refuse that: the quote toggles, the SPACE inside the pair ends the word, and the line parses as
# `entry_arm="<everything up to the quote>"` followed by the words `no`, `wait**`, `because`, ... - so the
# assignment becomes an ENVIRONMENT PREFIX for a command named `no` and **the shell variable is left at its
# previous value in silence**. `bash -n` said "SYNTAX ok". The measured symptom was
# `tools/verify_press_ready.sh: line 700: wait** because rung 8 IS the no-wait arm ... : File name too
# long`, and the damage was invisible in the worst way: `entry_arm` silently kept the value it already had,
# so the arm-name row refused ("the arm named above does not quote the rung the entry record carries")
# while the line that broke it looked fine. The repair is `\"no wait\"`; the same line's neighbour in the
# rung-9 consequence block already used escaped quotes, so this was one line's slip and not a convention
# gap. **The row caught it, which is the row doing its job** - the same shape as
# [[mi4-one-value-two-definitions]] and [[mi4-silence-is-a-reading-only-if-success-is-silent]]: a failure
# that leaves a variable UNCHANGED is indistinguishable from a success unless something downstream reads it.
#
# (2) **THE CROSS-LANE HALF, AND NO PRESS MAY BE ARMED FOR THIS SET UNTIL IT IS GREEN.**
# `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS` is the build's new arm key and the peer lane's `preflight_boot_check.sh`
# `ENTRY_CFG_KEYS` does not carry it, so the gate's converse clause fires on the FIRST run - exactly the
# state 678's record describes and 683/686/690/692 each resolved, one name at a time, one arm later. The
# gate row reads: "REFUSING: out/stage90/xnu_arm_entry-config.txt carries key(s) this gate does not print:
# STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS". The change needed is three lines in that file - the key in
# `ENTRY_CFG_KEYS` (line 598), a comment paragraph in the 683/686/690/692 series with the two prose counts
# moving from twelve to thirteen, and the variant-subset list in the refusal at line 609 gaining the name
# with its count. **It was requested by message and NOT edited**: `run-experiment-526` owns that file
# ([[mi4-file-lanes]]) and was live and waiting when the message was sent, and the request carries the
# exact lines, the reason, what the switch is, and the explicit statement that no safety clause of that
# gate is in scope. The 683/686/690/692 records describe the same change as having been made "under the
# operator's standing authorisation for exactly this change"; the lane rule still puts the edit in the
# owning lane's hands first, and this record will say which of the two paths produced it.
#
# **READINESS AFTER THE REPAIR: 4 of 5, AND THE ONE RED ROW IS (2).** The arm-name row is now green and
# reports its own census - "the 56 name(s) and 2 glob(s) it writes were read against the arm's own
# `xnu_live_` strings, and every name is a suffix of a string this image publishes" - so the rung-9
# narration is measured against the artifact and not against itself. Row 1 (live bytes ARE the parked
# bytes), row 2 (the park verifies against the record) and row 5 (a press would be caught: `adb` lists
# `4a2fe00b` as `device`) are green. **The arm is armed, readable and correct; the gate is the only thing
# standing between it and a press.**

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 713: THE RUNG-9 ARM PRESSED - `armed-storage-pwrwait-8b824cac`. THE POLL SPENT ITS WHOLE BOUND, THE
# COMPLETION ARRIVED 60 us LATE, AND THE CONTEXT THAT WAITED HAD IRQs MASKED.
#
# Readiness 5 of 5, exit 0, at 00:31:01; ONE gate (exit 0, 578 stdout lines) at 00:31:31; EXACTLY ONE
# runner (`--expect-arm=armed-storage-pwrwait-8b824cac`) **EXIT 0** - returned and captured - at
# 00:32:39; capture 625,346 B `816b792244f726b9...` archived by hand as
# `out/stage90/captures/rung9-pwrwait-20260926-003122-{last_kmsg,press,gate,run}`; firer
# `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh` (`7f23cae5...`) with `done. gate=0 runner=0`, so NO FIRER IS ARMED.
# The device came back on its own: `adb devices` lists `4a2fe00b device`, and nothing was flashed
# (`fastboot boot` only; `33e80afe` was off the bus for the whole press).
#
# **THE RUNG'S OWN ANSWER, AND IT IS A REFUTATION OF THE ARM'S DESIGN RATHER THAN OF THE CONTROLLER.**
# All nineteen `_wait_*` cells are present:
#   `_wait_req=0x2` (REQ_BUS_ON, the only request), `_wait_bound=0x001d4c00` = 1,920,000 ticks =
#   100.000 ms (the switch, in the linked artifact, as the build clause asserts).
#   `_wait_reset=0` - the vendor's already-satisfied branch was NOT taken, so the poll ran.
#   `_wait_ctl_before=0x00` and `_wait_ctl_after=0x00` - **the controller's `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL` never
#   carried `BUS_SUCCESS`, before the wait or after it**. `_wait_timeout=1`.
#   `_wait_polls=0x00068800` = 428,032 = 418 inner batches of 1,024 reads; `_wait_ticks=0x001d50aa`
#   = 1,921,194 ticks = 100.062 ms, i.e. the bound crossed mid-batch (overshoot 1,194 ticks = 62.2 us),
#   at 4.49 ticks per device read.
#   **`_wait_cpsr=0x80000093` - SVC mode, `I` SET (IRQs MASKED), `F` clear.** This is the cell that
#   explains the rung.
#   `_wait_done_before=0`, `_wait_state_before=0`, `_wait_io_before=0` and their `_after` twins all 0:
#   the handler's published fields were never filled, and `_wait_done=0` with `_wait_ctl_after=0x00` is
#   the two-memory pair AGREEING - the device-side and image-side readings both say the handshake did
#   not complete, and neither one is a cache-shadowed false negative.
#   `_wait_calls=0x0` at the wait's own end.
# **AND THE HANDLER RAN 60 us AFTER THE POLL GAVE UP.** `_wait_t0=0x0748bc55`; the wait's end is
# `t0 + 0x1d50aa`; the handler's own timestamp `_pwr_irq_at=0x07661180` is **1,153 ticks = 60.05 us
# after that end** - and `_post_t0` (the ending's clock) is only 217 ticks = 11.3 us after it. The
# count keys fill in the same story from the other side: `_pwr_irq_calls_once_before_byte=0x0` (the
# byte raised the line), `_pwr_irq_calls_probe_end=0x0` (the handler had STILL not run at the probe's
# tail - the wait is inside the probe), and `_pwr_irq_calls=0x1` in the final log (it ran exactly
# once, after all of it). `_pwr_irq_ctl_before=0x0` -> `_pwr_irq_ctl_after=0x1` with
# `_pwr_irq_status=0x02` and `_pwr_irq_ack=0x01`: when it finally ran it did the rung-8 acknowledge
# correctly, and no `_irq_other_*` key appears, so the line still has its owner.
#
# **SO THE RUNG'S PREMISE IS FALSIFIED BY ITS OWN MEASUREMENT: A BOUNDED SPIN IN THIS CONTEXT CANNOT
# OBSERVE THIS COMPLETION AT ALL.** The completion rung 9 waits for is `BUS_SUCCESS` in
# `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL`, and that bit is written BY THE INTERRUPT HANDLER. The waiter runs with `I` set,
# so the handler cannot be taken while it spins; the handler is delivered 60 us after the mask lifts,
# i.e. as soon as it can be. **The vendor's own `wait_for_completion` works precisely because it
# SLEEPS - it unmasks - and this arm replaced a sleep with a spin.** That is the finding, and it
# reframes rung 9 rather than grading it: the port kept the predicate and the ack and lost the one
# property that makes the wait satisfiable in a masked context.
#
# **The two readings this press cannot separate, named rather than chosen.** (a) The IRQ was pending
# throughout the spin and was delivered when the mask lifted; (b) the controller really did take about
# 100 ms to acknowledge, and would have been caught by a longer bound. The evidence favours (a) -
# `_wait_cpsr`'s `I`, plus a handler delay that tracks the END of the spin rather than a device timer -
# but one run cannot separate a 100 ms device latency from a 100 ms masked window, because this arm's
# bound and its mask have the same length by construction. The distinction is worth a rung: a SECOND
# wait taken immediately after the first, with the IRQ no longer pending, returns at once under BOTH
# readings; what separates them is a wait that unmasks (or yields) DURING the spin, or a source of
# completion that is not the interrupt at all.
#
# **AND 711 SECTION 3 IS ANSWERED, ONE RUN INSTEAD OF TWO.** 711 could not tell whether
# `CLOCK_CONTROL 0x2C` bit 1 was a race or the machine's state, because it had two boots and no
# before/after inside either. This arm reads the same register THREE times in one run - rung 7's pair
# around the power byte and the waiter's pair around the wait: `_pwr_cc_before=0xE045` ->
# `_pwr_cc_after=0xE047` -> `_wait_cc_before=0xE047` -> `_wait_cc_after=0xE047`. **The bit is set BY THE
# POWER BYTE'S OWN STORE and is STABLE across a hundred milliseconds afterwards**: not a race, and
# deterministic. It also completes the correction of 711's one unpredicted cell - 709 read `0xE045` on
# both sides because 709's arm (rung 6) did not write the byte at all.
#
# **THE INHERITED CELLS ALL READ AS THEY DID ON THE PRESSED RUNG-8 ARM**, which is the check that this
# arm changed only what it says it changed: `_storage_calls=1`, `_loads=6`, `_writes` published 0->4
# through the mode sequence, `_rst_stores=1`, `_mode_bit_after=1`, `_gate=1`, `_bcr=0x0`,
# `_cbcr=0x4ff1`, `_gcc_map=1`, `_map=1`, `_pwr_before=0x00` -> `_pwr_after=0x0b` with `_pwr_vdd=7`.
# **The ending is unchanged and did not move**: `_post_end_calls=0x8` with `_post_elapsed=0x090f19e0`
# = 7,915.9 ms at `_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800` = 19,200,000 Hz, i.e. the 690 clock fired on its own
# schedule and the machine stayed up through it - so the wait cost the run 100 ms of a 6,000 ms
# window and nothing else.
#
# **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET.** No command, no sector, no partition table, no mount, no driver beyond
# the fixture - this rung's whole device act is two halfword reads and a poll of one byte, and its log
# carries no `xnu_live_storage_*` key that names the medium. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.** What
# this press buys is the one fact the next rung's design needs: the completion is interrupt-delivered
# and the waiter's context masks interrupts.

# 716: THE RUNG-10 ARM BUILT AND PARKED - `armed-storage-pwrwait-aa2b051d`. THE BOUND IS THE
# EXPERIMENT: rung 9's wait, and NOTHING ELSE, with `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS` 1920000 -> 384000.
#
# WHAT THE ARM IS, IN ONE SENTENCE: the arm the operator's rung-9 press left behind, re-linked with
# one switch moved - 20 ms instead of 100 ms - because the press could not separate two readings that
# its own design made the same length (experiment-714 section 3): (a) the IRQ was pending throughout
# the spin and was delivered when the mask lifted, or (b) the controller really took about 100 ms.
# Under (a) the handler's arrival is pinned to the END OF THE SPIN and stays ~60 us from it whatever
# the bound; under (b) it is pinned to the BYTE and moves with the bound. `_pwr_irq_at - _wait_t0` is
# the reading; the separation is 80 ms, four times the bound. Pre-registration and the four outcomes:
# experiment-715.
#
# **THE ARM'S NAME IS ITS SWITCH VALUE, NOT ITS RUNG DIGIT.** `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=9` names TWO
# arms now - the pressed one (`..._PWR_WAIT_TICKS=1920000`, 714's 100 ms arm) and this one
# (`=384000`, the 20 ms arm) - so every sentence below and in the readiness narration names the tick
# value. That is the whole reason 715 section 5 exists: two arms one rung digit apart are one name
# with two definitions, which is this project's oldest defect (`mi4-one-value-two-definitions`).
#
# **MEASURED CONTAINMENT, AGAINST THE PRESSED RUNG-9 PARK (`armed-storage-pwrwait-8b824cac`), AND IT
# IS THE TIGHTEST THIS PROJECT HAS RECORDED.** This arm is a *constant* away from an arm that has
# been to the device:
#   xnu_arm_entry.bin  differs in EXACTLY 4 BYTES, at file offsets 0xD284, 0xD28C, 0xD420 and 0xD428
#                      (VA 0x8000D284/0x8000D28C/0x8000D420/0x8000D428): the two `mov`+`movt` pairs
#                      that carry the budget - `mov r1, #0x4C00`/`movt r1, #0x1D` -> `#0xDC00`/`#0x05`
#                      on the PUBLISHED `xnu_live_storage_pwr_wait_bound` cell, and the same pair on
#                      r9, the LOOP'S OWN bound, compared at 0x8000D468 `cmp r0, r9`. Both spellings
#                      are `mov` (rotated imm8) + `movt`, NOT `movw` + `movt`.
#   stage90.bin        differs in the same 4 bytes, all four INSIDE `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` (file
#                      offset 494236 = 0x78a9c) at blob-relative 0xD284 and 0xD420; **ZERO differing
#                      bytes anywhere else in a 6031876-byte file**.
#   stage90.img /      differ in EXACTLY 24 bytes: those 4, plus the boot header's 20-byte `id` field
#   stage90-qcdt.img   at 576-595, which is the legacy AOSP `sha1(kernel || kernel_size ||
#                      ramdisk_size || second_size)` - REPRODUCED here from the file itself, not
#                      quoted: sha1(payload + pack('<III', 6031876, 0, 0)) == the field, on both arms.
#                      **ZERO differing bytes outside those two regions**; the 2521088-byte
#                      device-tree blob after the payload is BYTE-IDENTICAL, the header's dt_size word
#                      at 40 is 0x00267800 (img: 0) in both, and the sizes are UNCHANGED (8556544 /
#                      6035456). So unlike 713 - whose arm sat 4 bytes outside the blob because the
#                      entry image GREW - this arm has NOTHING outside: its two images are the
#                      pressed arm's, plus two constants.
#   stage90.elf        section sizes UNCHANGED (text 6030666, data 1208, bss 644048) and `nm -S`
#                      output IDENTICAL as a whole to the pressed arm's - all 465 symbols at the same
#                      address with the same size, and `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` at VA 0x80a9c size
#                      0x547a7c in both.
#   xnu_arm_entry.elf  section sizes UNCHANGED (text 0x512740, data 0x326b0, bss 0x5cb88, debug same,
#                      6715636 bytes) and `nm -S` IDENTICAL as a whole - 26540 symbols, same addresses
#                      and sizes. **NO SYMBOL MOVED THIS STEP** (rung 9 moved `entry_storage_probe` by
#                      +8; a changed constant moves nothing), so `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` 0x800482dc is
#                      untouched for the third rung running and no file was re-pinned.
#   xnu_arm_entry-     differs in EXACTLY 2 LINES: the artifact SHA256 field and
#   config.txt         `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS=1920000` -> `=384000`. All 18 arm keys present.
#   xnu_arm_entry-     differs in EXACTLY 1 LINE - the SHA256 field. **NO SOURCE FILE CHANGED THIS
#   sources.txt        STEP**: the arm is a switch value, so `build_entry.sh` and `entry_storage.c`
#                      hash as they did on 713's arm, and the manifest keeps its 2348 bytes.
#   stage90-build-     BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pressed park (and to every arm since 653).
#   config.txt / the
#   fixture
# **THE BUILD REFUSED THE FIRST ATTEMPT AND THAT IS THE RECORD WORKING.** The arm-change guard
# (`build_entry.sh:875-920`) compares the 18 arm keys against the previous record and stops before
# writing anything; this invocation needed `STAGE90_ENTRY_ARM_CHANGE=1` because
# `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS` is one of them. A blind `./build_entry.sh` would have produced the
# pressed arm again and every downstream check would have agreed with it.
#
# ONE CONSEQUENCE WORTH NAMING, AND IT IS A MESSAGE AND NOT A CHECK: the build's rung-9 clause accepts
# `mov`/`movw` + `movt` (its refusal message says "no mov/movw + movt pair"), but its SUCCESS echo
# still says "carried by the movw/movt pair in its own body" - and on this arm the pair is
# `mov`+`movt`. The clause is right (it is the acceptance that matters); the sentence a reader takes
# from the log names the other encoding. Recorded as m724 rather than repaired here, because repairing
# a print is a build edit and this arm was parked by the build that printed it.

# 717: THE RUNG-10 ARM PRESSED - `armed-storage-pwrwait-aa2b051d`. **THE BOUND IS NOT THE DEVICE:**
# the completion arrived 20.17 ms after the byte, and the handler's arrival tracks the END OF THE
# MASKED SPIN to within 0.73 us.
#
# readiness 5/5 exit 0 (00:51:41), ONE gate (`--allow-xnu-entry`, exit 0, 579 stdout lines, 00:52:47),
# EXACTLY ONE runner (`--expect-arm=armed-storage-pwrwait-aa2b051d`) **EXIT 0** - returned and
# captured - at 00:53:56, 136 s of wall clock end to end. Firer `/tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh`
# (sha256 7f23cae5fb4edf21159d6241606f203c4320eed59641d207fb9a641ec6c485e6), budget 600 s,
# `done. gate=0 runner=0` - **no firer is armed**. Capture 625,029 B, sha256
# 98584a316213b735369a7f875150ac0d0d1cc7f32bc1e269a1f464a9e6691688, archived by hand as
# `out/stage90/captures/rung10-pwrwait20-20260926-005430-{last_kmsg.txt,press,gate,run}` (the runner
# does NOT archive; the press log's own copy is the `press` file). One non-persistent `fastboot boot`,
# nothing flashed, `33e80afe` off the bus, device back on its own (`adb devices` -> `4a2fe00b device`).
#
# **THE ANSWER, IN TICKS OF THE 19,200,000 Hz COUNTER THE HARDWARE REPORTED (`_post_cntfrq`), BOTH
# ARMS SIDE BY SIDE.** This arm's bound is 20 ms where the pressed arm's was 100 ms, and the bound is
# the only thing that moved:
#
#   quantity                    rung 9 (bound 1920000)     THIS ARM (bound 384000)     difference
#   _wait_bound                 1920000                    384000                      the one switch
#   _wait_ticks                 1921194 = 100.062 ms       386131 = 20.111 ms          -80.000 ms
#   _wait_polls                 428032                     86016 (84 x 1024)           -4.98x
#   _wait_timeout               1                          1                           spent again
#   _wait_ctl_before/after      0x00 / 0x00                0x00 / 0x00                 the ack still absent
#   _wait_cpsr                  0x80000093                 0x80000093                  SVC, I SET
#   post_t0 - spin end          217 = 11.30 us             217 = 11.30 us              IDENTICAL
#   _pwr_irq_at - spin end      1153 = 60.05 us            1167 = 60.78 us             +14 = +0.73 us
#   _pwr_irq_at - _wait_t0      1922347 = 100.122 ms       387298 = 20.172 ms          -80.000 ms
#   _pwr_irq_at - post_t0       936 = 48.75 us             950 = 49.48 us              +14 = +0.73 us
#
# **READING (b) IS DEAD AND IT DOES NOT NEED THE COUPLING ARGUMENT.** 714 section 3 refused to choose
# between (a) "the IRQ was pending throughout the spin and was delivered when the mask lifted" and (b)
# "the controller really takes about 100 ms, and the bound was a hair short". `_pwr_irq_at - _wait_t0`
# is **20.172 ms**: on this arm the completion arrived **inside 20.17 ms of the byte**, which is where
# `_wait_t0` is taken. A mechanism that takes ~100 ms cannot deliver in 20.17 ms, so there was never a
# 100 ms device latency to miss - the 100 ms 714 measured was **its own mask**. That is the reading
# 714's own evidence favoured and could not prove, and 714's own caution ("this arm set its bound and
# its mask to the same length by construction") is what made the proof possible by moving the bound.
#
# **AND READING (a) IS CONFIRMED TO 0.73 us.** The handler entered 60.78 us after the poll gave up and
# 49.48 us after the probe's own post record; on the 100 ms arm the same two offsets are 60.05 us and
# 48.75 us. `post_t0 - spin end` is **217 ticks on both arms** - the probe's own tail reaching its post
# record, identical because it is the same code - so the unmask is a property of the code AFTER the
# probe and not of the probe, and it costs a fixed ~49 us. The 0.73 us spread across an 80 ms change
# in the bound is 0.9 % of the quantity and four orders of magnitude below the change.
#
# **WHAT THIS BUYS THE PORT, AS A REQUIREMENT RATHER THAN A FINDING.** The completion is available
# essentially immediately (the latch is set within microseconds of the byte - rung 7's own pair, re-read
# on this run as `_pwr_status_before=0x00` -> `_pwr_status_after=0x02`), so **a bounded spin in a
# context that masks interrupts cannot observe it at ANY budget**: the budget and the mask are not two
# independent quantities, because the mask ends where the code that owns it returns and the spin is
# inside that code. The vendor's `wait_for_completion` works precisely because it SLEEPS; a port that
# keeps the predicate and drops the sleep loses the only mechanism by which the completion can arrive.
# **The next rung therefore has exactly two shapes and they are not a matter of taste: unmask around
# the spin, or take the wait where the mask is already clear.** Both are now choices with a measured
# fact behind them.
#
# **THE MATCHED PREDICTION.** 715 pre-registered, before the build: `_wait_bound = 0x0005DC00` in the
# linked artifact (the log carries 0x0005dc00); `_wait_ticks` = the bound plus a mid-batch remainder
# (386131, overshoot 2131 ticks = 111 us, inside one 1024-read batch, at 4.49 ticks per read); and
# `_pwr_irq_at - _wait_t0` at about 385,000 ticks under (a) against about 1,922,000 under (b)
# (measured 387,298 - the extra ~2,300 ticks are the batch overshoot plus the 1,167-tick wait for the
# mask to lift).
#
# **THE INHERITED CELLS ALL READ AS THEY DID ON THE PRESSED RUNG-9 ARM AND ON THE PRESSED RUNG-8 ARM**,
# which is the check that this arm changed only what it says it changed: `_storage_calls=1`,
# `_loads=6`, `_writes` published 0->4 through the mode sequence, `_rst_stores=1`, `_mode_bit_after=1`,
# `_gate=1`, `_bcr=0x0`, `_cbcr=0x4ff1`, `_gcc_map=1`, `_map=1`, `_pwr_before=0x00` -> `_pwr_after=0x0b`
# with `_pwr_vdd=7`. **711 section 3 is answered a SECOND time on a SECOND arm**:
# `_wait_cc_before = _wait_cc_after = 0xE047`, identical halves either side of the twenty milliseconds.
# The handler's side is unchanged and correct: `_pwr_irq_calls=1`, `_pwr_irq_intid=0xaa` (170),
# `_pwr_irq_ctl_before=0x0` -> `_pwr_irq_ctl_after=0x1`, `_pwr_irq_status=0x02`, `_pwr_irq_ack=0x01`,
# `_pwr_irq_status_after=0x00` (the latch let go), `_pwr_irq_state=0x02` and `_pwr_irq_io_level=0x08` -
# the two fields the predicate reads, written by the handler's tail. No `_irq_other_*` key at all.
#
# **AND TWO OF THOSE CELLS ARE EXPECTED BY CONSTRUCTION, WHICH IS m723.**
# `_pwr_irq_calls_once_before_byte` and `_pwr_irq_calls_probe_end` are 0 on BOTH arms and cannot be
# anything else while the arm masks interrupts: the handler cannot run between the byte and the read
# that follows it, and the probe's tail is 11.30 us wide. 710 section 2 pre-registered them as
# predictions; a cell whose value is fixed by the arm's own mask predicts nothing about the arm, and
# naming it a prediction makes the next reader trust it twice. Recorded, not repaired, in
# `mi4-measurement-defects`.
#
# **THE ENDING DID NOT MOVE**: `_post_end_calls=0x8` with `_post_cntfrq=0x0124f800` = 19,200,000 Hz and
# the elapsed pair past 7,997 ms - the 690 clock fired on its own schedule and the machine stayed up
# through it. **The shorter bound cost the run 20 ms of a 6,000 ms window and nothing else**, which is
# the safety property the rung was designed for, re-measured on a second arm: the poll is bounded, so
# the run always continues.
#
# **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET.** No command, no sector, no partition table, no mount, no driver beyond
# the fixture - this rung's whole device act is two halfword reads and a poll of one byte, and its log
# carries no key that names the medium. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.** What this press buys is the
# next rung's shape: the completion is interrupt-delivered, the waiter's context masks interrupts, and
# the completion is available long before any budget this arm could spend.

# =====================================================================================================
# 718: THE RUNG-11 ARM - THE WAIT TAKEN WITH THE MASK OFF, AND THE FIRST INTERRUPT THIS IMAGE TAKES
#      INSIDE THE CACHE-OFF IDLE-EXIT WINDOW. Built 2026-09-26 01:03-01:10, parked here unspent.
# =====================================================================================================
#
# **THE ARM IS `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=10` WITH `STAGE90_XNU_PWR_WAIT_TICKS=384000`** - 716's arm
# (20 ms) with ONE MECHANISM ADDED and no switch value moved. The mechanism is `st_pwr_wait`'s: the
# CPU's `I` bit is cleared for the poll (`mrs cpsr_saved, cpsr` / `cpsie i` / `mrs cpsr, cpsr`), the
# SAME bounded poll runs with the same `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL` end condition and the same 1024-read batch,
# and the mask is put back from the SAVED register (`msr cpsr_c, cpsr_saved` / `mrs cpsr_after, cpsr`)
# before the function's own tail. Encoded in the linked body at 0x8000d430 `mrs sl, CPSR`, 0x8000d434
# `cpsie i`, 0x8000d438 `mrs r1, CPSR`, then 0x8000d498 `msr CPSR_c, sl` and 0x8000d49c `mrs r1, CPSR`
# - one register carries the saved state, and the restore is adjacent to the reading that checks it.
#
# **WHY, IN ONE LINE OF 717's EVIDENCE.** Rung 10 measured the completion `20.172 ms` after the byte
# and the handler `60.78 us` after the spin gave up, on a 20 ms bound, against `100.122 ms` and
# `60.05 us` on the 100 ms one: the handler's distance from the spin's END did not move while its
# distance from the BYTE moved with the bound, so **the poll was measuring its own mask** and no budget
# can separate them (the mask ends where the code that owns it returns, and the spin is inside that
# code). 717 section 2 named two shapes for the next rung - unmask around the spin, or take the wait
# where the mask is already clear - and **only the first exists in this fixture**, because the whole
# probe runs inside the seam and `_wait_cpsr` reads `0x80000093` on every arm of this ladder.
#
# **THE ONE NEW CELL, AND THE CELL WHOSE VALUE IS THE ARM.** `_wait_cpsr` keeps its definition - the
# CPSR the poll runs under - and must now read `0x80000013` (SVC, `I` clear, `F` clear, `N` set: the
# same value with bit 7 cleared) where rungs 9 and 10 read `0x80000093`; **`_wait_cpsr_after`** is the
# new quantity, the CPSR the probe gives back, which must read `0x80000093` - the value rungs 9 and 10
# read as their PREMISE, which is what says the restore restored the SAVED state and not a constant
# that happens to look tidy (and why the restore is `msr` from the saved register and not `cpsid i`).
# **The outcome cells are rung 10's, flipped**: `_wait_timeout` must read `0` (it read `1` on both
# rungs 9 and 10) beside `_wait_ctl_after` = `0x01` (it read `0x00`), with `_wait_done` = `1` and
# `_wait_calls` = `1` - the vendor's predicate satisfied for the first time on this ladder, and the
# first time the waiter reads the handler's two driver-side fields AFTER the handler wrote them (so
# the flag-against-ack pair has a comparison for the first time: agreement through DRAM is the
# expected reading, disagreement is 686's cache-boundary defect measured rather than argued).
#
# **WHAT IS NEW ABOUT THE CONTEXT, WHICH IS THE REAL QUESTION: no interrupt has ever been delivered
# inside this window.** Every earlier delivery happened ~50 us after the spin, on the way OUT of the
# payload's cache-off idle-exit seam (`xnu_live_seam_sctlr` reads `C` clear on every run of this
# ladder). The delivery here is nearly certain - this site has taken the handler three times, so the
# line is enabled, in Group 0, at a priority `GICC_PMR` admits and targeted at this CPU - and the
# context is entirely new, which is what this press is for. A non-return (exit 2), an `_irq_other_*`
# key or an ending on `exception: irq line` is therefore a READING on this arm and names the line or
# the fault. Nothing is flashed, the poll is still bounded, the gate is still read before any store
# and the watchdog is still armed.
#
# **THE SOURCE THIS ARM IS THE BUILD OF: TWO FILES, BOTH NAMED IN THE SOURCES MANIFEST.**
# `entry_storage.c` gains the unmask, the restore, the new cell and the `#if STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE
# >= 10` guards (so that `STORAGE_PROBE=9` still builds the arm below it byte for byte), and its ladder
# `#error` now refuses above 10; `build_entry.sh` gains the `10)` case in its rung ladder and the
# matching sentence in the refusal, which is what made the first invocation refuse with "must be 0, 1,
# 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 or 9, not [10]" - the ladder is a `#if` in two files and a `case` in a third,
# and the third is the one a rung digit reaches first.
#
# **NO BUILD CLAUSE NEEDED A CHANGE, AND THAT IS A FACT ABOUT THE CLASSIFIER RATHER THAN A LUCK.** The
# rung-9 clause over `st_pwr_wait`'s body ran on this arm unchanged and printed: device accesses
# `[f982492c:ldrh f98240e8:ldrb]` with counts `[f982492c:ldrh=2 f98240e8:ldrb=4]`, non-device accesses
# `[IMG:ldr IMG:str]` at the same four `.bss` words, ONE `bl` to the waiter, and the budget 384000 in
# its own body as a `mov`/`movt` pair. **`cpsie`/`msr`/`mrs` are not memory accesses, and a published
# cell is a CALL to `entry_live_write` with a string** - so `_wait_cpsr_after` adds nothing to either
# set: no device access, no `.bss` word, no new symbol. The write set is rung 10's exactly.
#
# **THE CONTAINMENT, AGAINST THE PRESSED RUNG-10 ARM `armed-storage-pwrwait-aa2b051d`.** This is a
# CODE arm and not a constant arm, so the blob's bytes differ all through the region the code moved
# through - and **what matters is that NOTHING OUTSIDE THE BLOB MOVED, which is tighter than 713's arm
# and is a consequence of the growth being in `.text` only**: `stage90.bin` differs in 556511 bytes and
# **ZERO of them are outside `stage90_xnu_entry_blob`** (5,536,380 bytes at file offset 494236); both
# `stage90.img` and `stage90-qcdt.img` differ in 556531 = those 556511 inside the blob + **exactly the
# 20 bytes at 576-595**, the boot header's legacy-AOSP `id` field, which is a FUNCTION of the payload:
# `sha1(payload || <6031876,0,0>)` reproduces it from the file on BOTH arms (`9717a9e1...` here,
# `d444ee40...` on the pressed arm) - so the field changed for the reason it must and for no other.
# **`stage90.elf`'s `nm -S` output EQUALS the pressed arm's as a whole** (0 differing lines; text
# 6,030,666 / data 1,208 / bss 644,048 all unchanged; `stage90_xnu_entry_blob` still at VA 0x80a9c,
# size 0x547a7c) and the 2,521,088-byte device tree tail of the qcdt is BYTE-IDENTICAL, with `dt_size`
# at 40 unchanged and the file size unchanged at 8,556,544. **THE TWO LAYOUT CONSTANTS DID NOT MOVE**,
# and the reason is the entry image's growth: `xnu_arm_entry.elf`'s `.text` grew 32 bytes (5,323,240 ->
# 5,323,272) while `.data` (206,804) and `.bss` (379,784) are UNCHANGED, and the payload's BSS_END and
# BSS-length words are functions of `.bss` alone - which is why `stage90.bin` has zero bytes outside
# the blob where 713's arm had four. Inside the entry image: `st_pwr_wait` is at 0x8000d230 size
# 0x284 (was 0x250) and `entry_storage_probe` moved 0x8000d480 -> 0x8000d4b4 with its own size
# unchanged at 0x173c. **The seam did NOT move**: `platform_cache_idle_exit` is at 0x800482d4 and
# `STAGE90_XNU_SEAM_LR` 0x800482dc in both arms, so `entry_trace.c`'s constant and `run_and_capture.sh`'s
# literal were NOT re-pinned - the FOURTH rung running for which that is true.
#
# **ONE MEASUREMENT CAVEAT FOUND WHILE WRITING THIS RECORD, RECORDED AND NOT REPAIRED.** The rung-10
# record's "26,540 symbols" and this arm's check disagree with `nm`'s own line count on the same file
# (26,636): the recorded number is the count of DISTINCT NAMES, the other is the count of `nm` ENTRIES,
# and the two differ by 96 duplicated names (`.LANCHOR0` appears 7 times, `L_telemetry_needs_record`,
# `L_kdebug_enable`, `L_intstack_top` and `L_gVirtBase` 6 times each). Both arms measure 26,540
# distinct / 26,636 entries, so **no name was added or lost this step** - the defect is that the
# record's number did not say which of the two it was, and a reader comparing counts across arms would
# read a 96-symbol jump that is not there. This is `mi4-one-value-two-definitions`' shape at its
# mildest: one count, two definitions, both reproduce.
#
# **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET.** No command, no sector, no partition table, no mount, no driver beyond
# the fixture; the arm's whole device act is still two halfword reads of `CLOCK_CONTROL` and a poll of
# one `CORE_PWRCTL_CTL` byte, and nothing is written to the medium. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.**
# What this press buys, if it comes back with `_wait_timeout = 0`, is the LAST of the three mechanisms
# the driver's first handshake needs - request, interrupt, completion - measured working in the only
# context this fixture can offer, which is the precondition for every rung above it.

# =====================================================================================================
# 720: THE RUNG-11 PRESS - `armed-storage-pwrwait-unmask-13366e08`, PRESSED 2026-09-26.
#      readiness 5/5 exit 0 (01:16:30), ONE gate (--allow-xnu-entry, exit 0, 580 lines, 01:17:00),
#      EXACTLY ONE runner (--expect-arm=armed-storage-pwrwait-unmask-13366e08) -> EXIT 0 at 01:18:06.
#      Capture 622312 B sha256 fef6a5e8bf6cf3dea0ad8b28ca1e87f4d1d8908a2ae840f71e9b75afc9bd5d38,
#      archived by hand as out/stage90/captures/rung11-unmask-20260926-011552-{last_kmsg.txt,press,gate,run}
#      (the runner does NOT archive; the firer was /tmp/g668/press-on-clear.v5.sh, sha 7f23cae5..., and
#      it logged `done. gate=0 runner=0`, so NO FIRER IS ARMED). One non-persistent `fastboot boot`,
#      nothing flashed, 33e80afe off the bus, the device back on its own (adb lists 4a2fe00b device).
# =====================================================================================================
#
# **THE ARM ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS THE VENDOR'S OWN HANDSHAKE SATISFIED.** `_wait_timeout = 0`
# (it read 1 on BOTH rungs 9 and 10) beside **`_wait_ctl_after = 0x01`** - the controller carries
# `BUS_SUCCESS` for the first time on this ladder - with `_wait_done = 1`, `_wait_calls = 1`,
# `_wait_state_after = 0x02` and `_wait_io_after = 0x08`: the vendor's own predicate
# ((REQ_BUS_ON & curr_pwr_state) | (REQ_BUS_ON & curr_io_level)) is satisfied, and **this is the first
# time the waiter reads the handler's two driver-side fields AFTER the handler wrote them** - the
# flag-against-ack comparison rung 9's design argued for and never had. Agreement on both sides, which
# is the expected reading; disagreement would have been 686's cache-boundary defect measured.
#
# **AND `_wait_polls = 1` BESIDE `_wait_ticks = 1,403` (73.07 us) IS THE CELL THAT EXPLAINS HOW.**
# The exception is taken at the first instruction boundary after `cpsie i`, so **the poll never got to
# run**: `_pwr_irq_at` is 1,033 ticks (53.80 us) after `_wait_t0` - the client's first statement - its
# work took ~370 ticks (19.27 us), and the poll's ONE read then saw the handler's own store.
#   _wait_t0 = 0x076545e2 | _pwr_irq_at = 0x076549eb (+1033 = +53.80 us) | wait end = 0x07654b5d
#   (+1403 = +73.07 us) | _post_t0 = 0x07654c44 (+231 ticks past the wait's end, against 217 on both
#   masked arms - the probe's own tail, the same code).
# **THE ALTERNATIVE READING IS REFUTED BY AN EARLIER PRESS AND NOT BY AN ARGUMENT**: if the poll's
# first read had executed BEFORE the handler, `_wait_polls = 1` would mean the controller answered by
# itself within a few ticks of the mask coming off - and rungs 9 and 10 polled that same byte 428,032
# and 86,016 times and read `0x00` every time, on the same byte written by the same arm. Nothing on
# this machine raises that bit inside 100 ms except rung 8's handler, so the ack the poll found is the
# handler's store, the handler is what the interrupt delivered, and the wait was satisfied by the
# interrupt. **The budget was never touched** (384,000 ticks available, 1,403 spent): with the mask off
# the bounded poll is no longer an approximation of the block it replaced - it IS the block, and the
# bound is only the failure path. **The interrupt's own latency is the new reading: 53.80 us from
# `_wait_t0` to the client's first statement**, against the same path measured from the other end on
# rungs 9 and 10 as 1,153 and 1,167 ticks (60.05 and 60.78 us) after the spin's end - where the
# payload's own idle-exit code lifts `I`. Two masked arms and one unmasked arm agree on the order of
# the delivery's cost, and the ~6 us between them is the difference between the two starting points.
#
# **`_pwr_irq_calls_probe_end = 1` IS THE DELIVERY-INSIDE-THE-PROBE WITNESS.** It reads 0 on all three
# masked arms - where m723 recorded it as EXPECTED BY CONSTRUCTION and therefore not evidence about
# the spin - so this arm gives that note its counter-example: the client had run before the probe's
# tail, i.e. inside the probe, i.e. in the window. **AND THE RUN SURVIVED THE FIRST INTERRUPT THIS
# IMAGE HAS EVER TAKEN INSIDE THE CACHE-OFF IDLE-EXIT SEAM** (`xnu_live_seam_sctlr = 0x30c57879`, `C`
# clear): no `_irq_other_*` key, no `_irq_spurious_*`, no `_irq_cli_storm`, and the ending fired on its
# own schedule (`_post_end_calls = 0x7`, `_post_cntfrq = 0x0124f800`, the elapsed pair past
# 0x06e15472 = 6,011.8 ms of 6,000 ms). The context was new and it did not misbehave; the cost was
# 73 us of a 6,000 ms window, against 20,000 us on the arm below it.
#
# **ONE PREDICTION MISSED, AND IT MISSED ABOUT THE SCOPE OF AN INSTRUCTION.** 718 section 3 said
# `_wait_cpsr_after` MUST read `0x80000093`; it read **`0x20000093`**. The same control field (mode
# 0x13 SVC, **I SET**, F clear) with the condition flags left as the poll's own `cmp` left them - bit
# 29 C set instead of bit 31 N - **because `msr cpsr_c` writes the CONTROL field and not the flags**.
# Restoring the flags would have been wrong: they belong to the running C code and this function is a
# called function, not an exception return. The cell is right and the sentence was wrong; **the mask
# went back on**, which is the arm's safety claim and it is measured, not promised. Two limitations of
# the premise cell are recorded too: `_wait_cpsr` is read AFTER the `cpsie`, so with the exception
# taken at the next boundary it is read with the handler already run (it still measures the context
# the poll runs in, its definition, but it does not order the unmask against the delivery - the cell
# that does is `_pwr_irq_calls_probe_end`).
#
# **EVERY INHERITED CELL READS AS ON THE PRESSED RUNG-10 AND RUNG-9 ARMS**, which is the check that
# this arm changed only what it says it changed: `_storage_calls=1`, `_loads=6`, `_writes` published
# 0->4, `_rst_stores=1`, `_mode_bit_after=1`, `_gate=1`, `_bcr=0x0`, `_cbcr=0x4ff1`, `_gcc_map=1`,
# `_map=1`, `_pwr_before=0x00` -> `_pwr_after=0x0b` with `_pwr_vdd=7`, and the wait's own unchanged
# cells `_wait_req=0x2`, `_wait_reset=0`, `_wait_bound=0x0005DC00`, `_wait_done_before=0`,
# `_wait_state_before=0`, `_wait_io_before=0`. **711 section 3 is answered on a THIRD arm**:
# `_wait_cc_before = _wait_cc_after = 0xE047`. The handler's side is unchanged in value and new in
# context: `_pwr_irq_calls=1`, `_pwr_irq_intid=0xaa`, `_pwr_irq_ctl_before=0x0` ->
# `_pwr_irq_ctl_after=0x1`, `_pwr_irq_status=0x02`, `_pwr_irq_ack=0x01`, `_pwr_irq_status_after=0x00`,
# `_pwr_irq_state=0x02`, `_pwr_irq_io_level=0x08`.
#
# **THE GOAL IS STILL NOT MET.** No command, no sector, no partition table, no mount, no driver beyond
# the fixture - this rung's whole device act is still two halfword reads and a poll of one byte, and
# its log carries no key that names the medium. **TWRP-to-storage stays withheld.** What this press
# buys is that the last of the three mechanisms the driver's first handshake needs - the request (rung
# 7's byte), the interrupt (rung 8's client on intid 170) and the completion (this wait) - now stands
# in ONE RUN with the vendor's predicate satisfied, in a context that can be interrupted, which is
# exactly what 717 section 2 asked for. The next rung is therefore a step UP the line rather than
# another reading about the wait: the card's rail, the RCG rate write (705/706 measured 192 MHz where
# the driver believes 400 kHz), rung 6's two stores to hc_mem + 0x10C with the readback cell, or the
# command path (sdhci_send_command), where "the card answers" begins.

# --- armed-storage-cmd-892b8b68: THE RUNG-11 ARM, THE COMMAND PATH -------------------------------
#
# WHAT IT IS. `STAGE90_XNU_STORAGE_PROBE=11`: `sdhci_send_command` (`sdhci.c:1076-1155`) ported for the
# driver's own first two commands - CMD0 `MMC_GO_IDLE_STATE` (argument 0, `MMC_RSP_NONE`) and CMD1
# `MMC_SEND_OP_COND` (argument 0, `MMC_RSP_R3`), the pair `mmc.c:1356-1359` issues back to back - with
# the driver's IRQ completion replaced by a BOUNDED poll of the controller's own `INT_STATUS` because
# this image enables no SDHCI interrupt (the block's line is SPI 123 -> intid 155 and a delivery would
# END the run). It is the first act of this line that addresses the CARD rather than the controller,
# and `docs/experiments/experiment-721-the-rung-12-pre-registration-...md` is its pre-registration.
#
# WHY IT IS RECORDED AND PARKED, and it is 611's reason and not a new one: `./build.sh` is not
# byte-reproducible (408), so this payload exists in exactly one place on earth, and a run of either
# build script would destroy it. `xnu_arm_entry.bin`'s own sha256 begins `892b8b68`, which is the
# suffix this directory is named for - the convention every `armed-storage-*` park in `frozen/` uses.
#
# **THE TWO DEFECTS THIS ARM'S OWN CLAUSES CAUGHT, BOTH IN THE CLAUSES AND NOT IN THE HARDWARE** -
# recorded here because each is a shape that will recur: (1) `noinline` alone was not enough. GCC's
# interprocedural constant propagation rewrote the whole command body into a specialised clone and
# dropped the original, so `nm` showed `st_send_command.constprop.0` and NO `st_send_command`, and the
# clause's `sym_addr` refused the build - a clone is a second body with its own extent, and a clause
# reading the original's window while the image's only command path lived in the clone would have
# described a function no call reaches. The source carries `noinline` AND `noclone` now (m727). (2) The
# image-side clause was written as an emptiness - "this function owns no `.bss` word" - and refused a
# CORRECT image, because `classify_body` reports an access whose base register the body never
# materialized an address for as `UNK:<mnemonic>`, and this function's result struct is reached through
# its POINTER ARGUMENT. The claim is now the named pair `[UNK:ldr UNK:str]` with no address, which still
# refuses the class the clause is about (a symbol this rung never declared must be reached through a
# materialized base, and that prints `IMG:` WITH an address) - an assertion that cannot hold is not a
# check (m728).
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, all four of them `build_entry.sh` clauses rather than sentences: no data-path
# register (`BLOCK_SIZE`/`BLOCK_COUNT`/`TRANSFER_MODE`/`BUFFER` appear in no access of either body), no
# `POWER_CONTROL 0x29` (the probe's own window is unchanged at `47 44 44 41`), no store to either
# interrupt-enable register (`0x34`/`0x38` are READ, in `st_cmd_path`, and written nowhere in this
# image), no clock write, no GCC word, no `core_mem` word, and no byte of the medium. The write set is
# three stores per call and the clause fixes their ORDER as well as their set: `INT_STATUS` 0x30
# (write-1-to-clear), then `ARGUMENT` 0x08, then `COMMAND` 0x0E.
#
# **THIS SET SUPERSEDES THE `pwrwait-unmask-13366e08` ARM AS THE ONE THE NEXT PRESS SENDS** - that
# sentence lives in 718/719/720's record and is left where it is, per this file's own rule that a later
# step supersedes such a sentence rather than editing it.
